# qumidi Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build `qumidi`, a dependency-free Python 3.10+ library giving complete programmatic control and state observation of Allen & Heath Qu-series digital mixers over MIDI-on-TCP.

**Architecture:** Five layers, each depending only on the one below: transport (bytes, keepalive, reconnect) → MIDI codec (stream parser, NRPN assembly) → registry (a declarative `ParamSpec` table that is the single home of all protocol knowledge) → client (state cache, sync, events) → object facade (a thin proxy containing zero protocol constants). Both the flat command API and the ergonomic object API are generated from the registry, so a parameter is described exactly once.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.10+, standard library only for runtime (`asyncio`, `socket`, `struct`, `dataclasses`, `enum`). Dev: `pytest`, `pytest-asyncio`, `pytest-cov`, `hypothesis`, `mypy`, `ruff`.

**Spec:** `docs/qumidi-prd.md` (PRD v1.1). Every task below cites the PRD sections it implements. **Read the cited sections before starting a task** — this plan states the decisions and the code, but the PRD carries the reasoning, and several requirements are deliberate deviations from the vendor spec that a reader will otherwise "fix" back.

---

## Global Constraints

These apply to every task. They are not repeated in individual tasks.

- **Python floor:** 3.10. Use `X | Y` union syntax, `match` where it helps, no 3.11+ features (`Self`, `StrEnum`, `ExceptionGroup`, `asyncio.TaskGroup`, `datetime.UTC`).
- **Runtime dependencies:** zero. Standard library only. Any `import` of a third-party package in `qumidi/` outside `qumidi/testing/` is a plan violation.
- **Typing:** fully annotated. `mypy --strict` must be clean at the end of every task.
- **Style:** `ruff` defaults, line length 100. Clean at the end of every task.
- **License:** MIT.
- **Package name:** `qumidi` for both import package and PyPI distribution. Repository layout is flat (`qumidi/` at repo root), matching PRD §2.
- **Logging:** one logger per module, `logging.getLogger(__name__)`, all under the `qumidi` namespace. The library configures **no** handlers and never calls `basicConfig`. Wire-level hex dumps go to a separate `qumidi.wire` logger at `DEBUG`, and every call site guards formatting behind `if log.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):` because it would otherwise format every meter frame.
- **No unbounded queues anywhere** (PRD §18). Meter queue drops oldest; outbound queue applies backpressure; the thread-safe callback queue drops with a warning.
- **Timestamps are `time.monotonic()`**, never `time.time()`. Echo windows and staleness must not break when NTP steps the clock (PRD §10.1).
- **Never synthesize a default value.** "Not yet told" and "is at −∞" are categorically different and conflating them will make a user unmute something they shouldn't (PRD §10.1).
- **The library must never raise from inbound data.** Malformed input from the console yields fewer messages plus a log line, never an exception that kills the read loop.
- **Every exception message names the channel, parameter and value involved** (PRD §11.3). `"Invalid value"` with no context is useless against a 40-channel console.
- **1-based vs 0-based, fixed once:** users and log messages see the 1-based numbers the console displays (MIDI channel 1–16, scene 1–100, input 1–32, DCA 1–4). The wire sees 0-based. Conversion happens in exactly one place per concept, at the API boundary. GEQ band indices are the exception — 0-based throughout, to match the wire encoding (PRD §11.1).
- **Errata are load-bearing.** Where this plan contradicts the vendor MIDI protocol PDF, it is deliberate; PRD §14 lists all thirteen cases. Every such site carries a code comment citing the erratum number.
- **Commit after every task** (the final step of each task), with the message given in the task.

---

## File Structure

```
qumidi/
  __init__.py            # public exports
  errors.py              # exception hierarchy (§11.3)
  transport/
    base.py              # Transport ABC, ConnectionState
    tcp.py               # TcpTransport: connect, keepalive, reconnect
    queue.py             # OutboundQueue: ordered, rate-limited, bounded
  midi/
    messages.py          # message dataclasses + byte encoders
    parser.py            # MidiStreamParser (incremental, never raises)
    nrpn.py              # NRPN assembly/disassembly
  protocol/
    constants.py         # port, sysex header, command bytes, timing defaults
    channels.py          # ChannelClass, ChannelId, factories, Model, ModelCaps
    params.py            # ParamSpec, VxMode, Domain, Direction, Tier
    registry.py          # THE parameter registry + send-target table
    units.py             # taper tables + bidirectional interpolation
    sysex.py             # Qu sysex encode/decode, 7-bit packing
    meters.py            # meter layouts, MeterSlot, frame decoding
    strips.py            # StripId + MIDI fader strip messages (experimental)
  state.py               # Freshness, CachedValue, StateCache
  events.py              # EventBus + event payloads
  client_async.py        # AsyncQuMixer
  client_sync.py         # QuMixer (threaded facade over the async one)
  layout.py              # SurfaceMap (client-side only, no protocol)
  model/
    base.py              # ParamProxy, SectionProxy — generated from registry
    channel.py           # InputChannel, StereoInput, MixMaster, DcaMaster, ...
  testing/
    fake_qu.py           # FakeQu server + fault injection
    fixtures/            # recorded byte streams, golden vectors
  cli.py                 # probe / monitor / dump-state / set / record / replay
tests/
tools/
  calibrate.py           # hardware taper calibration sweep
docs/
```

**Responsibility boundaries that must not blur:**

- `midi/` knows nothing about mixers. It parses and builds MIDI, full stop. No Qu constant appears in it.
- `protocol/` knows nothing about connections. It is pure functions and tables. No `async` appears in it except in nothing at all — it is entirely synchronous.
- `model/` contains zero protocol constants. Every method is a thin call into `AsyncQuMixer.set`/`.get` with a registry-supplied name.
- `client_sync.py` contains zero protocol logic. It proxies coroutines onto a loop thread and nothing else.

---

## Slice Map

Each slice ends in something you can run by hand against `FakeQu` and, from Slice 2 onward, against a real console.

| Slice | Deliverable | Hand test |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | It connects and reports what it is | `qumidi probe` prints model, firmware, MIDI channel |
| 2 | It can mute a channel | Mute LED lights on the console |
| 3 | It can move a fader | Physical fader moves to −6 dB |
| 4 | It knows what the console is doing | `qumidi monitor` prints your fader moves |
| 5 | Every parameter works | Set a PEQ gain / gate threshold / GEQ band, watch the screen |
| 6 | It knows the channel names | `qumidi dump-state` shows your real names |
| 7 | Scene recall doesn't lie | Recall a scene, cache goes stale and rebuilds |
| 8 | It survives the cable being pulled | Unplug, replug, it re-syncs itself |
| 9 | The ergonomic API | Run the PRD §11.1 example verbatim |
| 10 | Meters | Clap into a mic, watch the number move |
| 11 | Strips, shutdown, surface maps, capture | Move a MIDI strip, see a raw strip event |
| 12 | Hardware verification and ship | `pytest -m hardware --qu-host=...` green on Qu-24 and Qu-32 |

---

# Slice 1 — It connects and reports what it is

**Slice goal:** A `qumidi probe` command that connects to a Qu (or `FakeQu`), completes the all-call system-state handshake, and prints the model, firmware version and MIDI channel. This forces the whole vertical: transport, keepalive, a fully robust stream parser, sysex framing, and a minimal client.

**Why the parser is fully robust in slice 1 rather than later:** the handshake reply is a sysex that will arrive split across TCP reads with `FE` keepalive bytes interleaved inside it, on real hardware, on the very first connection. A "good enough for now" parser does not survive slice 1's own hand test.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test for the exception hierarchy**

The hierarchy is public API — applications will catch `QuConnectionError` and expect it to also catch a lost connection. Assert the shape, not just existence.

`tests/test_errors.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi import errors


def test_every_exception_derives_from_qu_error() -> None:
    names = [
        "QuConnectionError",
        "QuConnectionLostError",
        "SyncTimeoutError",
        "NotSyncedError",
        "UnsupportedOnModelError",
        "ParameterRangeError",
        "UnknownParameterError",
        "ReadOnlyParameterError",
        "ValueUnknownError",
        "ShutdownNotPermittedError",
        "MeterLayoutError",
    ]
    for name in names:
        cls = getattr(errors, name)
        assert issubclass(cls, errors.QuError), name


def test_connection_lost_is_a_connection_error() -> None:
    assert issubclass(errors.QuConnectionLostError, errors.QuConnectionError)


def test_qu_error_is_a_plain_exception_not_a_base_exception() -> None:
    # Catching Exception must catch everything this library raises; nothing
    # here should escape a caller's blanket handler the way KeyboardInterrupt
    # does.
    assert issubclass(errors.QuError, Exception)
    assert not issubclass(errors.QuError, (SystemExit, KeyboardInterrupt))


def test_errors_carry_their_message() -> None:
    exc = errors.ParameterRangeError("pan on Input 6: raw 0x7F exceeds maximum 0x4A")
    assert "Input 6" in str(exc)
    assert "0x4A" in str(exc)
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `qumidi/errors.py`**

```python
"""Exception hierarchy for qumidi (PRD 11.3).

Every exception message must name the channel, parameter and value involved.
"Invalid value" with no context is useless when debugging a 40-channel console.
"""

from __future__ import annotations


class QuError(Exception):
    """Base class for every error raised by this library."""


class QuConnectionError(QuError):
    """Could not establish or maintain the TCP MIDI session.

    Most commonly raised because the mixer permits exactly one TCP MIDI
    connection at a time and something else (Qu-Pad, another script) holds it.
    """


class QuConnectionLostError(QuConnectionError):
    """A write was attempted after an unrecoverable disconnect."""


class SyncTimeoutError(QuError):
    """The console replied to the state request but never sent the end marker."""


class NotSyncedError(QuError):
    """An operation needing the model or the console's MIDI channel ran before sync."""


class UnsupportedOnModelError(QuError):
    """The addressed channel does not exist on the detected model."""


class ParameterRangeError(QuError):
    """A value or index is outside the range the parameter accepts."""


class UnknownParameterError(QuError):
    """No registry entry matches the requested parameter name."""


class ReadOnlyParameterError(QuError):
    """Attempted to write a Direction.RX_ONLY parameter."""


class ValueUnknownError(QuError):
    """Read of a value the console has never reported, with no default supplied."""


class ShutdownNotPermittedError(QuError):
    """Remote shutdown attempted without both guards satisfied."""


class MeterLayoutError(QuError):
    """A meter frame's length disagrees with the computed layout for the model."""
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write `qumidi/protocol/constants.py`**

All literals from PRD §4.2, §4.3, §4.5, §9.1, §9.2, §9.3, §9.4, §10.3.

```python
"""Protocol constants and tuned defaults (PRD 4, 9, 10).

Every timing default here is documented in the PRD with the reason it has the
value it has. Change one only with the corresponding PRD section in hand.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

# --- Transport (PRD 4.2, 4.3, 4.5) ---------------------------------------

QU_TCP_PORT: Final = 51325
CONNECT_TIMEOUT: Final = 5.0

#: Sent unconditionally on this interval whenever the link is up. Safe under
#: both keepalive regimes and satisfies the 5s iPadFlag=1 deadline (PRD 4.3).
KEEPALIVE_INTERVAL: Final = 2.0

#: No bytes of any kind for this long means the link is dead. Do not wait for
#: the socket to notice (PRD 4.3).
INBOUND_TIMEOUT: Final = 10.0

#: Roughly the 31.25 kbaud DIN-MIDI rate the console's parser is built around.
MAX_SEND_RATE: Final = 1000  # bytes per second
OUTBOUND_QUEUE_MAXSIZE: Final = 256

RECONNECT_BACKOFF_MIN: Final = 0.5
RECONNECT_BACKOFF_MAX: Final = 30.0
RECONNECT_BACKOFF_FACTOR: Final = 2.0
RECONNECT_JITTER: Final = 0.2

# --- MIDI status bytes ---------------------------------------------------

NOTE_OFF: Final = 0x80
NOTE_ON: Final = 0x90
CONTROL_CHANGE: Final = 0xB0
PROGRAM_CHANGE: Final = 0xC0
SYSEX_START: Final = 0xF0
SYSEX_END: Final = 0xF7
ACTIVE_SENSING: Final = 0xFE

CC_NRPN_MSB: Final = 0x63
CC_NRPN_LSB: Final = 0x62
CC_DATA_MSB: Final = 0x06
CC_DATA_LSB: Final = 0x26
CC_BANK_SELECT_MSB: Final = 0x00
CC_BANK_SELECT_LSB: Final = 0x20

MAX_SYSEX_BYTES: Final = 65536

# --- Qu sysex framing (PRD 9.1, 9.2) -------------------------------------

#: F0 00 00 1A 50 11 01 00 -- the channel byte follows, then the payload.
QU_SYSEX_PREFIX: Final = bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0x00, 0x1A, 0x50, 0x11, 0x01, 0x00])
ALL_CALL_CHANNEL_BYTE: Final = 0x7F

CMD_GET_NAME: Final = 0x01
CMD_NAME_REPLY: Final = 0x02
CMD_SET_NAME: Final = 0x03
CMD_GET_SYSTEM_STATE: Final = 0x10
CMD_SYSTEM_STATE_REPLY: Final = 0x11
CMD_METERS: Final = 0x12
CMD_METER_DATA: Final = 0x13
CMD_END_OF_SYNC: Final = 0x14

# --- Sync and naming (PRD 9.3, 9.4) --------------------------------------

DEFAULT_IPAD_FLAG: Final = 1
REPLY_TIMEOUT: Final = 5.0
SYNC_TIMEOUT: Final = 15.0
NAME_SWEEP_INTERVAL: Final = 0.020
NAME_REQUEST_TIMEOUT: Final = 0.5
NAME_REQUEST_RETRIES: Final = 2

#: Provisional -- PRD 9.4 requires this be measured on hardware in M6 and the
#: measured value substituted here.
MAX_NAME_LENGTH: Final = 6

#: The minimum firmware this registry was transcribed against (PRD 6.2).
MIN_FIRMWARE: Final = (1, 9)

# --- NRPN decode and state (PRD 5.3, 10.3, 7.2) --------------------------

PENDING_FLUSH_TIMEOUT: Final = 0.050
ECHO_WINDOW: Final = 1.0
SLOW_CALLBACK_WARN: Final = 0.100
SCENE_SETTLE_DELAY: Final = 0.500
```

### Task 1.2: MIDI message types and encoders

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/midi/messages.py`
- Test: `tests/midi/test_messages.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `qumidi.protocol.constants` status bytes.
- Produces: frozen dataclasses `NoteOn`, `NoteOff`, `ControlChange`, `ProgramChange`, `SysEx`, `SystemRealtime`; the union alias `MidiMessage`; each message's `.to_bytes() -> bytes`. `SysEx.data` holds the payload **without** the `F0`/`F7` framing bytes. `channel` on channel-voice messages is the 0-based wire channel.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/midi/test_messages.py` (create `tests/midi/__init__.py` as an empty file first):

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.midi.messages import (
    ControlChange,
    NoteOff,
    NoteOn,
    ProgramChange,
    SysEx,
    SystemRealtime,
)


def test_note_on_encodes() -> None:
    assert NoteOn(channel=0, note=0x25, velocity=0x7F).to_bytes() == bytes([0x90, 0x25, 0x7F])


def test_note_on_uses_the_wire_channel() -> None:
    assert NoteOn(channel=5, note=0x25, velocity=0x7F).to_bytes() == bytes([0x95, 0x25, 0x7F])


def test_note_off_encodes() -> None:
    assert NoteOff(channel=0, note=0x25, velocity=0x00).to_bytes() == bytes([0x80, 0x25, 0x00])


def test_control_change_encodes() -> None:
    assert ControlChange(channel=3, controller=0x63, value=0x25).to_bytes() == bytes(
        [0xB3, 0x63, 0x25]
    )


def test_program_change_encodes_two_bytes() -> None:
    assert ProgramChange(channel=0, program=0x0B).to_bytes() == bytes([0xC0, 0x0B])


def test_sysex_data_excludes_the_framing_bytes() -> None:
    msg = SysEx(data=bytes([0x00, 0x00, 0x1A]))
    assert msg.to_bytes() == bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0x00, 0x1A, 0xF7])


def test_system_realtime_encodes_one_byte() -> None:
    assert SystemRealtime(status=0xFE).to_bytes() == bytes([0xFE])


def test_messages_are_frozen_and_hashable() -> None:
    msg = ControlChange(channel=0, controller=6, value=0x62)
    assert hash(msg) == hash(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=6, value=0x62))
    with pytest.raises(AttributeError):
        msg.value = 1  # type: ignore[misc]


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "factory",
    [
        lambda: NoteOn(channel=16, note=0, velocity=0),
        lambda: NoteOn(channel=0, note=0x80, velocity=0),
        lambda: ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0, value=0x80),
        lambda: SysEx(data=bytes([0x80])),
    ],
)
def test_out_of_range_fields_are_rejected(factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        factory()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/midi/messages.py`**

Validation in `__post_init__` catches construction bugs at the source. The parser never constructs an out-of-range message (it enforces the same rule at parse time), so this raising is safe under the "never raise on inbound data" rule.

```python
"""MIDI message types (PRD 5.2).

This module knows nothing about mixers. It is generic MIDI.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Union

from qumidi.protocol import constants as c


def _check(name: str, value: int, hi: int) -> None:
    if not 0 <= value <= hi:
        raise ValueError(f"{name} must be 0..{hi}, got {value!r}")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NoteOn:
    channel: int
    note: int
    velocity: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        _check("channel", self.channel, 15)
        _check("note", self.note, 0x7F)
        _check("velocity", self.velocity, 0x7F)

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([c.NOTE_ON | self.channel, self.note, self.velocity])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NoteOff:
    channel: int
    note: int
    velocity: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        _check("channel", self.channel, 15)
        _check("note", self.note, 0x7F)
        _check("velocity", self.velocity, 0x7F)

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([c.NOTE_OFF | self.channel, self.note, self.velocity])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ControlChange:
    channel: int
    controller: int
    value: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        _check("channel", self.channel, 15)
        _check("controller", self.controller, 0x7F)
        _check("value", self.value, 0x7F)

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([c.CONTROL_CHANGE | self.channel, self.controller, self.value])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ProgramChange:
    channel: int
    program: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        _check("channel", self.channel, 15)
        _check("program", self.program, 0x7F)

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([c.PROGRAM_CHANGE | self.channel, self.program])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SysEx:
    """A system-exclusive message. ``data`` excludes the F0/F7 framing bytes."""

    data: bytes

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if any(b > 0x7F for b in self.data):
            raise ValueError("sysex payload bytes must all be 7-bit")

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([c.SYSEX_START]) + self.data + bytes([c.SYSEX_END])


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SystemRealtime:
    """A single-byte F8-FF message."""

    status: int

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not 0xF8 <= self.status <= 0xFF:
            raise ValueError(f"system realtime status must be 0xF8..0xFF, got {self.status:#04x}")

    def to_bytes(self) -> bytes:
        return bytes([self.status])


MidiMessage = Union[NoteOn, NoteOff, ControlChange, ProgramChange, SysEx, SystemRealtime]
```

### Task 1.3: The incremental MIDI stream parser

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/midi/parser.py`
- Test: `tests/midi/test_parser.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `qumidi.midi.messages`, `qumidi.protocol.constants`.
- Produces: `MidiStreamParser(max_sysex_bytes: int = 65536)` with `feed(data: bytes) -> list[MidiMessage]`. Pure — no I/O, no async. **Never raises on any input.**

This is the single highest-risk piece of generic code in the project. All nine rules from PRD §5.1 are implemented here and every one has a test, because each corresponds to something that actually occurs in Qu traffic.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests for the nine rules**

`tests/midi/test_parser.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.midi.messages import (
    ControlChange,
    NoteOn,
    ProgramChange,
    SysEx,
    SystemRealtime,
)
from qumidi.midi.parser import MidiStreamParser


def parse_all(data: bytes) -> list[object]:
    return MidiStreamParser().feed(data)


def test_simple_control_change() -> None:
    assert parse_all(bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25])) == [
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)
    ]


def test_running_status_repeats_the_status_byte() -> None:
    # Rule 1: the Qu sends long NRPN bursts under running status.
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0x62, 0x17, 0x06, 0x62, 0x26, 0x07])
    assert parse_all(data) == [
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25),
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x62, value=0x17),
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x06, value=0x62),
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x26, value=0x07),
    ]


def test_sysex_split_across_feeds() -> None:
    # Rule 2: a meter frame will not arrive in one TCP read.
    parser = MidiStreamParser()
    assert parser.feed(bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0x00])) == []
    assert parser.feed(bytes([0x1A, 0x50])) == []
    assert parser.feed(bytes([0xF7])) == [SysEx(data=bytes([0x00, 0x00, 0x1A, 0x50]))]


def test_realtime_inside_sysex_is_extracted_without_disturbing_it() -> None:
    # Rule 3: FE arrives every 300ms, including mid-sysex.
    data = bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0xFE, 0x01, 0xF7])
    assert parse_all(data) == [SystemRealtime(status=0xFE), SysEx(data=bytes([0x00, 0x01]))]


def test_realtime_between_running_status_data_does_not_clear_running_status() -> None:
    # Rule 3 + rule 6: F8-FF must not clear running status.
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xFE, 0x62, 0x17])
    assert parse_all(data) == [
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25),
        SystemRealtime(status=0xFE),
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x62, value=0x17),
    ]


def test_realtime_between_the_two_data_bytes_of_one_message() -> None:
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0xFE, 0x25])
    assert parse_all(data) == [
        SystemRealtime(status=0xFE),
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25),
    ]


def test_truncated_sysex_is_discarded_not_raised() -> None:
    # Rule 4: a new status byte before F7 aborts the sysex.
    data = bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0x01, 0xB0, 0x63, 0x25])
    assert parse_all(data) == [ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)]


def test_unknown_status_byte_is_skipped() -> None:
    # Rule 5. 0xF4 is undefined; the CC after it must still parse.
    data = bytes([0xF4, 0xB0, 0x63, 0x25])
    assert parse_all(data) == [ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)]


def test_system_common_clears_running_status() -> None:
    # Rule 6: F1-F6 clear running status; the trailing 62 17 is then orphaned.
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xF6, 0x62, 0x17])
    assert parse_all(data) == [ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)]


def test_oversized_sysex_is_discarded_and_the_stream_resynchronises() -> None:
    # Rule 7: a lost F7 must not grow the buffer without limit.
    parser = MidiStreamParser(max_sysex_bytes=8)
    out = parser.feed(bytes([0xF0]) + bytes(32) + bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25]))
    assert out == [ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)]


def test_lone_sysex_end_is_ignored() -> None:
    # Rule 8.
    assert parse_all(bytes([0xF7, 0xB0, 0x63, 0x25])) == [
        ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)
    ]


def test_high_bit_byte_where_a_data_byte_is_expected_restarts_parsing() -> None:
    # Rule 9: never let 0x80+ reach a value field.
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x90, 0x25, 0x7F])
    assert parse_all(data) == [NoteOn(channel=0, note=0x25, velocity=0x7F)]


def test_program_change_is_two_bytes_and_supports_running_status() -> None:
    data = bytes([0xC0, 0x0B, 0x0C])
    assert parse_all(data) == [
        ProgramChange(channel=0, program=0x0B),
        ProgramChange(channel=0, program=0x0C),
    ]


def test_parser_never_raises_on_arbitrary_bytes() -> None:
    parser = MidiStreamParser()
    for value in range(256):
        parser.feed(bytes([value]) * 3)


REPRESENTATIVE = bytes(
    [0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0x62, 0x17, 0x06, 0x62, 0x26, 0x07]
    + [0xF0, 0x00, 0x00, 0x1A, 0x50, 0x11, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x11, 0x03, 0x01, 0x09, 0xF7]
    + [0x90, 0x25, 0x7F, 0x90, 0x25, 0x00]
    + [0xC0, 0x0B]
)


def test_boundary_fuzz_every_single_split_point() -> None:
    # PRD 13.3: split at every possible position; the message list is identical.
    expected = MidiStreamParser().feed(REPRESENTATIVE)
    for cut in range(len(REPRESENTATIVE) + 1):
        parser = MidiStreamParser()
        got = parser.feed(REPRESENTATIVE[:cut]) + parser.feed(REPRESENTATIVE[cut:])
        assert got == expected, f"split at {cut}"


def test_boundary_fuzz_byte_at_a_time() -> None:
    expected = MidiStreamParser().feed(REPRESENTATIVE)
    parser = MidiStreamParser()
    got: list[object] = []
    for byte in REPRESENTATIVE:
        got.extend(parser.feed(bytes([byte])))
    assert got == expected


def test_realtime_injection_at_every_index_is_transparent() -> None:
    # PRD 13.3: insert FE at every index; non-realtime messages parse identically.
    expected = [m for m in MidiStreamParser().feed(REPRESENTATIVE)]
    for index in range(len(REPRESENTATIVE) + 1):
        injected = REPRESENTATIVE[:index] + bytes([0xFE]) + REPRESENTATIVE[index:]
        got = [
            m for m in MidiStreamParser().feed(injected) if not isinstance(m, SystemRealtime)
        ]
        assert got == expected, f"FE injected at {index}"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/midi/parser.py`**

```python
"""Incremental MIDI stream parser (PRD 5.1).

Byte-at-a-time, pure, and it never raises. Malformed input yields fewer
messages plus a log line. Every numbered rule below is a real behaviour of Qu
traffic, not a hypothetical -- see PRD 5.1.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging

from qumidi.midi.messages import (
    ControlChange,
    MidiMessage,
    NoteOff,
    NoteOn,
    ProgramChange,
    SysEx,
    SystemRealtime,
)
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

#: Number of data bytes each channel-voice status expects.
_DATA_LENGTH = {0x80: 2, 0x90: 2, 0xA0: 2, 0xB0: 2, 0xC0: 1, 0xD0: 1, 0xE0: 2}


class MidiStreamParser:
    """Feed bytes in, get whole messages out. Holds partial state between calls."""

    def __init__(self, max_sysex_bytes: int = c.MAX_SYSEX_BYTES) -> None:
        self._max_sysex_bytes = max_sysex_bytes
        self._running_status: int | None = None
        self._data: list[int] = []
        self._expected: int = 0
        self._in_sysex = False
        self._sysex: bytearray = bytearray()

    def feed(self, data: bytes) -> list[MidiMessage]:
        out: list[MidiMessage] = []
        for byte in data:
            self._feed_byte(byte, out)
        return out

    # -- internals --------------------------------------------------------

    def _feed_byte(self, byte: int, out: list[MidiMessage]) -> None:
        # Rule 3/6: system realtime is extracted from anywhere, including
        # inside a sysex payload and between the data bytes of a message, and
        # it does not clear running status or disturb anything in progress.
        if byte >= 0xF8:
            out.append(SystemRealtime(status=byte))
            return

        if byte >= 0x80:
            self._handle_status(byte, out)
            return

        self._handle_data(byte, out)

    def _handle_status(self, byte: int, out: list[MidiMessage]) -> None:
        if self._in_sysex:
            # Rule 4: any status byte before F7 aborts the sysex.
            if byte == c.SYSEX_END:
                out.append(SysEx(data=bytes(self._sysex)))
                self._end_sysex()
                return
            log.debug("truncated sysex abandoned after %d bytes", len(self._sysex))
            self._end_sysex()
            # fall through and treat this byte as the new status

        if byte == c.SYSEX_START:
            self._in_sysex = True
            self._sysex = bytearray()
            self._data.clear()
            # A sysex is a system common message: it clears running status.
            self._running_status = None
            return

        if byte == c.SYSEX_END:
            # Rule 8: a lone F7 with no open sysex is ignored, not an error.
            return

        if 0xF1 <= byte <= 0xF6:
            # Rule 6: system common clears running status. We do not surface
            # these -- the Qu never sends them and they carry nothing we need.
            self._running_status = None
            self._data.clear()
            return

        status = byte & 0xF0
        if status not in _DATA_LENGTH:
            # Rule 5: unknown status byte, skip gracefully.
            log.debug("skipping unknown status byte %#04x", byte)
            self._running_status = None
            self._data.clear()
            return

        self._running_status = byte
        self._expected = _DATA_LENGTH[status]
        self._data.clear()

    def _handle_data(self, byte: int, out: list[MidiMessage]) -> None:
        if self._in_sysex:
            if len(self._sysex) >= self._max_sysex_bytes:
                # Rule 7: a lost F7 must not grow the buffer without limit.
                log.warning(
                    "sysex exceeded %d bytes without F7; discarding and resynchronising",
                    self._max_sysex_bytes,
                )
                self._end_sysex()
                return
            self._sysex.append(byte)
            return

        if self._running_status is None:
            log.debug("orphan data byte %#04x with no running status", byte)
            return

        self._data.append(byte)
        if len(self._data) < self._expected:
            return

        message = self._build(self._running_status, self._data)
        self._data.clear()  # Rule 1: running status persists; only data resets.
        if message is not None:
            out.append(message)

    def _build(self, status: int, data: list[int]) -> MidiMessage | None:
        channel = status & 0x0F
        kind = status & 0xF0
        if kind == c.NOTE_ON:
            return NoteOn(channel=channel, note=data[0], velocity=data[1])
        if kind == c.NOTE_OFF:
            return NoteOff(channel=channel, note=data[0], velocity=data[1])
        if kind == c.CONTROL_CHANGE:
            return ControlChange(channel=channel, controller=data[0], value=data[1])
        if kind == c.PROGRAM_CHANGE:
            return ProgramChange(channel=channel, program=data[0])
        # Polyphonic aftertouch (A0), channel pressure (D0) and pitch bend (E0)
        # are consumed for framing but not surfaced; the Qu does not use them.
        return None

    def _end_sysex(self) -> None:
        self._in_sysex = False
        self._sysex = bytearray()
```

Note on rule 9: a byte `>= 0x80` arriving mid-message reaches `_handle_status`, which resets `_data` and installs the new status. That is exactly "terminates the current message and re-dispatches it as a status byte", so no separate branch is needed — but the test pins the behaviour so a future refactor cannot lose it.

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the Hypothesis property test**

Append to `tests/midi/test_parser.py`:

```python
from hypothesis import given, settings
from hypothesis import strategies as st

_messages = st.one_of(
    st.builds(
        ControlChange,
        channel=st.integers(0, 15),
        controller=st.integers(0, 0x7F),
        value=st.integers(0, 0x7F),
    ),
    st.builds(
        NoteOn,
        channel=st.integers(0, 15),
        note=st.integers(0, 0x7F),
        velocity=st.integers(0, 0x7F),
    ),
    st.builds(ProgramChange, channel=st.integers(0, 15), program=st.integers(0, 0x7F)),
    st.builds(SysEx, data=st.binary(min_size=0, max_size=32).map(bytes)
              .filter(lambda b: all(x < 0x80 for x in b))),
)


@settings(max_examples=200)
@given(msgs=st.lists(_messages, max_size=20), cuts=st.lists(st.integers(0, 500), max_size=10))
def test_encode_chunk_parse_is_the_identity(msgs: list[object], cuts: list[int]) -> None:
    # PRD 13.3: arbitrary well-formed messages -> encode -> chunk -> parse -> identity.
    stream = b"".join(m.to_bytes() for m in msgs)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    points = sorted({0, len(stream)} | {min(x, len(stream)) for x in cuts})
    parser = MidiStreamParser()
    got: list[object] = []
    for start, end in zip(points, points[1:]):
        got.extend(parser.feed(stream[start:end]))
    assert got == msgs
```

def test_build_encodes_a_non_zero_midi_channel() -> None:

    # because the mixer's MIDI channel is unknown until it replies.
    assert build_sysex(None, bytes([0x10, 0x01])) == HEADER + bytes([0x7F, 0x10, 0x01, 0xF7])

    assert get_system_state(0) == HEADER + bytes([0x7F, 0x10, 0x00, 0xF7])

    parsed = parse_sysex(SysEx(data=raw[1:-1]))

    assert parsed is not None
    assert parsed.midi_channel is None
    assert parsed.command == 0x14

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/sysex.py`**

```python
"""Qu system-exclusive framing (PRD 9.1, 9.2).

All Qu sysex shares one eight-byte prefix, then a channel byte, then a command
byte and its payload. The channel byte is the wire channel 0-15, or 0x7F for
the all-call form used before the console's channel is known.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass

from qumidi.midi.messages import SysEx
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_PREFIX_BODY = c.QU_SYSEX_PREFIX[1:]  # the prefix without its leading F0


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class QuSysex:
    """A decoded Qu sysex message.

    ``midi_channel`` is the wire channel 0-15, or ``None`` for the all-call form.
    """

    midi_channel: int | None
    command: int
    payload: bytes


def build_sysex(midi_channel: int | None, payload: bytes) -> bytes:
    """Frame ``payload`` (command byte first) for ``midi_channel``.

    ``midi_channel=None`` selects the all-call header (PRD 9.1).
    """
    if midi_channel is not None and not 0 <= midi_channel <= 15:
        raise ValueError(f"midi_channel must be the wire channel 0..15, got {midi_channel!r}")
    if any(b > 0x7F for b in payload):
        raise ValueError("all sysex payload bytes must be 7-bit (PRD 9.2)")
    channel_byte = c.ALL_CALL_CHANNEL_BYTE if midi_channel is None else midi_channel
    return c.QU_SYSEX_PREFIX + bytes([channel_byte]) + payload + bytes([c.SYSEX_END])


def parse_sysex(msg: SysEx) -> QuSysex | None:
    """Decode a Qu sysex, or return None if this is not one.

    Never raises: a foreign or malformed sysex is simply not ours.
    """
    data = msg.data
    if len(data) < len(_PREFIX_BODY) + 2:
        return None
    if data[: len(_PREFIX_BODY)] != _PREFIX_BODY:
        return None
    channel_byte = data[len(_PREFIX_BODY)]
    if channel_byte == c.ALL_CALL_CHANNEL_BYTE:
        midi_channel: int | None = None
    elif 0x00 <= channel_byte <= 0x0F:
        midi_channel = channel_byte
    else:
        # PRD 9.1: reject rather than deriving a nonsense channel.
        log.warning("Qu sysex with invalid channel byte %#04x; ignoring", channel_byte)
        return None
    body = data[len(_PREFIX_BODY) + 1 :]
    return QuSysex(midi_channel=midi_channel, command=body[0], payload=bytes(body[1:]))


def get_system_state(ipad_flag: int = c.DEFAULT_IPAD_FLAG) -> bytes:
    """Build the all-call system-state request (PRD 9.3)."""
    if ipad_flag not in (0, 1):
        raise ValueError(f"ipad_flag must be 0 or 1, got {ipad_flag!r}")
    return build_sysex(None, bytes([c.CMD_GET_SYSTEM_STATE, ipad_flag]))


def parse_system_state_reply(sx: QuSysex) -> tuple[int, tuple[int, int]]:
    """Return ``(box_id, (major, minor))`` from an ``11`` reply (PRD 9.3)."""
    if sx.command != c.CMD_SYSTEM_STATE_REPLY:
        raise ValueError(f"expected command 0x11, got {sx.command:#04x}")
    if len(sx.payload) < 3:
        raise ValueError(
            f"system state reply needs 3 payload bytes, got {len(sx.payload)}: "
            f"{sx.payload.hex(' ')}"
        )
    return sx.payload[0], (sx.payload[1], sx.payload[2])


def meters_enable(midi_channel: int, on: bool) -> bytes:
    """Build the ``12`` meter on/off command (PRD 9.2)."""
    return build_sysex(midi_channel, bytes([c.CMD_METERS, 0x01 if on else 0x00]))
```

### Task 1.5: Transport ABC and TcpTransport

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/transport/base.py`
- Create: `qumidi/transport/tcp.py`
- Test: `tests/transport/test_tcp.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `MidiStreamParser`, `constants`, `errors`.
- Produces:
  - `ConnectionState` enum: `DISCONNECTED, CONNECTING, CONNECTED, SYNCING, READY, RECONNECTING`.
  - `Transport` ABC with `connect()`, `close()`, `send(data)`, `messages() -> AsyncIterator[MidiMessage]`, `is_connected`, `on_state_change(cb)`, and a `set_state(state)` helper for subclasses.
  - `TcpTransport(host, port=51325, connect_timeout=5.0, keepalive_interval=2.0, inbound_timeout=10.0, forward_active_sense=False)`.

Reconnect is **not** in this task — it lands in Slice 8 with the cache invalidation it must drive. This task's `TcpTransport` connects once and reports the link dead when it dies.

**Why the transport yields parsed messages rather than bytes:** `TcpTransport` owns a `MidiStreamParser`; a future `UsbTransport` receives pre-framed messages from `mido` and skips the parser entirely. That asymmetry is the reason the parser lives here and not in the client (PRD §4.1).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/transport/__init__.py` (empty), then `tests/transport/test_tcp.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionError
from qumidi.midi.messages import ControlChange, SystemRealtime
from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState
from qumidi.transport.tcp import TcpTransport


class Echo:
    """A minimal TCP server for transport-level tests."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.received = bytearray()
        self.writers: list[asyncio.StreamWriter] = []
        self.connections = 0
        self._server: asyncio.AbstractServer | None = None

    async def start(self) -> int:
        self._server = await asyncio.start_server(self._handle, "127.0.0.1", 0)
        return int(self._server.sockets[0].getsockname()[1])

    async def _handle(self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        self.connections += 1
        self.writers.append(writer)
        try:
            while data := await reader.read(4096):
                self.received.extend(data)
        except (ConnectionResetError, asyncio.CancelledError):
            pass

    async def push(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        for w in self.writers:
            w.write(data)
            await w.drain()

    async def stop(self) -> None:
        for w in self.writers:
            w.close()
        if self._server is not None:
            self._server.close()
            await self._server.wait_closed()


@pytest.fixture
async def echo():  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server = Echo()
    port = await server.start()
    yield server, port
    await server.stop()


async def test_connect_reports_connected(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    states: list[ConnectionState] = []
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port)
    t.on_state_change(states.append)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        assert t.is_connected
        assert states == [ConnectionState.CONNECTING, ConnectionState.CONNECTED]
    finally:
        await t.close()
    assert states[-1] == ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED


async def test_connect_to_a_closed_port_raises_qu_connection_error() -> None:
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=1, connect_timeout=0.5)
    with pytest.raises(QuConnectionError) as excinfo:
        await t.connect()
    # PRD 4.2: the message must name the single-connection limit -- this is
    # the number one user support question.
    assert "one" in str(excinfo.value).lower()


async def test_send_reaches_the_server(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await t.send(bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25]))
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25]) in bytes(server.received)
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_inbound_bytes_arrive_as_parsed_messages(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await server.push(bytes([0xB0, 0x63]))
        await server.push(bytes([0x25]))
        agen = t.messages()
        msg = await asyncio.wait_for(agen.__anext__(), timeout=1.0)
        assert msg == ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_active_sense_is_consumed_not_forwarded(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await server.push(bytes([0xFE, 0xB0, 0x63, 0x25]))
        msg = await asyncio.wait_for(t.messages().__anext__(), timeout=1.0)
        assert msg == ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25)
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_active_sense_is_forwarded_when_requested(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port, forward_active_sense=True)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await server.push(bytes([0xFE]))
        msg = await asyncio.wait_for(t.messages().__anext__(), timeout=1.0)
        assert msg == SystemRealtime(status=0xFE)
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_keepalive_is_emitted_on_the_configured_interval(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port, keepalive_interval=0.05)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await asyncio.sleep(0.28)
        assert bytes(server.received).count(0xFE) >= 4
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_inbound_silence_declares_the_link_dead(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.3: do not wait for the socket to notice.
    server, port = echo
    states: list[ConnectionState] = []
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port, keepalive_interval=0.05, inbound_timeout=0.2)
    t.on_state_change(states.append)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
        assert ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED in states
        assert not t.is_connected
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_close_is_idempotent_and_leaves_no_tasks(echo) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    server, port = echo
    before = len(asyncio.all_tasks())
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=port)
    await t.connect()
    await t.close()
    await t.close()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
    assert len(asyncio.all_tasks()) <= before
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/transport/base.py`**

```python
"""Transport interface (PRD 4.1).

A transport yields *parsed messages*, not bytes. TcpTransport owns a
MidiStreamParser internally; a future UsbTransport receives pre-framed
messages from mido and skips the parser. That asymmetry is why the parser is
not part of the client.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Callable
from enum import Enum, auto

from qumidi.midi.messages import MidiMessage

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class ConnectionState(Enum):
    DISCONNECTED = auto()
    CONNECTING = auto()
    CONNECTED = auto()
    SYNCING = auto()
    READY = auto()
    RECONNECTING = auto()


class Transport(ABC):
    """Bytes in, parsed messages out."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._state = ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED
        self._state_callbacks: list[Callable[[ConnectionState], None]] = []

    @abstractmethod
    async def connect(self) -> None: ...

    @abstractmethod
    async def close(self) -> None: ...

    @abstractmethod
    async def send(self, data: bytes) -> None: ...

    @abstractmethod
    def messages(self) -> AsyncIterator[MidiMessage]: ...

    @property
    @abstractmethod
    def is_connected(self) -> bool: ...

    @property
    def state(self) -> ConnectionState:
        return self._state

    def on_state_change(self, cb: Callable[[ConnectionState], None]) -> None:
        self._state_callbacks.append(cb)

    def set_state(self, state: ConnectionState) -> None:
        """Record a transition and notify subscribers. Callback errors never propagate."""
        if state is self._state:
            return
        self._state = state
        for cb in list(self._state_callbacks):
            try:
                cb(state)
            except Exception:
                log.exception("connection-state callback raised; continuing")
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `qumidi/transport/tcp.py`**

```python
"""TCP MIDI transport for Qu mixers (PRD 4.2, 4.3)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator

from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionError
from qumidi.midi.messages import MidiMessage, SystemRealtime
from qumidi.midi.parser import MidiStreamParser
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c
from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState, Transport

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
wire = logging.getLogger("qumidi.wire")

_SINGLE_CONNECTION_HINT = (
    "The Qu permits exactly one TCP MIDI connection at a time. If Qu-Pad, "
    "Qu-Control or another client holds the session, close it and retry."
)


class TcpTransport(Transport):
    """One TCP session to a Qu, with keepalive and inbound-liveness accounting."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        host: str,
        port: int = c.QU_TCP_PORT,
        *,
        connect_timeout: float = c.CONNECT_TIMEOUT,
        keepalive_interval: float = c.KEEPALIVE_INTERVAL,
        inbound_timeout: float = c.INBOUND_TIMEOUT,
        forward_active_sense: bool = False,
    ) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.host = host
        self.port = port
        self.connect_timeout = connect_timeout
        self.keepalive_interval = keepalive_interval
        self.inbound_timeout = inbound_timeout
        self.forward_active_sense = forward_active_sense

        self._reader: asyncio.StreamReader | None = None
        self._writer: asyncio.StreamWriter | None = None
        self._parser = MidiStreamParser()
        self._inbox: asyncio.Queue[MidiMessage] = asyncio.Queue(maxsize=4096)
        self._tasks: list[asyncio.Task[None]] = []
        self._last_inbound = 0.0

    # -- lifecycle --------------------------------------------------------

    async def connect(self) -> None:
        self.set_state(ConnectionState.CONNECTING)
        try:
            self._reader, self._writer = await asyncio.wait_for(
                asyncio.open_connection(self.host, self.port), timeout=self.connect_timeout
            )
        except (OSError, asyncio.TimeoutError) as exc:
            self.set_state(ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED)
            raise QuConnectionError(
                f"could not connect to {self.host}:{self.port} -- {exc}. {_SINGLE_CONNECTION_HINT}"
            ) from exc

        self._last_inbound = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
        self._parser = MidiStreamParser()
        self._tasks = [
            asyncio.create_task(self._read_loop(), name="qumidi-read"),
            asyncio.create_task(self._keepalive_loop(), name="qumidi-keepalive"),
            asyncio.create_task(self._liveness_loop(), name="qumidi-liveness"),
        ]
        self.set_state(ConnectionState.CONNECTED)

    async def close(self) -> None:
        """Idempotent. Cancels every task and closes the socket."""
        for task in self._tasks:
            task.cancel()
        for task in self._tasks:
            try:
                await task
            except (asyncio.CancelledError, Exception):  # noqa: B014 - never propagate
                pass
        self._tasks = []
        if self._writer is not None:
            self._writer.close()
            try:
                await self._writer.wait_closed()
            except OSError:
                pass
        self._reader = None
        self._writer = None
        self.set_state(ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED)

    @property
    def is_connected(self) -> bool:
        return self._writer is not None and not self._writer.is_closing()

    # -- io ---------------------------------------------------------------

    async def send(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        if self._writer is None or self._writer.is_closing():
            raise QuConnectionError(f"not connected to {self.host}:{self.port}")
        if wire.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
            wire.debug("TX %s", data.hex(" "))
        self._writer.write(data)
        await self._writer.drain()

    async def messages(self) -> AsyncIterator[MidiMessage]:
        while True:
            yield await self._inbox.get()

    # -- loops ------------------------------------------------------------

    async def _read_loop(self) -> None:
        assert self._reader is not None
        try:
            while True:
                data = await self._reader.read(4096)
                if not data:
                    break
                self._last_inbound = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
                if wire.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
                    wire.debug("RX %s", data.hex(" "))
                for msg in self._parser.feed(data):
                    if isinstance(msg, SystemRealtime) and not self.forward_active_sense:
                        # PRD 4.3: FE is consumed for liveness accounting and
                        # not forwarded to upper layers by default.
                        continue
                    try:
                        self._inbox.put_nowait(msg)
                    except asyncio.QueueFull:
                        log.warning("inbound queue full; dropping %r", msg)
        except (OSError, asyncio.IncompleteReadError) as exc:
            log.info("read loop ended: %s", exc)
        except asyncio.CancelledError:
            raise
        self._on_link_down()

    async def _keepalive_loop(self) -> None:
        # PRD 4.3: send FE unconditionally. Safe under both keepalive regimes
        # and satisfies the 5s iPadFlag=1 deadline.
        while True:
            await asyncio.sleep(self.keepalive_interval)
            if not self.is_connected:
                return
            try:
                assert self._writer is not None
                self._writer.write(bytes([c.ACTIVE_SENSING]))
                await self._writer.drain()
            except OSError as exc:
                log.info("keepalive write failed: %s", exc)
                return

    async def _liveness_loop(self) -> None:
        # PRD 4.3: declare the link dead ourselves rather than waiting for the
        # socket to notice.
        while True:
            await asyncio.sleep(self.inbound_timeout / 4)
            loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
            if loop.time() - self._last_inbound > self.inbound_timeout:
                log.warning(
                    "no inbound bytes for %.1fs; declaring the link dead", self.inbound_timeout
                )
                self._on_link_down()
                return

    def _on_link_down(self) -> None:
        if self._writer is not None:
            self._writer.close()
        self.set_state(ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED)
```

### Task 1.6: FakeQu skeleton

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Create: `tests/conftest.py`
- Test: `tests/testing/test_fake_qu.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `constants`, `sysex`, `MidiStreamParser`.
- Produces:
  - `FakeQu(model: str = "qu32", *, midi_channel: int = 0, firmware: tuple[int,int] = (1,9), box_id: int | None = None, echo: bool = True, send_end_marker: bool = True, sync_delay: float = 0.0, keepalive_interval: float = 0.3, idle_timeout: float = 12.0, ipad_timeout: float = 5.0)`.
  - `await fake.start() -> int` (returns the bound port), `await fake.stop()`, `fake.connections: int`, `fake.received: bytearray`.
  - A pytest fixture `fake_qu` in `tests/conftest.py` yielding a started `FakeQu` on an ephemeral port.
  - `python -m qumidi.testing.fake_qu --model qu32 --port 51325`.

This task builds only what Slice 1 needs: accept one connection, refuse the second, emit `FE`, answer the all-call `10` with `11`, then send the `14` end marker. Slice 3 adds the NRPN state burst, Slice 4 the echo, Slice 6 the name table, Slice 10 the meter stream, Slice 8 the fault modes.

import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.midi.messages import SysEx
from qumidi.midi.parser import MidiStreamParser
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import get_system_state, parse_sysex


async def read_qu_sysex(reader: asyncio.StreamReader, command: int, timeout: float = 2.0):
    """Read until a Qu sysex with the given command byte arrives."""
    parser = MidiStreamParser()
    async def _pump():
        while True:
            data = await reader.read(4096)
            if not data:
                raise AssertionError("connection closed while waiting for command")
            for msg in parser.feed(data):
                if isinstance(msg, SysEx):
                    parsed = parse_sysex(msg)
                    if parsed is not None and parsed.command == command:
                        return parsed
    return await asyncio.wait_for(_pump(), timeout=timeout)


async def test_answers_the_all_call_state_request(fake_qu) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake_qu.port)
    try:
        writer.write(get_system_state(1))
        await writer.drain()
        reply = await read_qu_sysex(reader, 0x11)
        assert reply.midi_channel == fake_qu.midi_channel
        assert reply.payload == bytes([3, 1, 9])  # qu32, firmware 1.9
    finally:
        writer.close()


async def test_sends_the_end_of_sync_marker(fake_qu) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake_qu.port)
    try:
        writer.write(get_system_state(1))
        await writer.drain()
        await read_qu_sysex(reader, 0x11)
        end = await read_qu_sysex(reader, 0x14)
        assert end.payload == b""
    finally:
        writer.close()


async def test_emits_active_sensing_on_connect(fake_qu) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake_qu.port)
    try:
        data = await asyncio.wait_for(reader.read(16), timeout=1.0)
        assert 0xFE in data
    finally:
        writer.close()


async def test_refuses_a_second_connection(fake_qu) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 13.1: mirroring real behaviour, which tests must cover.
    r1, w1 = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake_qu.port)
    try:
        r2, w2 = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake_qu.port)
        assert await asyncio.wait_for(r2.read(1), timeout=1.0) == b""
        w2.close()
    finally:
        w1.close()


@pytest.mark.parametrize("model,box_id", [("qu16", 1), ("qu24", 2), ("qu32", 3), ("qupac", 4), ("qusb", 5)])
async def test_reports_the_configured_model(fake_qu_factory, model, box_id) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model=model)
    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake.port)
    try:
        writer.write(get_system_state(1))
        await writer.drain()
        reply = await read_qu_sysex(reader, 0x11)
        assert reply.payload[0] == box_id
    finally:
        writer.close()


async def test_reports_a_non_zero_midi_channel(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 16.15: the suite must pass with a non-zero N, not only the default.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(midi_channel=5)
    reader, writer = await asyncio.open_connection("127.0.0.1", fake.port)
    try:
        writer.write(get_system_state(1))
        await writer.drain()
        reply = await read_qu_sysex(reader, 0x11)
        assert reply.midi_channel == 5
    finally:
        writer.close()

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`**

```python
"""FakeQu -- an asyncio server implementing the Qu's state machine (PRD 13.1).

This is both the primary test fixture and a shipped dev tool, so that anyone
can build against qumidi without owning a console. Run it standalone with:

    python -m qumidi.testing.fake_qu --model qu32 --port 51325
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import asyncio
import logging

from qumidi.midi.messages import SysEx
from qumidi.midi.parser import MidiStreamParser
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import build_sysex, parse_sysex

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

#: model name -> BoxID (PRD 6.2).
BOX_IDS = {"qu16": 1, "qu24": 2, "qu32": 3, "qupac": 4, "qusb": 5}


class FakeQu:
    """A configurable stand-in for a real Qu console."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: str = "qu32",
        *,
        midi_channel: int = 0,
        firmware: tuple[int, int] = c.MIN_FIRMWARE,
        box_id: int | None = None,
        echo: bool = True,
        send_end_marker: bool = True,
        sync_delay: float = 0.0,
        keepalive_interval: float = 0.3,
    ) -> None:
        self.model = model
        self.midi_channel = midi_channel
        self.firmware = firmware
        self.box_id = BOX_IDS[model] if box_id is None else box_id
        self.echo = echo
        self.send_end_marker = send_end_marker
        self.sync_delay = sync_delay
        self.keepalive_interval = keepalive_interval

        self.port = 0
        self.connections = 0
        self.received = bytearray()
        self._server: asyncio.AbstractServer | None = None
        self._client: asyncio.StreamWriter | None = None
        self._tasks: set[asyncio.Task[None]] = set()

    async def start(self, host: str = "127.0.0.1", port: int = 0) -> int:
        self._server = await asyncio.start_server(self._handle, host, port)
        self.port = int(self._server.sockets[0].getsockname()[1])
        return self.port

    async def stop(self) -> None:
        for task in self._tasks:
            task.cancel()
        self._tasks.clear()
        if self._client is not None:
            self._client.close()
            self._client = None
        if self._server is not None:
            self._server.close()
            await self._server.wait_closed()
            self._server = None

    # -- connection handling ---------------------------------------------

    async def _handle(self, reader: asyncio.StreamReader, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        if self._client is not None:
            # PRD 4.2: exactly one TCP MIDI connection at a time.
            log.info("refusing a second connection")
            writer.close()
            return
        self._client = writer
        self.connections += 1
        keepalive = asyncio.create_task(self._keepalive(writer))
        self._tasks.add(keepalive)
        try:
            writer.write(bytes([c.ACTIVE_SENSING]))
            await writer.drain()
            parser = MidiStreamParser()
            while data := await reader.read(4096):
                self.received.extend(data)
                for msg in parser.feed(data):
                    await self._on_message(msg, writer)
        except (ConnectionResetError, asyncio.CancelledError, OSError):
            pass
        finally:
            keepalive.cancel()
            self._tasks.discard(keepalive)
            if self._client is writer:
                self._client = None
            writer.close()

    async def _keepalive(self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        try:
            while True:
                await asyncio.sleep(self.keepalive_interval)
                writer.write(bytes([c.ACTIVE_SENSING]))
                await writer.drain()
        except (asyncio.CancelledError, OSError):
            pass

    async def _on_message(self, msg: object, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        if not isinstance(msg, SysEx):
            return
        parsed = parse_sysex(msg)
        if parsed is None:
            return
        if parsed.command == c.CMD_GET_SYSTEM_STATE:
            await self._do_sync(writer)

    async def _do_sync(self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        writer.write(
            build_sysex(
                self.midi_channel,
                bytes([c.CMD_SYSTEM_STATE_REPLY, self.box_id, *self.firmware]),
            )
        )
        await writer.drain()
        if self.sync_delay:
            await asyncio.sleep(self.sync_delay)
        await self._push_state(writer)
        if self.send_end_marker:
            writer.write(build_sysex(self.midi_channel, bytes([c.CMD_END_OF_SYNC])))
            await writer.drain()

    async def _push_state(self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        """Emit the canned scene as an NRPN burst. Filled in by Slice 3."""
        return None


def main() -> None:  # pragma: no cover - manual dev tool
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Fake Allen & Heath Qu mixer")
    ap.add_argument("--model", default="qu32", choices=sorted(BOX_IDS))
    ap.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=c.QU_TCP_PORT)
    ap.add_argument("--host", default="127.0.0.1")
    ap.add_argument("--midi-channel", type=int, default=0, help="wire channel 0-15")
    ap.add_argument("--no-echo", action="store_true")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)

    async def run() -> None:
        fake = FakeQu(args.model, midi_channel=args.midi_channel, echo=not args.no_echo)
        await fake.start(args.host, args.port)
        print(f"FakeQu {args.model} listening on {args.host}:{fake.port}")
        await asyncio.Event().wait()

    try:
        asyncio.run(run())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        pass


if __name__ == "__main__":  # pragma: no cover
    main()
```

- Produces:

  - `await mixer.connect()`, `await mixer.close()`, `async with mixer:` support.
  - `await mixer.wait_synced(timeout: float | None = None) -> None`.
  - Read-only properties `mixer.box_id: int | None`, `mixer.firmware: tuple[int,int] | None`, `mixer.midi_channel: int | None` (wire, 0-based), `mixer.midi_channel_display: int | None` (1-based), `mixer.state: ConnectionState`.
  - `qumidi probe --host H [--port P]` printing those values.

Slices 2–8 grow this class; nothing here is throwaway. `model` (a `Model` enum) arrives in Slice 2 — for now `box_id` is the raw byte.

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionError, SyncTimeoutError
from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState


async def test_probe_learns_box_id_firmware_and_channel(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu24", midi_channel=5, firmware=(1, 9))
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:

        # the console displays.
        assert mixer.midi_channel_display == 6
        assert mixer.state is ConnectionState.READY

    # PRD 9.3.9: no 11 within reply_timeout means the host is not a Qu, or

    port = int(server.sockets[0].getsockname()[1])
    try:

        await mixer.close()
    finally:

        await server.wait_closed()

    # PRD 9.3.9: a 11 but no 14 is a *different* failure and must say so.

    with pytest.raises(SyncTimeoutError):
        await mixer.connect()
    await mixer.close()


async def test_context_manager_closes_cleanly(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    before = len(asyncio.all_tasks())
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
    assert len(asyncio.all_tasks()) <= before

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/client_async.py`**

```python
"""The async client (PRD 9.3, 11.1).

Slice 1 scope: connect, run the state-sync handshake, learn BoxID, firmware
and the console's MIDI channel. Later slices add the cache, events, the
registry-driven set/get, names, scenes, reconnect and meters.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from types import TracebackType

from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionError, SyncTimeoutError
from qumidi.midi.messages import MidiMessage, SysEx
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import get_system_state, parse_system_state_reply, parse_sysex
from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState, Transport
from qumidi.transport.tcp import TcpTransport

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_NOT_A_QU_HINT = (
    "no system-state reply from {host}:{port} within {timeout}s. Either the "
    "host is not a Qu mixer, or another client holds the session -- the Qu "
    "permits exactly one TCP MIDI connection at a time."
)


class AsyncQuMixer:
    """Async-first control of one Qu console."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        host: str,
        port: int = c.QU_TCP_PORT,
        *,
        ipad_flag: int = c.DEFAULT_IPAD_FLAG,
        reply_timeout: float = c.REPLY_TIMEOUT,
        sync_timeout: float = c.SYNC_TIMEOUT,
        transport: Transport | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.host = host
        self.port = port
        self.ipad_flag = ipad_flag
        self.reply_timeout = reply_timeout
        self.sync_timeout = sync_timeout

        self._transport = transport if transport is not None else TcpTransport(host, port)
        self._reader_task: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None
        self._reply_event = asyncio.Event()
        self._sync_event = asyncio.Event()

        self._box_id: int | None = None
        self._firmware: tuple[int, int] | None = None
        self._midi_channel: int | None = None

    # -- properties -------------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def box_id(self) -> int | None:
        return self._box_id

    @property
    def firmware(self) -> tuple[int, int] | None:
        return self._firmware

    @property
    def midi_channel(self) -> int | None:
        """The console's MIDI channel as a wire value, 0-15."""
        return self._midi_channel

    @property
    def midi_channel_display(self) -> int | None:
        """The console's MIDI channel as the console displays it, 1-16."""
        return None if self._midi_channel is None else self._midi_channel + 1

    @property
    def state(self) -> ConnectionState:
        return self._transport.state

    # -- lifecycle --------------------------------------------------------

    async def connect(self) -> None:
        await self._transport.connect()
        self._reader_task = asyncio.create_task(self._read_loop(), name="qumidi-client-read")
        await self._sync()

    async def close(self) -> None:
        if self._reader_task is not None:
            self._reader_task.cancel()
            try:
                await self._reader_task
            except (asyncio.CancelledError, Exception):  # noqa: B014
                pass
            self._reader_task = None
        await self._transport.close()

    async def __aenter__(self) -> AsyncQuMixer:
        await self.connect()
        return self

    async def __aexit__(
        self,
        exc_type: type[BaseException] | None,
        exc: BaseException | None,
        tb: TracebackType | None,
    ) -> None:
        await self.close()

    async def wait_synced(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> None:
        """Block until the state push has completed (PRD 11.1)."""
        await asyncio.wait_for(self._sync_event.wait(), timeout=timeout)

    # -- sync -------------------------------------------------------------

    async def _sync(self) -> None:
        self._reply_event.clear()
        self._sync_event.clear()
        self._transport.set_state(ConnectionState.SYNCING)
        # PRD 9.1: the all-call form is required here, because the mixer's
        # MIDI channel is unknown until it replies.
        await self._transport.send(get_system_state(self.ipad_flag))
        try:
            await asyncio.wait_for(self._reply_event.wait(), timeout=self.reply_timeout)
        except asyncio.TimeoutError as exc:
            raise QuConnectionError(
                _NOT_A_QU_HINT.format(host=self.host, port=self.port, timeout=self.reply_timeout)
            ) from exc
        try:
            await asyncio.wait_for(self._sync_event.wait(), timeout=self.sync_timeout)
        except asyncio.TimeoutError as exc:
            raise SyncTimeoutError(
                f"{self.host}:{self.port} replied to the state request but sent no end-of-sync "
                f"marker within {self.sync_timeout}s; the state push failed"
            ) from exc
        self._transport.set_state(ConnectionState.READY)

    # -- inbound ----------------------------------------------------------

    async def _read_loop(self) -> None:
        async for msg in self._transport.messages():
            try:
                self._dispatch(msg)
            except Exception:
                # PRD: the read loop must never die on one bad message.
                log.exception("error handling inbound message %r", msg)

    def _dispatch(self, msg: MidiMessage) -> None:
        if isinstance(msg, SysEx):
            self._on_sysex(msg)

    def _on_sysex(self, msg: SysEx) -> None:
        parsed = parse_sysex(msg)
        if parsed is None:
            return
        if parsed.command == c.CMD_SYSTEM_STATE_REPLY:
            self._on_state_reply(parsed)
        elif parsed.command == c.CMD_END_OF_SYNC:
            self._sync_event.set()

    def _on_state_reply(self, parsed: object) -> None:
        from qumidi.protocol.sysex import QuSysex

        assert isinstance(parsed, QuSysex)
        if self._reply_event.is_set():
            # PRD 9.3.10: ignore a second 11 reply; bind to the first.
            log.warning(
                "second system-state reply (BoxID %s) after binding to BoxID %s; ignoring",
                parsed.payload[0] if parsed.payload else "?",
                self._box_id,
            )
            return
        try:
            box_id, firmware = parse_system_state_reply(parsed)
        except ValueError as exc:
            log.warning("malformed system-state reply: %s", exc)
            return
        if parsed.midi_channel is None:
            log.warning("system-state reply carried the all-call channel byte; ignoring")
            return
        self._box_id = box_id
        self._firmware = firmware
        self._midi_channel = parsed.midi_channel
        log.info(
            "connected to BoxID %d firmware %d.%d on MIDI channel %d",
            box_id,
            firmware[0],
            firmware[1],
            parsed.midi_channel + 1,
        )
        self._reply_event.set()
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `qumidi/cli.py` with the `probe` subcommand**

```python
"""Command line tools (PRD 17).

``probe`` is the first thing to run when something is wrong: connect, report
model, firmware and MIDI channel, disconnect.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import asyncio
import logging
import sys

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import QuError
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c


async def cmd_probe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
    async with AsyncQuMixer(args.host, port=args.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        print(f"host:          {args.host}:{args.port}")
        print(f"BoxID:         {mixer.box_id}")
        assert mixer.firmware is not None
        print(f"firmware:      {mixer.firmware[0]}.{mixer.firmware[1]}")
        print(f"MIDI channel:  {mixer.midi_channel_display} (wire {mixer.midi_channel})")
    return 0


def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="qumidi", description="Allen & Heath Qu control")
    ap.add_argument("-v", "--verbose", action="store_true", help="debug logging")
    ap.add_argument("--wire", action="store_true", help="hex-dump all MIDI traffic")
    sub = ap.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)

    probe = sub.add_parser("probe", help="connect, report model/firmware/channel, disconnect")
    probe.add_argument("--host", required=True)
    probe.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=c.QU_TCP_PORT)
    probe.set_defaults(func=cmd_probe)

    return ap


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    args = build_parser().parse_args(argv)
    logging.basicConfig(
        level=logging.DEBUG if args.verbose else logging.WARNING,
        format="%(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s",
    )
    if args.wire:
        logging.getLogger("qumidi.wire").setLevel(logging.DEBUG)
    try:
        return asyncio.run(args.func(args))
    except QuError as exc:
        print(f"error: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        return 130


if __name__ == "__main__":  # pragma: no cover
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

# Slice 2 — It can mute a channel

**Slice goal:** `qumidi set --host H mute input:6 on` lights the mute LED on channel 6 of a real console. This is the first write path, and it forces channel identity, model capabilities, and the ordered outbound queue into existence.

**Why mute first, before faders:** mute is Note On/Off, not NRPN (PRD §7.2). It exercises the entire write path — identity, capability check, queue, socket — without needing the registry, the taper tables, or NRPN assembly. If mute works, the plumbing is right and slice 3 only has to get the encoding right.

---

### Task 2.1: Channel identity

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/channels.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_channels.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `errors`.
- Produces:
  - `ChannelClass` enum: `FX_SEND, FX_RETURN, DCA, INPUT, STEREO_INPUT, MUTE_GROUP, MIX_MONO, MIX_STEREO, LR, GROUP, MATRIX`, plus the two synthetic classes `GLOBAL` and `DSNAKE` used for cache keying only (PRD §7.2, §10.1a).
  - `ChannelId` frozen dataclass: `.value: int` (the wire `CH` byte), `.cls: ChannelClass`, `.label: str`, `.index: int` (1-based within its class).
  - Factories, all 1-based: `Input(n)`, `StereoInput(n)`, `Dca(n)`, `MuteGroup(n)`, `FxSend(n)`, `FxReturn(n)`, `Group(n)`, `Matrix(n)`.
  - `Mix` namespace with `Mix.MIX1..MIX4`, `Mix.MIX56`, `Mix.MIX78`, `Mix.MIX910`, `Mix.LR` — each a `ChannelId`.
  - `GLOBAL: ChannelId` and `dsnake_socket_channel(socket: int) -> ChannelId`.
  - `channel_from_raw(raw: int) -> ChannelId` for inbound decode.
  - `parse_channel_ref(text: str) -> ChannelId` for the CLI (`"input:6"`, `"mix:3"`, `"lr"`, `"dca:2"`).

**The core invariant this type enforces (PRD §6.3):** `CH` is always a channel *identity*, never a strip position. Input 6 is `0x25` whether it sits on Layer A strip 6, a Custom Layer strip 3, or nowhere on the surface at all. Nothing in this module knows what a layer is.

import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import (
    GLOBAL,
    ChannelClass,
    Dca,
    FxReturn,
    FxSend,
    Group,
    Input,
    Matrix,
    Mix,
    MuteGroup,
