```python
)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "channel,raw,cls",
    [
        (FxSend(1), 0x00, ChannelClass.FX_SEND),
        (FxSend(4), 0x03, ChannelClass.FX_SEND),
        (FxReturn(1), 0x08, ChannelClass.FX_RETURN),
        (FxReturn(4), 0x0B, ChannelClass.FX_RETURN),
        (Dca(1), 0x10, ChannelClass.DCA),
        (Dca(4), 0x13, ChannelClass.DCA),
        (Input(1), 0x20, ChannelClass.INPUT),
        (Input(6), 0x25, ChannelClass.INPUT),
        (Input(32), 0x3F, ChannelClass.INPUT),
        (StereoInput(1), 0x40, ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT),
        (StereoInput(3), 0x42, ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT),
        (MuteGroup(1), 0x50, ChannelClass.MUTE_GROUP),
        (MuteGroup(4), 0x53, ChannelClass.MUTE_GROUP),
        (Mix.MIX1, 0x60, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO),
        (Mix.MIX4, 0x63, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO),
        (Mix.MIX56, 0x64, ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO),
        (Mix.MIX910, 0x66, ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO),
        (Mix.LR, 0x67, ChannelClass.LR),
        (Group(1), 0x68, ChannelClass.GROUP),
        (Group(4), 0x6B, ChannelClass.GROUP),
        (Matrix(1), 0x6C, ChannelClass.MATRIX),
        (Matrix(2), 0x6D, ChannelClass.MATRIX),
    ],
)
def test_channel_ids_match_the_spec_table(channel, raw, cls) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    assert channel.value == raw
    assert channel.cls is cls


def test_mix_is_seven_ids_not_ten() -> None:
    # PRD 6.1: "Mix 1 to 10" occupies only seven ids because 5-6/7-8/9-10 are stereo.
    ids = {Mix.MIX1, Mix.MIX2, Mix.MIX3, Mix.MIX4, Mix.MIX56, Mix.MIX78, Mix.MIX910}
    assert len(ids) == 7
    assert {m.value for m in ids} == set(range(0x60, 0x67))


def test_lr_is_its_own_class_not_a_stereo_mix() -> None:
    # PRD 6.1: pan and delay behave differently on LR, and the meter layout
    # treats it as a separate block.
    assert Mix.LR.cls is ChannelClass.LR
    assert Mix.MIX56.cls is ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO


def test_factories_are_one_based_and_range_checked() -> None:
    assert Input(1).value == 0x20
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Input(0)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Input(33)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Dca(5)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Matrix(3)


def test_labels_use_the_numbers_the_console_displays() -> None:
    assert Input(6).label == "Input 6"
    assert Dca(2).label == "DCA 2"
    assert Mix.MIX56.label == "Mix 5-6"
    assert Mix.LR.label == "LR"
    assert Group(3).label == "Group 5-6"
    assert Matrix(2).label == "Matrix 3-4"


def test_channel_from_raw_round_trips() -> None:
    for raw in list(range(0x00, 0x04)) + list(range(0x20, 0x40)) + list(range(0x60, 0x6E)):
        assert channel_from_raw(raw).value == raw


def test_channel_from_raw_rejects_an_unassigned_byte() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        channel_from_raw(0x7E)


def test_global_is_synthetic_and_has_no_wire_value() -> None:
    # PRD 7.2: Group Mode has no documented CH; store it under a synthetic id.
    assert GLOBAL.cls is ChannelClass.GLOBAL
    assert GLOBAL.value == -1


def test_parse_channel_ref() -> None:
    assert parse_channel_ref("input:6") == Input(6)
    assert parse_channel_ref("mix:3") == Mix.MIX3
    assert parse_channel_ref("mix:5-6") == Mix.MIX56
    assert parse_channel_ref("lr") == Mix.LR
    assert parse_channel_ref("dca:2") == Dca(2)
    assert parse_channel_ref("st:1") == StereoInput(1)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        parse_channel_ref("banana:1")


def test_channel_ids_are_hashable_and_usable_as_dict_keys() -> None:
    assert {Input(6): "kick"}[Input(6)] == "kick"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/channels.py`**

```python
"""Channel identity (PRD 6.1, 6.3).

Core invariant: the Qu MIDI protocol is entirely identity-addressed and never
position-addressed. No message carries a physical strip position or a layer
index. Nothing in this module knows what a layer is, and nothing added to it
ever should -- see PRD 6.3 and qumidi/layout.py.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum, auto

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError


class ChannelClass(Enum):
    """One member per row of the PRD 6.1 channel table, plus two synthetics.

    MIX_MONO/MIX_STEREO/LR are deliberately separate: pan and delay behave
    differently on them and the meter layout treats them as separate blocks.
    """

    FX_SEND = auto()
    FX_RETURN = auto()
    DCA = auto()
    INPUT = auto()
    STEREO_INPUT = auto()
    MUTE_GROUP = auto()
    MIX_MONO = auto()
    MIX_STEREO = auto()
    LR = auto()
    GROUP = auto()
    MATRIX = auto()
    #: Console-global settings with no documented CH (Group Mode, PRD 7.2).
    GLOBAL = auto()
    #: Socket-addressed dSNAKE preamp state, keyed per socket (PRD 7.2).
    DSNAKE = auto()


@dataclass(frozen=True, order=True)
class ChannelId:
    """A channel identity. ``value`` is the wire ``CH`` byte; -1 for synthetics.

    Deliberately *not* an int subclass: PRD 14.4 requires that ChannelId and
    ParamId cannot be passed for one another, and PRD 14.7 requires the same
    of ChannelId and StripId.
    """

    value: int
    cls: ChannelClass
    index: int
    label: str


def _make(base: int, count: int, cls: ChannelClass, fmt: str, kind: str):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    def factory(n: int) -> ChannelId:
        if not 1 <= n <= count:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"{kind} number must be 1..{count}, got {n}")
        return ChannelId(value=base + n - 1, cls=cls, index=n, label=fmt.format(n=n))

    factory.__name__ = kind.replace(" ", "")
    return factory


Input = _make(0x20, 32, ChannelClass.INPUT, "Input {n}", "input")
StereoInput = _make(0x40, 3, ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT, "ST{n}", "stereo input")
Dca = _make(0x10, 4, ChannelClass.DCA, "DCA {n}", "DCA")
MuteGroup = _make(0x50, 4, ChannelClass.MUTE_GROUP, "Mute Group {n}", "mute group")
FxSend = _make(0x00, 4, ChannelClass.FX_SEND, "FX Send {n}", "FX send")
FxReturn = _make(0x08, 4, ChannelClass.FX_RETURN, "FX Return {n}", "FX return")


def Group(n: int) -> ChannelId:  # noqa: N802 - a factory that reads as a type
    """Audio group 1-2 .. 7-8, numbered 1..4 (PRD 6.1)."""
    if not 1 <= n <= 4:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"group number must be 1..4, got {n}")
    lo = 2 * n - 1
    return ChannelId(0x68 + n - 1, ChannelClass.GROUP, n, f"Group {lo}-{lo + 1}")


def Matrix(n: int) -> ChannelId:  # noqa: N802
    """Matrix 1-2 or 3-4, numbered 1..2 (PRD 6.1)."""
    if not 1 <= n <= 2:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"matrix number must be 1..2, got {n}")
    lo = 2 * n - 1
    return ChannelId(0x6C + n - 1, ChannelClass.MATRIX, n, f"Matrix {lo}-{lo + 1}")


class Mix:
    """The seven mix identities and LR.

    PRD 6.1: "Mix 1 to 10" occupies only seven ids because 5-6, 7-8 and 9-10
    are stereo pairs. Model that explicitly rather than pretending there are ten.
    """

    MIX1 = ChannelId(0x60, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO, 1, "Mix 1")
    MIX2 = ChannelId(0x61, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO, 2, "Mix 2")
    MIX3 = ChannelId(0x62, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO, 3, "Mix 3")
    MIX4 = ChannelId(0x63, ChannelClass.MIX_MONO, 4, "Mix 4")
    MIX56 = ChannelId(0x64, ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO, 1, "Mix 5-6")
    MIX78 = ChannelId(0x65, ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO, 2, "Mix 7-8")
    MIX910 = ChannelId(0x66, ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO, 3, "Mix 9-10")
    LR = ChannelId(0x67, ChannelClass.LR, 1, "LR")


#: Console-global pseudo-channel for parameters with no documented CH (PRD 7.2).
GLOBAL = ChannelId(-1, ChannelClass.GLOBAL, 0, "GLOBAL")


def dsnake_socket_channel(socket: int) -> ChannelId:
    """A synthetic per-socket identity used only as a cache key (PRD 7.2)."""
    if not 0x00 <= socket <= 0x27:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"dSNAKE socket must be 0x00..0x27, got {socket:#04x}")
    return ChannelId(-1, ChannelClass.DSNAKE, socket, f"dSNAKE socket {socket}")


_ALL: list[ChannelId] = [
    *(FxSend(n) for n in range(1, 5)),
    *(FxReturn(n) for n in range(1, 5)),
    *(Dca(n) for n in range(1, 5)),
    *(Input(n) for n in range(1, 33)),
    *(StereoInput(n) for n in range(1, 4)),
    *(MuteGroup(n) for n in range(1, 5)),
    Mix.MIX1, Mix.MIX2, Mix.MIX3, Mix.MIX4, Mix.MIX56, Mix.MIX78, Mix.MIX910, Mix.LR,
    *(Group(n) for n in range(1, 5)),
    *(Matrix(n) for n in range(1, 3)),
]

_BY_RAW: dict[int, ChannelId] = {ch.value: ch for ch in _ALL}

ALL_CHANNELS: tuple[ChannelId, ...] = tuple(_ALL)


def channel_from_raw(raw: int) -> ChannelId:
    """Decode a wire ``CH`` byte. Raises for bytes the table does not assign."""
    try:
        return _BY_RAW[raw]
    except KeyError:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"no channel is assigned to CH {raw:#04x}") from None


_REF_FACTORIES = {
    "input": Input,
    "in": Input,
    "st": StereoInput,
    "stereo": StereoInput,
    "dca": Dca,
    "mutegroup": MuteGroup,
    "mg": MuteGroup,
    "group": Group,
    "grp": Group,
    "matrix": Matrix,
    "mtx": Matrix,
    "fxsend": FxSend,
    "fxreturn": FxReturn,
}

_MIX_REFS = {
    "1": Mix.MIX1, "2": Mix.MIX2, "3": Mix.MIX3, "4": Mix.MIX4,
    "5-6": Mix.MIX56, "56": Mix.MIX56,
    "7-8": Mix.MIX78, "78": Mix.MIX78,
    "9-10": Mix.MIX910, "910": Mix.MIX910,
}


def parse_channel_ref(text: str) -> ChannelId:
    """Parse a CLI channel reference such as ``input:6``, ``mix:5-6`` or ``lr``."""
    token = text.strip().lower()
    if token == "lr":
        return Mix.LR
    if ":" not in token:
        raise ParameterRangeError(
            f"channel reference {text!r} must look like 'input:6', 'mix:3', 'dca:2' or 'lr'"
        )
    kind, _, arg = token.partition(":")
    if kind == "mix":
        try:
            return _MIX_REFS[arg]
        except KeyError:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"unknown mix {arg!r}; valid: {', '.join(sorted(_MIX_REFS))}"
            ) from None
    factory = _REF_FACTORIES.get(kind)
    if factory is None:
        raise ParameterRangeError(
            f"unknown channel kind {kind!r} in {text!r}; valid: "
            f"{', '.join(sorted({*_REF_FACTORIES, 'mix', 'lr'}))}"
        )
    try:
        return factory(int(arg))
    except ValueError:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"channel number in {text!r} must be an integer") from None
```

### Task 2.2: Model capabilities

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/channels.py` (append the model section)
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py` (resolve BoxID to a model at sync)
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_models.py`
- Test: `tests/test_probe.py` (extend)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ChannelId`, `ChannelClass`, `errors`.
- Produces:
  - `Model` enum: `QU16, QU24, QU32, QUPAC, QUSB`.
  - `ModelCaps` frozen dataclass with `name, box_id, mono_inputs, stereo_inputs, mono_mixes, stereo_mixes, groups, matrices, fx_units, groups_provisional: bool`.
  - `CAPS_BY_BOX_ID: dict[int, ModelCaps]`, `PERMISSIVE_CAPS: ModelCaps`.
  - `check_channel(caps: ModelCaps | None, ch: ChannelId) -> None` — raises `UnsupportedOnModelError`, or warns when `caps is None` or the check is provisional.
  - `AsyncQuMixer.model: Model | None` and `.caps: ModelCaps | None` and `.raw_box_id: int | None`.

**Counts, not booleans (PRD §6.2).** A `has_groups: bool` cannot express Qu-24 having two group blocks against Qu-32's four, and would let the library address Group 5-6 on a console that has none.

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the model section to `qumidi/protocol/channels.py`**

```python
# --- Model capabilities (PRD 6.2) ----------------------------------------

import logging  # noqa: E402 - grouped with the model section for readability

_log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelCaps:
    """Counts, not booleans (PRD 6.2).

    ``mono_mixes``/``stereo_mixes``/``groups``/``matrices`` are *stereo pair*
    counts matching the channel-id table: mono_mixes=4, stereo_mixes=3 is the
    seven channel ids covering "Mix 1 to 10", not ten.
    """

    name: str
    box_id: int
    mono_inputs: int
    stereo_inputs: int
    mono_mixes: int
    stereo_mixes: int
    groups: int
    matrices: int
    fx_units: int
    #: True where the count is inferred rather than verified on hardware.
    groups_provisional: bool = False


class Model(Enum):
    QU16 = 1
    QU24 = 2
    QU32 = 3
    QUPAC = 4
    QUSB = 5

    @property
    def box_id(self) -> int:
        return int(self.value)

    @classmethod
    def from_box_id(cls, box_id: int) -> Model | None:
        try:
            return cls(box_id)
        except ValueError:
            return None


CAPS_BY_BOX_ID: dict[int, ModelCaps] = {
    1: ModelCaps("Qu-16", 1, 16, 3, 4, 3, 0, 0, 2),
    # PRD 6.2 dagger note: the 2 is inferred from the meter block table while
    # the channel-id table implies 4. M6 hardware item; provisional until then.
    2: ModelCaps("Qu-24", 2, 24, 3, 4, 3, 2, 2, 4, groups_provisional=True),
    3: ModelCaps("Qu-32", 3, 32, 3, 4, 3, 4, 2, 4),
    4: ModelCaps("Qu-Pac", 4, 32, 3, 4, 3, 4, 2, 4),
    5: ModelCaps("Qu-SB", 5, 32, 3, 4, 3, 4, 2, 4),
}

#: Used before sync and for unrecognised BoxIDs (PRD 6.2).
PERMISSIVE_CAPS = ModelCaps("unknown", -1, 32, 3, 4, 3, 4, 2, 4, groups_provisional=True)


def caps_for_box_id(box_id: int) -> ModelCaps:
    """Resolve a BoxID, falling back permissively for unrecognised consoles."""
    caps = CAPS_BY_BOX_ID.get(box_id)
    if caps is None:
        _log.warning(
            "unrecognised BoxID %d; falling back to the permissive capability set. "
            "Refusing to connect to hardware that would otherwise work is the worse failure.",
            box_id,
        )
        return PERMISSIVE_CAPS
    return caps


def _limit(caps: ModelCaps, ch: ChannelId) -> tuple[int, bool]:
    """Return (max valid 1-based index for this class, is_provisional)."""
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.INPUT:
        return caps.mono_inputs, False
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT:
        return caps.stereo_inputs, False
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.MIX_MONO:
        return caps.mono_mixes, False
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO:
        return caps.stereo_mixes, False
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.GROUP:
        return caps.groups, caps.groups_provisional
    if ch.cls is ChannelClass.MATRIX:
        return caps.matrices, False
    if ch.cls in (ChannelClass.FX_SEND, ChannelClass.FX_RETURN):
        return caps.fx_units, False
    # DCA, mute group, LR and the synthetics exist on every model.
    return ch.index, False


def check_channel(caps: ModelCaps | None, ch: ChannelId) -> None:
    """Raise UnsupportedOnModelError if ``ch`` does not exist on this model.

    Before sync (``caps is None``) checks are permissive with a warning, and a
    provisional count warns rather than raising (PRD 6.2).
    """
    if caps is None:
        _log.warning(
            "capability check for %s ran before sync; permitting it. Call "
            "wait_synced() first to get a real check.",
            ch.label,
        )
        return
    limit, provisional = _limit(caps, ch)
    if ch.index <= limit:
        return
    message = (
        f"{ch.label} does not exist on {caps.name} "
        f"(this model has {limit} of that channel class)"
    )
    if provisional:
        _log.warning(
            "%s -- but that count is provisional and unverified on hardware, so this is "
            "permitted rather than refused (PRD 6.2)",
            message,
        )
        return
    raise UnsupportedOnModelError(message)
```

### Task 2.3: The ordered outbound queue

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/transport/queue.py`
- Test: `tests/transport/test_queue.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing but `constants`.
- Produces: `OutboundQueue(sink: Callable[[bytes], Awaitable[None]], *, max_send_rate: int | None = 1000, maxsize: int = 256)` with `start()`, `stop()`, `await send(data)` (resolves when drained), `await send_priority(data)` (bypasses the throttle entirely), `pending: int`, `clear()`.

**Why this exists (PRD §4.5):** MIDI-over-TCP has no flow control back to the console's MIDI parser. A caller doing a 128-step fade or a `set_all_mutes()` loop emits thousands of bytes in one event-loop tick and the mixer drops what it cannot parse — silently, exactly like a wrong `VX`. Every outbound byte except keepalive and meter enable/disable goes through here.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/transport/test_queue.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.transport.queue import OutboundQueue


class Sink:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.writes: list[bytes] = []
        self.times: list[float] = []

    async def __call__(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        self.writes.append(data)
        self.times.append(asyncio.get_running_loop().time())

    @property
    def joined(self) -> bytes:
        return b"".join(self.writes)


async def test_send_resolves_only_after_the_sink_has_seen_the_bytes() -> None:
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=None)
    await q.start()
    try:
        await q.send(b"\x01\x02")
        # PRD 4.5: resolves when handed to the socket and drained, not queued.
        assert sink.joined == b"\x01\x02"
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_writes_are_transmitted_in_issue_order() -> None:
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=None)
    await q.start()
    try:
        await asyncio.gather(*(q.send(bytes([i])) for i in range(20)))
        assert sink.joined == bytes(range(20))
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_concurrent_sends_never_interleave_a_multi_byte_payload() -> None:
    # PRD 4.5: concurrent set() calls from different tasks never interleave
    # the four CCs of an NRPN triplet.
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=None)
    await q.start()
    try:
        a = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17])
        b = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x26, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17])
        await asyncio.gather(*(q.send(a if i % 2 else b) for i in range(20)))
        for write in sink.writes:
            assert write in (a, b)
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_throttle_limits_the_byte_rate() -> None:
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=1000)
    await q.start()
    try:
        start = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
        for _ in range(20):
            await q.send(bytes(20))  # 400 bytes at 1000 B/s -> ~0.4s
        elapsed = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() - start
        assert elapsed >= 0.3, elapsed
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_priority_send_bypasses_the_throttle() -> None:
    # PRD 4.5: keepalive and meter enable/disable must never be starved by a
    # bulk write.
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=200)
    await q.start()
    try:
        bulk = asyncio.create_task(asyncio.gather(*(q.send(bytes(50)) for _ in range(10))))
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        start = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
        await q.send_priority(b"\xfe")
        assert asyncio.get_running_loop().time() - start < 0.05
        assert b"\xfe" in sink.writes
        bulk.cancel()
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_pending_reports_the_backlog() -> None:
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=100)
    await q.start()
    try:
        tasks = [asyncio.create_task(q.send(bytes(20))) for _ in range(8)]
        await asyncio.sleep(0.02)
        assert q.pending > 0
        for t in tasks:
            t.cancel()
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_queue_is_bounded_and_applies_backpressure() -> None:
    # PRD 18: no unbounded queues anywhere; the outbound queue applies
    # backpressure to set() rather than growing.
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=50, maxsize=4)
    await q.start()
    try:
        tasks = [asyncio.create_task(q.send(bytes(10))) for _ in range(12)]
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert q.pending <= 4
        for t in tasks:
            t.cancel()
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_clear_drops_the_backlog_and_fails_its_waiters() -> None:
    # PRD 4.4: drop the outbound queue on disconnect.
    sink = Sink()
    q = OutboundQueue(sink, max_send_rate=50)
    await q.start()
    try:
        tasks = [asyncio.create_task(q.send(bytes(20))) for _ in range(6)]
        await asyncio.sleep(0.02)
        q.clear()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert q.pending == 0
        for t in tasks:
            t.cancel()
    finally:
        await q.stop()


async def test_stop_leaves_no_tasks() -> None:
    before = len(asyncio.all_tasks())
    q = OutboundQueue(Sink(), max_send_rate=None)
    await q.start()
    await q.stop()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.02)
    assert len(asyncio.all_tasks()) <= before
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/transport/queue.py`**

```python
"""Ordered, rate-limited outbound queue (PRD 4.5).

MIDI-over-TCP has no flow control back to the console's MIDI parser. Emitting
a 128-step fade in one event-loop tick makes the mixer drop what it cannot
parse -- silently, exactly like a wrong VX byte. Everything outbound except
the keepalive and the meter enable/disable passes through here, in issue order.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable

from qumidi.protocol import constants as c

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

Sink = Callable[[bytes], Awaitable[None]]

_Item = tuple[bytes, "asyncio.Future[None]"]


class OutboundQueue:
    """A single ordered send queue with a byte-rate throttle."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        sink: Sink,
        *,
        max_send_rate: int | None = c.MAX_SEND_RATE,
        maxsize: int = c.OUTBOUND_QUEUE_MAXSIZE,
    ) -> None:
        self._sink = sink
        self.max_send_rate = max_send_rate
        self._queue: asyncio.Queue[_Item] = asyncio.Queue(maxsize=maxsize)
        self._worker: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None
        #: Serialises priority writes against the worker's own sink call, so a
        #: keepalive can never land inside another payload's bytes.
        self._sink_lock = asyncio.Lock()
        self._next_send_at = 0.0

    async def start(self) -> None:
        if self._worker is None:
            self._worker = asyncio.create_task(self._run(), name="qumidi-outbound")

    async def stop(self) -> None:
        if self._worker is not None:
            self._worker.cancel()
            try:
                await self._worker
            except (asyncio.CancelledError, Exception):  # noqa: B014
                pass
            self._worker = None
        self.clear()

    @property
    def pending(self) -> int:
        """Payloads queued but not yet handed to the socket."""
        return self._queue.qsize()

    async def send(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        """Queue ``data`` and return once it has been written and drained."""
        future: asyncio.Future[None] = asyncio.get_running_loop().create_future()
        await self._queue.put((data, future))
        await future

    async def send_priority(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        """Write immediately, bypassing both the queue and the throttle.

        Reserved for the keepalive FE and meter enable/disable: liveness must
        never be starved by a bulk write (PRD 4.5).
        """
        async with self._sink_lock:
            await self._sink(data)

    def clear(self) -> None:
        """Drop the backlog, failing every waiter (PRD 4.4, on disconnect)."""
        dropped = 0
        while True:
            try:
                _, future = self._queue.get_nowait()
            except asyncio.QueueEmpty:
                break
            if not future.done():
                future.cancel()
            dropped += 1
        if dropped:
            log.info("dropped %d queued outbound payloads", dropped)

    async def _run(self) -> None:
        while True:
            data, future = await self._queue.get()
            try:
                await self._pace(len(data))
                async with self._sink_lock:
                    await self._sink(data)
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                if not future.done():
                    future.cancel()
                raise
            except Exception as exc:
                if not future.done():
                    future.set_exception(exc)
                continue
            if not future.done():
                future.set_result(None)

    async def _pace(self, nbytes: int) -> None:
        if self.max_send_rate is None:
            return
        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
        now = loop.time()
        if self._next_send_at > now:
            await asyncio.sleep(self._next_send_at - now)
            now = loop.time()
        self._next_send_at = now + nbytes / self.max_send_rate
```

### Task 2.4: Mute, end to end

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py` (queue wiring, `mute()`, `NotSyncedError` guard)
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py` (track mute state)
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py` (add `set`)
- Test: `tests/test_mute.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `OutboundQueue`, `ChannelId`, `check_channel`, `NoteOn`.
- Produces:
  - `await mixer.mute(ch: ChannelId, muted: bool) -> None`
  - `mixer.pending_writes: int`
  - `AsyncQuMixer._send(data: bytes)` — the single funnel every later write uses.
  - `AsyncQuMixer._await_channel(what) -> int` — the write path's channel guard. **This reconciles a tension in the PRD:** §11.1 says writes before sync are queued and flushed once the `11` reply establishes `N`; §11.3 lists `NotSyncedError` for a write needing `N` before sync. They reconcile as *wait if a connect is in flight, raise if none was ever started* — there is nothing to wait for in the second case. Document that a write issued this way is transmitted **after** the state burst and will therefore appear to "win" over the console's own state.
  - `FakeQu.mutes: dict[int, bool]`
  - `qumidi set --host H mute <channel-ref> on|off`

**The mute encoding (PRD §7.2), transcribed literally:**
```
Mute on:  9N CH 7F   then  9N CH 00
Mute off: 9N CH 3F   then  9N CH 00
```
On receive: velocity `00` and Note Off are **ignored**; `01`–`3F` = unmute; `40`–`7F` = mute. Both bytes go out as one queued payload so the pair can never be split by another writer.

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import NotSyncedError, UnsupportedOnModelError

async def test_mute_on_emits_the_documented_note_pair(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]

    # PRD 7.2: a Note On to 0x50-0x53 fires the whole mute group and to

    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:

    # PRD 11.3: NotSyncedError for an operation needing channel N before sync.
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=1)
    with pytest.raises(NotSyncedError):

async def test_a_write_issued_during_connect_is_queued_not_refused(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 11.1: writes before sync are queued and flushed after the 11 reply
    # establishes N -- they cannot be sent earlier because the MIDI channel is
    # not yet known. A write issued this way is transmitted AFTER the state
    # burst and will therefore appear to "win" over the console's own state.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(sync_delay=0.2)
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port)
    connecting = asyncio.create_task(mixer.connect())
    await asyncio.sleep(0.05)          # the 11 reply has not arrived yet
    await mixer.mute(Input(6), True)   # queues, then flushes
    await connecting
    try:
        assert fake.mutes[0x25] is True
    finally:
        await mixer.close()

    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu16")

        await mixer.wait_synced()
        with pytest.raises(UnsupportedOnModelError):
            await mixer.mute(Input(17), True)


async def test_the_note_pair_is_one_queued_payload(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # Concurrent mutes must never interleave their two notes.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fake.received.clear()
        await asyncio.gather(*(mixer.mute(Input(n), True) for n in range(1, 9)))
        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
    raw = bytes(fake.received)
    for n in range(1, 9):
        note = 0x20 + n - 1
        assert bytes([0x90, note, 0x7F, 0x90, note, 0x00]) in raw
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Wire the queue and `mute()` into `AsyncQuMixer`**

Add to `__init__` (after the transport is built):

```python
        self._outbound = OutboundQueue(
            self._transport.send, max_send_rate=max_send_rate
        )
```

with a new keyword argument `max_send_rate: int | None = c.MAX_SEND_RATE`.

In `connect()`, start the queue right after the transport connects and before `_sync()`:

```python
        await self._outbound.start()
```

In `close()`, stop it before closing the transport:

```python
        await self._outbound.stop()
```

Note that `_sync()` calls `self._transport.send(...)` directly rather than going through the queue: the system-state request must not queue behind anything, and at that moment nothing else can have been queued.

Add the write funnel and the mute method:

```python
    @property
    def pending_writes(self) -> int:
        """Payloads queued for the console but not yet on the wire (PRD 4.5)."""
        return self._outbound.pending

    def _require_synced(self, what: str) -> int:
        """Return the console's MIDI channel, or explain why we cannot write."""
        if self._midi_channel is None:
            raise NotSyncedError(
                f"cannot {what} before sync: the console's MIDI channel is not yet "
                f"known. Await connect()/wait_synced() first."
            )
        return self._midi_channel

    async def _await_channel(self, what: str) -> int:
        """Wait for the ``11`` reply if a connect is in flight, then return N.

        PRD 11.1 says writes before sync are queued and flushed once the reply
        establishes N; PRD 11.3 says NotSyncedError is raised for a write
        needing N before sync. Those reconcile as: if a connect is running,
        wait for it (the write queues); if no connect was ever started, raise,
        because there is nothing to wait for.
        """
        if self._midi_channel is not None:
            return self._midi_channel
        if self._reader_task is None:
            raise NotSyncedError(
                f"cannot {what}: not connected. Await connect() first."
            )
        await asyncio.wait_for(self._reply_event.wait(), timeout=self.reply_timeout)
        return self._require_synced(what)

    async def _send(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        """The single funnel for every control write (PRD 4.5)."""
        await self._outbound.send(data)

    async def mute(self, ch: ChannelId, muted: bool) -> None:
        """Mute or unmute a channel (PRD 7.2).

        Mute is Note On/Off, not NRPN. DCA masters (0x10-0x13) and mute-group
        masters (0x50-0x53) are muted exactly the same way -- firing a mute
        group has no dedicated message.
        """
        n = await self._await_channel(f"mute {ch.label}")
        check_channel(self._caps, ch)
        velocity = 0x7F if muted else 0x3F
        payload = (
            NoteOn(channel=n, note=ch.value, velocity=velocity).to_bytes()
            + NoteOn(channel=n, note=ch.value, velocity=0x00).to_bytes()
        )
        await self._send(payload)
```

Imports to add: `from qumidi.midi.messages import NoteOn`, `from qumidi.errors import NotSyncedError`, `from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelId, check_channel`, `from qumidi.transport.queue import OutboundQueue`.

```python
async def cmd_set(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
    from qumidi.protocol.channels import parse_channel_ref

    async with AsyncQuMixer(args.host, port=args.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        ch = parse_channel_ref(args.channel)
        if args.param == "mute":
            muted = args.value.lower() in ("on", "1", "true", "yes", "mute")
            await mixer.mute(ch, muted)
            print(f"{ch.label}: mute {'on' if muted else 'off'}")
            return 0
        print(f"error: unknown parameter {args.param!r}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
```

```python
    setp = sub.add_parser("set", help="write one parameter and exit")
    setp.add_argument("--host", required=True)
    setp.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=c.QU_TCP_PORT)
    setp.add_argument("param", help="parameter name, e.g. mute")
    setp.add_argument("channel", help="channel reference, e.g. input:6, mix:3, lr")
    setp.add_argument("value", help="value, e.g. on/off")
    setp.set_defaults(func=cmd_set)
```

# Slice 3 — It can move a fader

**Slice goal:** `qumidi set --host H fader input:6 -6db` physically moves fader 6 on a Qu-16/24/32. This forces NRPN assembly, the `ParamSpec` machinery, the fader taper, and the golden-vector fixture into existence.

**The risk this slice manages:** the `VX` filler byte is inconsistent across parameters and must be transcribed literally, never inferred. Getting it wrong produces messages the mixer silently ignores, which is nearly impossible to debug in the field (PRD §7.1). The golden-vector fixture exists primarily to guard against exactly that, and it is transcribed independently from the PDF, never generated from the registry — a fixture generated from the code under test proves nothing.

---

### Task 3.1: NRPN assembly and disassembly

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/midi/nrpn.py`
- Test: `tests/midi/test_nrpn.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `qumidi.midi.messages`, `constants`.
- Produces:
  - `encode_nrpn(midi_channel: int, ch: int, param_id: int, value: int, vx: int) -> bytes`
  - `RawParamUpdate` frozen dataclass: `midi_channel: int, ch: int, param_id: int, value: int, vx: int | None`
  - `NrpnDecoder()` with `feed(msg: MidiMessage) -> list[RawParamUpdate]`, `flush() -> list[RawParamUpdate]`, `tick(now: float) -> list[RawParamUpdate]`.

**The Qu's NRPN usage is idiosyncratic and must be modelled exactly (PRD §5.3):**
```
BN 63 CH    NRPN MSB   = channel id
BN 62 ID    NRPN LSB   = parameter id
BN 06 VA    Data MSB   = parameter value
BN 26 VX    Data LSB   = index / discriminator  <- NOT a fine value
```
`VX` is not a value refinement. It is either a filler constant or a genuine index.

**The four flush triggers.** The decoder emits on `06` and holds for at most one subsequent CC to attach a `26`. That held update must *also* be flushed on (a) the sysex `14` end-of-sync marker, (b) any non-CC message, and (c) `pending_flush_timeout` (50 ms) of inbound silence — otherwise the last parameter of every burst is lost forever. Realtime bytes must **not** flush it; `FE` arrives every 300 ms and would break every NRPN pair.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/midi/test_nrpn.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.midi.messages import ControlChange, NoteOn, SysEx
from qumidi.midi.nrpn import NrpnDecoder, RawParamUpdate, encode_nrpn
from qumidi.midi.parser import MidiStreamParser


def test_encode_emits_the_four_ccs_in_the_documented_order() -> None:
    assert encode_nrpn(0, ch=0x25, param_id=0x17, value=0x62, vx=0x07) == bytes(
        [0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62, 0xB0, 0x26, 0x07]
    )


def test_encode_uses_the_consoles_midi_channel() -> None:
    out = encode_nrpn(5, ch=0x25, param_id=0x17, value=0x62, vx=0x07)
    assert out[0] == 0xB5 and out[3] == 0xB5 and out[6] == 0xB5 and out[9] == 0xB5


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "kwargs",
    [
        dict(midi_channel=16, ch=0, param_id=0, value=0, vx=0),
        dict(midi_channel=0, ch=0x80, param_id=0, value=0, vx=0),
        dict(midi_channel=0, ch=0, param_id=0, value=0x80, vx=0),
        dict(midi_channel=0, ch=0, param_id=0, value=0, vx=0x80),
    ],
)
def test_encode_range_checks_every_field(kwargs) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        encode_nrpn(**kwargs)


def feed_bytes(decoder: NrpnDecoder, data: bytes) -> list[RawParamUpdate]:
    out: list[RawParamUpdate] = []
    for msg in MidiStreamParser().feed(data):
        out.extend(decoder.feed(msg))
    return out


def test_decode_a_complete_quad() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    out = feed_bytes(d, encode_nrpn(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, 0x07))
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert out == [
        RawParamUpdate(midi_channel=0, ch=0x25, param_id=0x17, value=0x62, vx=0x07)
    ]


def test_decode_under_running_status() -> None:
    # PRD 5.3: the (CH, ID) pair persists across messages under running status.
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    data = bytes(
        [0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0x62, 0x17, 0x06, 0x62, 0x26, 0x07, 0x62, 0x18, 0x06, 0x01, 0x26, 0x07]
    )
    out = feed_bytes(d, data)
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert out == [
        RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, 0x07),
        RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x18, 0x01, 0x07),
    ]


def test_update_with_no_data_lsb_emits_with_vx_none() -> None:
    # PRD 5.3: some senders omit the LSB. Emit on 06, revise on a following 26.
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    data = bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x18])
    out = feed_bytes(d, data)
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert out[0] == RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)


def test_stream_ending_after_data_msb_still_yields_one_update() -> None:
    # PRD 5.3: test explicitly that a stream ending in "... B0 06 62" with
    # nothing after it still yields one ParamUpdate.
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    out = feed_bytes(d, bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62]))
    assert out == []
    assert d.flush() == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)]


def test_active_sensing_does_not_flush_the_held_update() -> None:
    # PRD 5.3: FE arrives every 300ms and would break every NRPN pair if it
    # were treated as "a subsequent message".
    from qumidi.midi.messages import SystemRealtime

    d = NrpnDecoder()
    out = feed_bytes(d, bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62]))
    out.extend(d.feed(SystemRealtime(status=0xFE)))
    assert out == []
    out.extend(d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x26, value=0x07)))
    assert out == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, 0x07)]


def test_a_non_cc_message_flushes_the_held_update() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    out = feed_bytes(d, bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62]))
    out.extend(d.feed(NoteOn(channel=0, note=0x25, velocity=0x7F)))
    assert out == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)]


def test_a_sysex_flushes_the_held_update() -> None:
    # PRD 9.3.7: flush when 14 arrives, or the last parameter in the burst is
    # stranded and the cache is silently one value short after every sync.
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    out = feed_bytes(d, bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x25, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x17, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x62]))
    out.extend(d.feed(SysEx(data=bytes([0x00, 0x14]))))
    assert out == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)]


def test_tick_flushes_after_the_silence_timeout() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder(pending_flush_timeout=0.05, now=lambda: 100.0)
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x62, value=0x17))
    assert d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x06, value=0x62)) == []
    assert d.tick(now=100.02) == []
    assert d.tick(now=100.10) == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)]


def test_decoder_is_per_midi_channel() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x62, value=0x17))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=1, controller=0x63, value=0x26))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=1, controller=0x62, value=0x18))
    out = d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x06, value=0x62))
    out.extend(d.feed(ControlChange(channel=1, controller=0x06, value=0x63)))
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None) in out
    assert RawParamUpdate(1, 0x26, 0x18, 0x63, None) in out


def test_data_msb_with_no_preceding_address_is_ignored() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    assert d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x06, value=0x62)) == []


def test_bank_select_ccs_do_not_disturb_the_accumulator() -> None:
    # Scene recall sends CC 0 and CC 0x20 immediately before a program change.
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x63, value=0x25))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x62, value=0x17))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x00, value=0x00))
    d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x20, value=0x00))
    out = d.feed(ControlChange(channel=0, controller=0x06, value=0x62))
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert out == [RawParamUpdate(0, 0x25, 0x17, 0x62, None)]


def test_round_trip_over_the_parser_for_a_long_burst() -> None:
    d = NrpnDecoder()
    expected = []
    stream = b""
    for i in range(40):
        stream += encode_nrpn(0, ch=0x20 + (i % 16), param_id=0x17, value=i, vx=0x07)
        expected.append(RawParamUpdate(0, 0x20 + (i % 16), 0x17, i, 0x07))
    out = feed_bytes(d, stream)
    out.extend(d.flush())
    assert out == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/midi/nrpn.py`**

```python
"""NRPN assembly and disassembly (PRD 5.3).

The Qu's NRPN usage is idiosyncratic:

    BN 63 CH    NRPN MSB   = channel id
    BN 62 ID    NRPN LSB   = parameter id
    BN 06 VA    Data MSB   = parameter value
    BN 26 VX    Data LSB   = index / discriminator  <- NOT a fine value

VX is not a value refinement. It is either a filler constant, or a genuine
index (send destination, GEQ band, dSNAKE socket, FX tap). Which it is, is
registry data -- this module stays generic and just carries the byte through.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import time
from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass

from qumidi.midi.messages import ControlChange, MidiMessage, SystemRealtime
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def encode_nrpn(midi_channel: int, ch: int, param_id: int, value: int, vx: int) -> bytes:
    """Build the four CCs, always in the documented order, on channel N."""
    for name, val, hi in (
        ("midi_channel", midi_channel, 15),
        ("ch", ch, 0x7F),
        ("param_id", param_id, 0x7F),
        ("value", value, 0x7F),
        ("vx", vx, 0x7F),
    ):
        if not 0 <= val <= hi:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be 0..{hi}, got {val!r}")
    status = c.CONTROL_CHANGE | midi_channel
    return bytes(
        [
            status, c.CC_NRPN_MSB, ch,
            status, c.CC_NRPN_LSB, param_id,
            status, c.CC_DATA_MSB, value,
            status, c.CC_DATA_LSB, vx,
        ]
    )


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RawParamUpdate:
    """One decoded NRPN parameter write, before registry resolution."""

    midi_channel: int
    ch: int
    param_id: int
    value: int
    vx: int | None


@dataclass
class _Accumulator:
    ch: int | None = None
    param_id: int | None = None


class NrpnDecoder:
    """Reassembles NRPN quads from a message stream, per MIDI channel."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        pending_flush_timeout: float = c.PENDING_FLUSH_TIMEOUT,
        now: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,
    ) -> None:
        self.pending_flush_timeout = pending_flush_timeout
        self._now = now
        self._acc: dict[int, _Accumulator] = {}
        self._held: RawParamUpdate | None = None
        self._held_at = 0.0

    def feed(self, msg: MidiMessage) -> list[RawParamUpdate]:
        # PRD 5.3: realtime bytes must not flush or disturb the held update --
        # they arrive constantly and would break every NRPN pair.
        if isinstance(msg, SystemRealtime):
            return []
        if not isinstance(msg, ControlChange):
            # Flush trigger (b): any non-CC message. A sysex 14 end-of-sync
            # marker reaches us here (PRD 9.3.7).
            return self._release()

        acc = self._acc.setdefault(msg.channel, _Accumulator())
        if msg.controller == c.CC_NRPN_MSB:
            out = self._release()
            acc.ch = msg.value
            acc.param_id = None
            return out
        if msg.controller == c.CC_NRPN_LSB:
            out = self._release()
            acc.param_id = msg.value
            return out
        if msg.controller == c.CC_DATA_MSB:
            out = self._release()
            if acc.ch is None or acc.param_id is None:
                log.debug("Data MSB with no NRPN address on channel %d; ignoring", msg.channel)
                return out
            # PRD 5.3: emit on 06, not on 26, and hold for at most one
            # subsequent CC in case a 26 follows.
            self._held = RawParamUpdate(msg.channel, acc.ch, acc.param_id, msg.value, None)
            self._held_at = self._now()
            return out
        if msg.controller == c.CC_DATA_LSB:
            if self._held is not None and self._held.midi_channel == msg.channel:
                update = RawParamUpdate(
                    self._held.midi_channel,
                    self._held.ch,
                    self._held.param_id,
                    self._held.value,
                    msg.value,
                )
                self._held = None
                return [update]
            return []
        # Any other CC (bank select during a scene recall, for instance) is
        # not part of the NRPN sequence and must not disturb the accumulator,
        # but it does end the hold window.
        return self._release()

    def tick(self, now: float | None = None) -> list[RawParamUpdate]:
        """Flush trigger (c): pending_flush_timeout of inbound silence."""
        if self._held is None:
            return []
        current = self._now() if now is None else now
        if current - self._held_at < self.pending_flush_timeout:
            return []
        return self._release()

    def flush(self) -> list[RawParamUpdate]:
        """Force-release the held update. Called on end-of-sync and shutdown."""
        return self._release()

    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Forget all state. Called on disconnect."""
        self._acc.clear()
        self._held = None

    def _release(self) -> list[RawParamUpdate]:
        if self._held is None:
            return []
        held, self._held = self._held, None
        return [held]
```

### Task 3.2: ParamSpec, VxMode and Domain

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/params.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_params.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ChannelClass`, `errors`, `units` (Task 3.3 — write that first if you prefer; the two are independent except that `Interpolated` holds a `Taper`).
- Produces:
  - `Direction` enum: `TX_ONLY, RX_ONLY, BIDIRECTIONAL`.
  - `Tier` enum: `VERIFIED, SUPPORTED, EXPERIMENTAL`.
  - `VxMode` variants: `Fixed(value)`, `SendTarget()`, `GeqBand()`, `DsnakeSocket()`, `FxTap()`. Each has `validate(vx: int | None, param: str) -> int` returning the byte to send, and `sub_index(vx: int, value: int) -> int | None`.
  - `Domain` variants: `Boolean(off=0x00, on=0x01)`, `Raw7Bit(lo, hi)`, `Interpolated(taper, lo, hi)`, `EnumDomain(mapping)`, `FlaggedIndex(on_flag=0x40, count=4)`. Each has `validate_raw(value, param, ch_label) -> int`, `clamp_inbound(value, param, ch_label) -> int`, and `sub_index_from_value(value) -> int | None`.
  - `ParamSpec` frozen dataclass with the fields from PRD §7.1.

**Two things this task must get right, both listed in the PRD errata:**
- `Raw7Bit` carries explicit bounds and every write is range-checked. Pan stops at `0x4A`, input Delay Time at `0x40`, GEQ band index at `0x1B`, dSNAKE socket at `0x27`. Out-of-range **writes** raise `ParameterRangeError`; out-of-range **reads** from the console are clamped and logged, never rejected (PRD §7.1).
- `FlaggedIndex` is a set-valued parameter whose index lives in the **value**, not in `VX` (PRD §14.10). `sub_index_from_value(0x42)` is `2`; the decoded value is the flag bit alone.

`tests/protocol/test_params.py`:

```python
import logging

import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass
from qumidi.protocol.params import (
    Boolean,
    Direction,
    DsnakeSocket,
    EnumDomain,
    Fixed,
    FlaggedIndex,
    FxTap,
    GeqBand,
    Interpolated,
    ParamSpec,
    Raw7Bit,
    SendTarget,
    Tier,
)
from qumidi.protocol.units import FADER_TAPER


def test_fixed_vx_supplies_the_filler_byte() -> None:
    assert Fixed(0x07).validate(None, "fader") == 0x07
    assert Fixed(0x00).validate(None, "peq_in") == 0x00


def test_fixed_vx_rejects_a_caller_supplied_index() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError) as excinfo:
        Fixed(0x07).validate(0x02, "fader")
    assert "fader" in str(excinfo.value)


def test_fixed_vx_has_no_sub_index() -> None:
    # PRD 10.1a: a Fixed filler VX contributes no addressing index.
    assert Fixed(0x07).sub_index(0x07, 0x62) is None


def test_geq_band_bounds() -> None:
    assert GeqBand().validate(0x00, "geq_gain") == 0x00
    assert GeqBand().validate(0x1B, "geq_gain") == 0x1B
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        GeqBand().validate(0x1C, "geq_gain")
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        GeqBand().validate(None, "geq_gain")
    assert GeqBand().sub_index(0x0F, 0x40) == 0x0F


def test_dsnake_socket_bounds() -> None:
    assert DsnakeSocket().validate(0x27, "dsnake_gain") == 0x27
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        DsnakeSocket().validate(0x28, "dsnake_gain")


def test_fx_tap_accepts_only_the_three_documented_values() -> None:
    for vx in (0x05, 0x06, 0x07):
        assert FxTap().validate(vx, "fx_delay_coarse") == vx
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        FxTap().validate(0x04, "fx_delay_coarse")


def test_boolean_domain() -> None:
    d = Boolean()
    assert d.validate_raw(0x00, "mute", "Input 6") == 0x00
    assert d.validate_raw(0x01, "mute", "Input 6") == 0x01
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        d.validate_raw(0x02, "peq_in", "Input 6")


def test_raw7bit_bounds_are_explicit_and_enforced_on_write() -> None:
    # PRD 7.1: several parameters do not use the full 00-7F span.
    pan = Raw7Bit(lo=0x00, hi=0x4A)
    assert pan.validate_raw(0x4A, "pan", "Input 6") == 0x4A
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError) as excinfo:
        pan.validate_raw(0x7F, "pan", "Input 6")
    assert "Input 6" in str(excinfo.value)
    assert "pan" in str(excinfo.value)
    assert "0x4a" in str(excinfo.value).lower()


def test_raw7bit_clamps_rather_than_rejecting_on_receive(caplog) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 7.1: out-of-range reads from the console are clamped and logged,
    # never rejected.
    pan = Raw7Bit(lo=0x00, hi=0x4A)
    with caplog.at_level(logging.WARNING):
        assert pan.clamp_inbound(0x7F, "pan", "Input 6") == 0x4A
    assert any("clamp" in r.message.lower() for r in caplog.records)


def test_flagged_index_puts_the_index_in_the_value() -> None:
    # PRD 14.10 / 10.1a: this is the single easiest thing to get wrong.
    d = FlaggedIndex(on_flag=0x40, count=4)
    assert d.sub_index_from_value(0x00) == 0
    assert d.sub_index_from_value(0x03) == 3
    assert d.sub_index_from_value(0x40) == 0
    assert d.sub_index_from_value(0x42) == 2
    assert d.is_on(0x42) is True
    assert d.is_on(0x02) is False
    assert d.encode(index=2, on=True) == 0x42
    assert d.encode(index=2, on=False) == 0x02


def test_flagged_index_rejects_an_out_of_range_group() -> None:
    d = FlaggedIndex(on_flag=0x40, count=4)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        d.encode(index=4, on=True)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        d.validate_raw(0x44, "dca_assign", "Input 6")


def test_interpolated_domain_delegates_to_the_taper() -> None:
    # Always construct Interpolated by keyword: it inherits Raw7Bit's lo/hi,
    # so the first positional argument is `lo`, not the taper.
    d = Interpolated(lo=0x00, hi=0x7F, taper=FADER_TAPER)
    assert d.to_raw(0.0) == 0x62
    assert d.to_db(0x62) == pytest.approx(0.0, abs=0.5)


def test_enum_domain() -> None:
    d = EnumDomain({0x00: "bell", 0x06: "shelf"})
    assert d.validate_raw(0x06, "lf_type", "Input 6") == 0x06
    assert d.label(0x00) == "bell"
    assert d.from_label("shelf") == 0x06
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        d.validate_raw(0x01, "lf_type", "Input 6")


def test_param_spec_defaults() -> None:
    spec = ParamSpec(
        name="fader",
        nrpn_id=0x17,
        vx=Fixed(0x07),
        domain=Interpolated(lo=0x00, hi=0x7F, taper=FADER_TAPER),
        applies_to=frozenset({ChannelClass.INPUT}),
    )
    assert spec.direction is Direction.BIDIRECTIONAL
    assert spec.tier is Tier.SUPPORTED
    assert spec.notes == ""
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/params.py`**

```python
"""The parameter description types (PRD 7.1).

A ParamSpec describes one controllable parameter completely: its NRPN id, what
its VX byte means, what its value means, and which channel classes accept it.
Both the flat command API and the object facade are generated from these, so
protocol knowledge lives in exactly one place.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from enum import Enum, auto

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass
from qumidi.protocol.units import Taper

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class Direction(Enum):
    TX_ONLY = auto()
    #: Writing raises ReadOnlyParameterError rather than emitting bytes the
    #: mixer ignores (PRD 7.1). Group Mode 5E and dSNAKE Patch 5D.
    RX_ONLY = auto()
    BIDIRECTIONAL = auto()


class Tier(Enum):
    #: Confirmed against hardware in M6. Only the calibration run may set this,
    #: never a hand edit (PRD 7.1).
    VERIFIED = auto()
    SUPPORTED = auto()
    EXPERIMENTAL = auto()


# --- VX modes -------------------------------------------------------------


class VxMode:
    """What the Data Entry LSB byte means for one parameter."""

    def validate(self, vx: int | None, param: str) -> int:
        raise NotImplementedError

    def sub_index(self, vx: int, value: int) -> int | None:
        """The cache addressing index this parameter shape contributes."""
        raise NotImplementedError


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Fixed(VxMode):
    """A constant filler byte.

    PRD 7.1: the filler value is inconsistent across parameters and must be
    transcribed literally, not inferred. Getting it wrong produces messages
    the mixer silently ignores.
    """

    value: int

    def validate(self, vx: int | None, param: str) -> int:
        if vx is not None and vx != self.value:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{param} takes no index; its VX is the fixed filler {self.value:#04x}, "
                f"but {vx:#04x} was supplied"
            )
        return self.value

    def sub_index(self, vx: int, value: int) -> int | None:
        return None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _RangedVx(VxMode):
    lo: int
    hi: int
    what: str

    def validate(self, vx: int | None, param: str) -> int:
        if vx is None:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"{param} requires a {self.what} index in VX")
        if not self.lo <= vx <= self.hi:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{param}: {self.what} index must be {self.lo:#04x}..{self.hi:#04x}, "
                f"got {vx:#04x}"
            )
        return vx

    def sub_index(self, vx: int, value: int) -> int | None:
        return vx


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GeqBand(_RangedVx):
    """28 GEQ bands, index 0x00-0x1B (PRD 7.2)."""

    lo: int = 0x00
    hi: int = 0x1B
    what: str = "GEQ band"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DsnakeSocket(_RangedVx):
    """dSNAKE socket 0x00-0x27 (PRD 7.2)."""

    lo: int = 0x00
    hi: int = 0x27
    what: str = "dSNAKE socket"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SendTarget(VxMode):
    """A send destination.

    The valid bytes are *not* a dense range (PRD 7.2: note the gap at 0E-0F),
    so VX must come from SEND_TARGET_VX by lookup, never from arithmetic on a
    mix index. Validation against that table lives in the registry, which owns
    it; this class only enforces presence and 7-bit range.
    """

    def validate(self, vx: int | None, param: str) -> int:
        if vx is None:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"{param} requires a send target in VX")
        if not 0x00 <= vx <= 0x7F:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"{param}: send target VX must be 7-bit, got {vx:#04x}")
        return vx

    def sub_index(self, vx: int, value: int) -> int | None:
        return vx


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FxTap(VxMode):
    """FX delay tap: 0x05 left, 0x07 right, 0x06 link (PRD 7.2)."""

    LEFT: int = 0x05
    LINK: int = 0x06
    RIGHT: int = 0x07

    def validate(self, vx: int | None, param: str) -> int:
        if vx not in (self.LEFT, self.LINK, self.RIGHT):
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{param}: FX tap VX must be 0x05 (left), 0x06 (link) or 0x07 (right), "
                f"got {vx!r}"
            )
        assert vx is not None
        return vx

    def sub_index(self, vx: int, value: int) -> int | None:
        return vx


# --- Domains --------------------------------------------------------------


class Domain:
    """What a parameter's 7-bit value means."""

    def validate_raw(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        raise NotImplementedError

    def clamp_inbound(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        """Coerce a value received from the console. Never raises (PRD 7.1)."""
        return value

    def sub_index_from_value(self, value: int) -> int | None:
        return None


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Boolean(Domain):
    off: int = 0x00
    on: int = 0x01

    def validate_raw(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if value not in (self.off, self.on):
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{ch_label} {param}: value must be {self.off:#04x} or {self.on:#04x}, "
                f"got {value:#04x}"
            )
        return value

    def clamp_inbound(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if value not in (self.off, self.on):
            log.warning(
                "%s %s: received %#04x, expected %#04x or %#04x; treating non-zero as on",
                ch_label, param, value, self.off, self.on,
            )
            return self.on if value else self.off
        return value


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Raw7Bit(Domain):
    """A bounded 7-bit value. Bounds are explicit, never assumed to be 0-0x7F."""

    lo: int = 0x00
    hi: int = 0x7F

    def validate_raw(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if not self.lo <= value <= self.hi:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{ch_label} {param}: raw value must be {self.lo:#04x}..{self.hi:#04x}, "
                f"got {value:#04x}"
            )
        return value

    def clamp_inbound(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if value < self.lo or value > self.hi:
            clamped = min(max(value, self.lo), self.hi)
            log.warning(
                "%s %s: console reported %#04x outside %#04x..%#04x; clamping to %#04x",
                ch_label, param, value, self.lo, self.hi, clamped,
            )
            return clamped
        return value


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Interpolated(Raw7Bit):
    """A bounded raw value with a dB/Hz/ms taper attached (PRD 8)."""

    taper: Taper = field(default_factory=lambda: Taper(points=((0, 0.0), (127, 1.0))))

    def to_db(self, raw: int) -> float:
        return self.taper.to_db(raw)

    def to_raw(self, db: float) -> int:
        return self.taper.to_raw(db)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EnumDomain(Domain):
    #: Stored as a tuple of pairs so the spec stays hashable and frozen.
    mapping: tuple[tuple[int, str], ...]

    # dataclasses' _set_new_attribute does not overwrite a name already in the
    # class dict, so this hand-written __init__ wins over the generated one.
    # That is what lets callers pass a plain dict while the field stays a tuple.
    def __init__(self, mapping: dict[int, str]) -> None:
        object.__setattr__(self, "mapping", tuple(mapping.items()))

    @property
    def as_dict(self) -> dict[int, str]:
        return dict(self.mapping)

    def validate_raw(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if value not in self.as_dict:
            valid = ", ".join(f"{k:#04x} ({v})" for k, v in self.mapping)
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{ch_label} {param}: {value:#04x} is not a valid value; valid: {valid}"
            )
        return value

    def clamp_inbound(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        if value not in self.as_dict:
            log.warning("%s %s: console reported undocumented value %#04x", ch_label, param, value)
        return value

    def label(self, value: int) -> str | None:
        return self.as_dict.get(value)

    def from_label(self, label: str) -> int:
        for raw, name in self.mapping:
            if name == label:
                return raw
        valid = ", ".join(name for _, name in self.mapping)
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"unknown value {label!r}; valid: {valid}")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FlaggedIndex(Domain):
    """A set-valued parameter: "channel X is a member of group i".

    PRD 14.10: the group index lives in the *value*, not in VX. One NRPN
    message carries one bit of a four-bit set, so the cache must key on the
    index extracted from the value -- see PRD 10.1a.
    """

    on_flag: int = 0x40
    count: int = 4

    def encode(self, index: int, on: bool) -> int:
        if not 0 <= index < self.count:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"group index must be 0..{self.count - 1}, got {index}")
        return (self.on_flag | index) if on else index

    def is_on(self, value: int) -> bool:
        return bool(value & self.on_flag)

    def sub_index_from_value(self, value: int) -> int | None:
        return value & (self.on_flag - 1)

    def validate_raw(self, value: int, param: str, ch_label: str) -> int:
        index = value & (self.on_flag - 1)
        if value & ~(self.on_flag | (self.on_flag - 1)) or index >= self.count:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{ch_label} {param}: {value:#04x} is not a valid membership value; "
                f"expected 0x00-{self.count - 1:#04x} (off) or "
                f"{self.on_flag:#04x}-{self.on_flag + self.count - 1:#04x} (on)"
            )
        return value


# --- ParamSpec ------------------------------------------------------------


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ParamSpec:
    """One row of the parameter registry (PRD 7.1)."""

    name: str
    nrpn_id: int
    vx: VxMode
    domain: Domain
    applies_to: frozenset[ChannelClass]
    direction: Direction = Direction.BIDIRECTIONAL
    tier: Tier = Tier.SUPPORTED
    notes: str = ""
```

### Task 3.3: Unit conversion and the fader taper

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/units.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_units.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces:
  - `Taper` frozen dataclass: `points: tuple[tuple[int, float], ...]` ascending by raw, `neg_inf_at_zero: bool = False`, `extrapolate_floor: float | None = None`, `unit: str = "dB"`. Methods `to_db(raw) -> float`, `to_raw(db) -> int`, `is_approximate(raw) -> bool`.
  - `FADER_TAPER` (also used for send level).
  - `linear_taper(lo, hi, *, centre_raw=None, centre=0.0, hi_raw=0x7F)` helper.
  - Later tasks add `LOCAL_GAIN_TAPER`, `DSNAKE_GAIN_TAPER`, `TRIM_TAPER`, `PEQ_GAIN_TAPER`, `GEQ_GAIN_TAPER`, delay tapers, and the unverified frequency/time curves (Task 5.1).

**The contract (PRD §8):** the raw 0–127 value is always the ground truth. `.raw` is exact and lossless. `.db` is a convenience property, documented as approximate (~±0.5 dB, limited by 128 steps). Round-tripping `db → raw → db` is not guaranteed exact and tests assert tolerance, never equality.

**The deliberate asymmetry (PRD §8.1).** Raw `0x00` is a discrete −∞, not a point on the curve. Raw `0x01`–`0x0B` are undefined in the spec but the console *sends* them — the physical fader passes through them on its way to the bottom. So:
- `.db` for `0x01`–`0x0B` returns a linear extrapolation of the `0x0C`→`0x10` segment, floored at −60 dB, flagged approximate. Never `-inf` (that would make raw `0x0B` and raw `0x00` indistinguishable) and never an exception.
- `db_to_raw()` for anything below −45 dB returns `0x00`.
- Therefore `raw → db → raw` is **not** an identity for `0x01`–`0x0B`. Tests assert the asymmetry, not a round trip that cannot hold.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_units.py`:

```python
import math

import pytest

from qumidi.protocol.units import FADER_TAPER, Taper, linear_taper

# PRD 8.1, transcribed from the spec table.
FADER_ANCHORS = [
    (10.0, 0x7F), (5.0, 0x72), (0.0, 0x62), (-5.0, 0x4F), (-10.0, 0x3F),
    (-15.0, 0x36), (-20.0, 0x2F), (-25.0, 0x27), (-30.0, 0x1F), (-35.0, 0x17),
    (-40.0, 0x10), (-45.0, 0x0C),
]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("db,raw", FADER_ANCHORS)
def test_every_anchor_point_is_exact_in_both_directions(db, raw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw) == pytest.approx(db, abs=0.01)
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(db) == raw


def test_raw_zero_is_a_discrete_minus_infinity() -> None:
    # PRD 8.1: 00 is a discrete -inf, not a point on the curve. Do not
    # interpolate between 00 and 0C.
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_db(0x00) == -math.inf


def test_anything_below_minus_45_db_maps_to_raw_zero() -> None:
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(-46.0) == 0x00
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(-100.0) == 0x00
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(-math.inf) == 0x00


@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", list(range(0x01, 0x0C)))
def test_the_undefined_band_extrapolates_rather_than_returning_minus_inf(raw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 8.1: the console sends these -- the physical fader passes through
    # them. Do not return -inf (raw 0B and raw 00 must stay distinguishable).
    db = FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw)
    assert math.isfinite(db)
    assert -60.0 <= db < -45.0
    assert FADER_TAPER.is_approximate(raw) is True


def test_the_extrapolation_follows_the_0c_to_10_slope() -> None:
    # 0x0C = -45 dB, 0x10 = -40 dB -> 1.25 dB per raw step.
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_db(0x0B) == pytest.approx(-46.25, abs=0.01)
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_db(0x01) == pytest.approx(-58.75, abs=0.01)


def test_the_extrapolation_is_floored_at_minus_60() -> None:
    steep = Taper(points=((0x0C, -45.0), (0x10, -20.0)), neg_inf_at_zero=True,
                  extrapolate_floor=-60.0)
    assert steep.to_db(0x01) == -60.0


@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", list(range(0x01, 0x0C)))
def test_raw_to_db_to_raw_is_deliberately_not_an_identity_in_the_band(raw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 8.1: the inverse is not surjective onto this band, and this
    # asymmetry must be asserted rather than a round trip that cannot hold.
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw)) == 0x00


@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", list(range(0x0C, 0x80)))
def test_raw_to_db_to_raw_round_trips_above_the_undefined_band(raw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw)) == pytest.approx(raw, abs=1)


def test_the_taper_is_monotonic_across_the_whole_range() -> None:
    values = [FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw) for raw in range(0x01, 0x80)]
    assert values == sorted(values)


def test_db_round_trips_within_half_a_db() -> None:
    # PRD 8: .db is documented as approximate, ~+-0.5 dB.
    for db in [x / 2 for x in range(-90, 21)]:
        raw = FADER_TAPER.to_raw(db)
        if raw == 0x00:
            continue
        assert FADER_TAPER.to_db(raw) == pytest.approx(db, abs=0.6)


def test_to_raw_clamps_above_the_top_anchor() -> None:
    assert FADER_TAPER.to_raw(99.0) == 0x7F


def test_linear_taper_with_an_asymmetric_centre() -> None:
    # PRD 8.4: 00-7F is 128 steps around a centre of 40, so the span is
    # asymmetric -- 64 steps below zero and 63 above. Anchor on 40 = 0 dB and
    # do not assume +24 dB lands exactly on 7F by symmetry.
    trim = linear_taper(-24.0, 24.0, centre_raw=0x40)
    assert trim.to_db(0x40) == pytest.approx(0.0)
    assert trim.to_db(0x00) == pytest.approx(-24.0)
    assert trim.to_db(0x7F) == pytest.approx(24.0)
    assert trim.to_db(0x20) == pytest.approx(-12.0, abs=0.01)
    # Below centre: 24/64 dB per step. Above: 24/63.
    assert abs(trim.to_db(0x41) - 0.0) == pytest.approx(24.0 / 63, abs=0.001)
    assert abs(trim.to_db(0x3F) - 0.0) == pytest.approx(24.0 / 64, abs=0.001)


def test_linear_taper_without_a_centre() -> None:
    delay_input = linear_taper(0.0, 85.0, hi_raw=0x40)
    assert delay_input.to_db(0x00) == pytest.approx(0.0)
    assert delay_input.to_db(0x40) == pytest.approx(85.0)
    assert delay_input.to_raw(42.5) == 0x20


def test_taper_rejects_unsorted_points() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        Taper(points=((0x10, 0.0), (0x00, 1.0)))
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/units.py`**

```python
"""Unit conversion (PRD 8).

Contract: the raw 0-127 value is always the ground truth. ``.raw`` is exact
and lossless; ``.db`` is a convenience, documented as approximate (~+-0.5 dB,
limited by 128 steps across the range). Round-tripping db -> raw -> db is not
guaranteed exact; tests assert tolerance, not equality.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Taper:
    """Monotonic piecewise-linear interpolation over raw MIDI values.

    ``points`` are ``(raw, value)`` pairs, ascending by raw. The spec gives
    only sparse anchor points; everything between them is interpolated.
    """

    points: tuple[tuple[int, float], ...]
    #: Raw 0x00 means -inf rather than a point on the curve (fader, send level).
    neg_inf_at_zero: bool = False
    #: Values below the lowest anchor extrapolate its first segment, floored here.
    extrapolate_floor: float | None = None
    unit: str = "dB"

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        raws = [raw for raw, _ in self.points]
        if raws != sorted(raws) or len(set(raws)) != len(raws):
            raise ValueError(f"taper points must be strictly ascending by raw, got {raws}")
        if len(self.points) < 2:
            raise ValueError("a taper needs at least two anchor points")

    # -- raw -> value -----------------------------------------------------

    def to_db(self, raw: int) -> float:
        """Convert a raw value to engineering units. Never raises."""
        if self.neg_inf_at_zero and raw == 0:
            return -math.inf

        lowest_raw, lowest_val = self.points[0]
        if raw < lowest_raw:
            return self._extrapolate_low(raw)
        highest_raw, highest_val = self.points[-1]
        if raw >= highest_raw:
            return highest_val

        for (r0, v0), (r1, v1) in zip(self.points, self.points[1:]):
            if r0 <= raw <= r1:
                if r1 == r0:
                    return v0
                return v0 + (v1 - v0) * (raw - r0) / (r1 - r0)
        return lowest_val  # pragma: no cover - unreachable given the guards

    def _extrapolate_low(self, raw: int) -> float:
        """PRD 8.1: raw 01-0B are transmitted by the console but undefined.

        Extrapolate the first segment rather than returning -inf, which would
        make raw 0x0B and raw 0x00 indistinguishable, and never raise.
        """
        (r0, v0), (r1, v1) = self.points[0], self.points[1]
        slope = (v1 - v0) / (r1 - r0)
        value = v0 + slope * (raw - r0)
        if self.extrapolate_floor is not None:
            value = max(value, self.extrapolate_floor)
        return value

    def is_approximate(self, raw: int) -> bool:
        """True where ``to_db`` extrapolated beyond the documented anchors."""
        if self.neg_inf_at_zero and raw == 0:
            return False
        return raw < self.points[0][0]

    # -- value -> raw -----------------------------------------------------

    def to_raw(self, value: float) -> int:
        """Convert engineering units to the nearest raw value.

        PRD 8.1: this direction is deliberately not surjective onto an
        extrapolated band. Anything below the lowest anchor returns the
        bottom raw value, so raw -> db -> raw is *not* an identity there.
        """
        lowest_raw, lowest_val = self.points[0]
        highest_raw, highest_val = self.points[-1]

        if math.isinf(value) and value < 0:
            return 0 if self.neg_inf_at_zero else lowest_raw
        if value <= lowest_val:
            return 0 if self.neg_inf_at_zero else lowest_raw
        if value >= highest_val:
            return highest_raw

        for (r0, v0), (r1, v1) in zip(self.points, self.points[1:]):
            if v0 <= value <= v1:
                if v1 == v0:
                    return r0
                return round(r0 + (r1 - r0) * (value - v0) / (v1 - v0))
        return highest_raw  # pragma: no cover


def linear_taper(
    lo: float,
    hi: float,
    *,
    centre_raw: int | None = None,
    centre: float = 0.0,
    lo_raw: int = 0x00,
    hi_raw: int = 0x7F,
    unit: str = "dB",
) -> Taper:
    """Build a linear taper, optionally anchored on an asymmetric centre.

    PRD 8.4: 00-7F is 128 steps around a centre of 0x40, so the span is
    asymmetric -- 64 steps below zero and 63 above. Anchoring on the centre
    keeps 0 dB exactly on 0x40 rather than assuming symmetry lands +24 dB on
    0x7F.
    """
    if centre_raw is None:
        return Taper(points=((lo_raw, lo), (hi_raw, hi)), unit=unit)
    return Taper(points=((lo_raw, lo), (centre_raw, centre), (hi_raw, hi)), unit=unit)


#: PRD 8.1. Shared by Fader (17) and Send Level (20) -- the same taper, and
#: the same treatment of the undefined 01-0B band.
FADER_TAPER = Taper(
    points=(
        (0x0C, -45.0),
        (0x10, -40.0),
        (0x17, -35.0),
        (0x1F, -30.0),
        (0x27, -25.0),
        (0x2F, -20.0),
        (0x36, -15.0),
        (0x3F, -10.0),
        (0x4F, -5.0),
        (0x62, 0.0),
        (0x72, 5.0),
        (0x7F, 10.0),
    ),
    neg_inf_at_zero=True,
    extrapolate_floor=-60.0,
)
```

### Task 3.4: The registry and the send-target table

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/registry.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_registry.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `params`, `channels`, `units`.
- Produces:
  - `SEND_TARGET_VX: dict[ChannelId, int]` and `VX_TO_SEND_TARGET: dict[int, ChannelId]`.
  - `send_target_vx(target: ChannelId, caps: ModelCaps | None) -> int` — validates against the table *and* against `ModelCaps`.
  - `Registry` with `lookup(name, cls=None) -> ParamSpec`, `resolve(cls, param_id, vx) -> ParamSpec | None`, `params_for(cls) -> list[ParamSpec]`, `__iter__`, `__len__`.
  - `REGISTRY: Registry` — this task seeds it with the level/pan/routing family only. Slice 5 fills in the rest.

**The three-step inbound resolution order (PRD §5.3), which must stop at the first hit:**
1. Exact `(class, id, vx)` — needed for the FX-delay `48` collision (PRD §14.5).
2. `(class, id)` — the normal case.
3. `(id,)` across all classes, with a debug log noting the class was unexpected.

Keying on `id` alone is insufficient: the same id means different things on different channel classes (GEQ `70` exists only on mixes/LR, Local Gain `19` only on inputs). And if a step-2 match has `VxMode = Fixed(k)` but the observed `VX != k`, decode it anyway **and log at warning** — that is the signature of a transcription error in the registry, and is exactly what the golden vectors exist to catch.

**Send targets are not a dense range (PRD §7.2).** Note the gap at `0x0E`–`0x0F`. `VX` for a send comes from the table by lookup, never from arithmetic on a mix index.
