- [ ] **Step 5: HAND TEST — Slice 4 acceptance**

**Against a real console** (this is the slice that most needs hardware):

Terminal 1:
```bash
qumidi monitor --host <mixer-ip>
```

Now, at the console:
1. Move fader 6. Expect a `SURFACE  Input 6  fader  0x?? -> 0x??` line per motion step.
2. Press MUTE on channel 6. (Mute arrives as a Note, not NRPN — it will not appear yet. That is expected; Slice 5 does not add it either, because mute is not a registry parameter. Note this in the terminal and move on.)
3. Press PAFL on channel 6. Expect a `pafl` line.
4. Select Mix 3 and move channel 6's fader. Expect a `send_level[0x02]` line — confirming that the console reports sends with the send target in `VX`.

Terminal 2, while the monitor is still running:
```bash
qumidi set --host <mixer-ip> fader input:7 -6db
```
Expect the monitor to print a `local` line and **no** `SURFACE` line. That asymmetry is acceptance criterion 3 and the reason echo suppression exists.

Then:
```bash
qumidi dump-state --host <mixer-ip> | head -40
```
Expect a list of everything the console pushed at sync. Note how many values arrive — this is the real measure of how complete the state push is, and is worth recording for the M6 notes.

# Slice 5 — Every parameter works

**Slice goal:** every parameter in PRD §7.2 encodes correctly, is covered by a hand-transcribed golden vector, and can be driven from the CLI. Hand test: set a PEQ gain, a gate threshold and a GEQ band, and watch the console's screen follow.

**Work discipline for this whole slice.** Each task transcribes one family of the PRD §7.2 tables into `SPECS` and adds its golden vectors. Transcribe the `VX` column character by character; do not pattern-match from the previous family. The `VX` filler alternates between `0x07` and `0x00` in a way that looks arbitrary because it *is* arbitrary — every "In/Out" enable parameter uses `0x00` while its siblings use `0x07`, and that is the single most common transcription slip.

---

### Task 5.1: The remaining taper tables

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/units.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_units.py` (extend)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `LOCAL_GAIN_TAPER`, `DSNAKE_GAIN_TAPER`, `TRIM_TAPER`, `PEQ_GAIN_TAPER`, `GEQ_GAIN_TAPER`, `INPUT_DELAY_TAPER`, `MIX_DELAY_TAPER`, and the unverified `log_taper(lo, hi, unit)` helper plus `FREQ_TAPER`, `WIDTH_TAPER`, `GATE_ATTACK_TAPER`, `GATE_RELEASE_TAPER`, `GATE_HOLD_TAPER`, `GATE_THRESHOLD_TAPER`, `GATE_DEPTH_TAPER`, `COMP_ATTACK_TAPER`, `COMP_RELEASE_TAPER`, `COMP_RATIO_TAPER`, `COMP_THRESHOLD_TAPER`, `MAKEUP_TAPER`, `HPF_TAPER`.

**Which of these are trustworthy.** §8.1–8.3 give real measured anchor tables and are accurate. §8.4's linear ones (trim, PEQ/GEQ gain, delay) are stated as linear and are accurate. **Everything else — frequencies, widths, gate and compressor times — has only endpoints in the spec.** Those get a best-effort log interpolation and a docstring saying plainly that the curve is unverified, and they are the prime targets for `tools/calibrate.py` in Slice 12.

- [ ] **Step 3: Add a `verified` flag to `Taper` and append the tables**

Add the field to the dataclass: `verified: bool = False`, and set `verified=True` on `FADER_TAPER`.

Append to `qumidi/protocol/units.py`:

```python
def log_taper(lo: float, hi: float, *, lo_raw: int = 0x00, hi_raw: int = 0x7F,
              unit: str = "Hz", points: int = 17) -> Taper:
    """A logarithmic curve between two endpoints. UNVERIFIED.

    PRD 8.4: the spec gives endpoints only for frequencies, widths and the
    gate/compressor times. This is a best-effort interpolation, not a measured
    curve. Expose ``.raw`` as primary and treat these values as indicative
    until tools/calibrate.py has swept the parameter on real hardware.
    """
    span = hi_raw - lo_raw
    anchors = []
    for i in range(points):
        raw = lo_raw + round(span * i / (points - 1))
        frac = i / (points - 1)
        anchors.append((raw, lo * (hi / lo) ** frac))
    deduped = tuple(sorted({raw: value for raw, value in anchors}.items()))
    return Taper(points=deduped, unit=unit, verified=False)


#: PRD 8.2. Local preamp gain, ID 19. Measured anchors from the spec.
LOCAL_GAIN_TAPER = Taper(
    points=((0x00, -5.0), (0x0A, 0.0), (0x13, 5.0), (0x1D, 10.0), (0x30, 20.0),
            (0x44, 30.0), (0x57, 40.0), (0x6B, 50.0), (0x7F, 60.0)),
    verified=True,
)

#: PRD 8.3. dSNAKE preamp gain, ID 58. A DIFFERENT curve -- do not share the
#: table with the local preamp above.
DSNAKE_GAIN_TAPER = Taper(
    points=((0x00, 5.0), (0x0B, 10.0), (0x22, 20.0), (0x2E, 25.0), (0x39, 30.0),
            (0x45, 35.0), (0x50, 40.0), (0x67, 50.0), (0x7F, 60.0)),
    verified=True,
)

#: PRD 8.4. Digital/stereo trim (52, 54): linear, -24..+24 dB, 0 dB = 0x40.
TRIM_TAPER = linear_taper(-24.0, 24.0, centre_raw=0x40)

#: PRD 8.4. PEQ and GEQ gain: linear, -12..+12 dB, 0 dB = 0x40.
PEQ_GAIN_TAPER = linear_taper(-12.0, 12.0, centre_raw=0x40)
GEQ_GAIN_TAPER = linear_taper(-12.0, 12.0, centre_raw=0x40)

#: PRD 7.2. Input channels: 0-85 ms across 0x00-0x40. Half-scale.
INPUT_DELAY_TAPER = linear_taper(0.0, 85.0, hi_raw=0x40, unit="ms")
#: PRD 7.2. Mix/group/matrix: 0-170 ms across 0x00-0x7F.
MIX_DELAY_TAPER = linear_taper(0.0, 170.0, unit="ms")

# --- Unverified curves (PRD 8.4) -----------------------------------------
# The spec gives endpoints only. These are best-effort and are the main thing
# hardware calibration buys -- see tools/calibrate.py.

FREQ_TAPER = log_taper(20.0, 20000.0, unit="Hz")
HPF_TAPER = log_taper(20.0, 20000.0, unit="Hz")
#: 1.5 octaves down to 1/9 octave. Descending in value, so the anchors invert.
WIDTH_TAPER = Taper(
    points=tuple(
        (raw, 1.5 * ((1 / 9) / 1.5) ** (raw / 127.0)) for raw in range(0, 128, 8)
    ) + ((127, 1 / 9),),
    unit="oct",
    verified=False,
)

GATE_ATTACK_TAPER = log_taper(0.05, 300.0, unit="ms")      # 50 us .. 300 ms
GATE_RELEASE_TAPER = log_taper(10.0, 1000.0, unit="ms")    # 10 ms .. 1 s
GATE_HOLD_TAPER = log_taper(10.0, 5000.0, unit="ms")       # 10 ms .. 5 s
GATE_THRESHOLD_TAPER = linear_taper(-72.0, 18.0)           # -72 .. +18 dB
GATE_DEPTH_TAPER = linear_taper(0.0, 60.0)                 # 0 .. 60 dB

COMP_ATTACK_TAPER = log_taper(0.3, 300.0, unit="ms")       # 300 us .. 300 ms
COMP_RELEASE_TAPER = log_taper(100.0, 2000.0, unit="ms")   # 100 ms .. 2 s
COMP_THRESHOLD_TAPER = linear_taper(-46.0, 18.0)           # -46 .. +18 dB
MAKEUP_TAPER = linear_taper(0.0, 18.0)                     # 0 .. +18 dB

#: PRD 7.2 gives 1:1 at the bottom, infinity at the top, and one interior
#: anchor: 2.6:1 = 0x50. Anchored on that point and extended log-wise.
COMP_RATIO_TAPER = Taper(
    points=((0x00, 1.0), (0x50, 2.6), (0x7E, 20.0), (0x7F, 1000.0)),
    unit=":1",
    verified=False,
)
```

Set `FREQ_TAPER.__doc__` is not possible on an instance; instead the test above checks `log_taper.__doc__`, which carries the "unverified" wording, and `taper.verified`.

### Task 5.2: Registry — input source and preamp

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/registry.py`
- Modify: `tests/fixtures/golden_params.yaml`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_registry_preamp.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces registry entries: `usb_source` (`12`/`00`), `preamp_source` (`57`/`00`), `dsnake_patch` (`5D`/`00`, RX-only), `local_gain` (`19`/`07`), `local_48v` (`69`/`07`), `dsnake_gain` (`58`/socket), `dsnake_pad` (`59`/socket), `dsnake_48v` (`5A`/socket), `digital_trim` (`52`/`07`), `stereo_trim` (`54`/`07`), `polarity` (`6A`/`07`), `insert` (`6B`/`07`).

**Three things this table gets wrong if you pattern-match (PRD §7.2):**
- `usb_source`, `preamp_source` and `dsnake_patch` use `VX = 0x00`, while every other preamp parameter uses `0x07`.
- **Local preamp has no pad parameter.** Only dSNAKE does. Do not invent one.
- **The dSNAKE family (`58`, `59`, `5A`) is socket-addressed in `VX`, which makes `CH` ambiguous.** Two readings are possible and the distinction decides whether the cache key is per-channel or per-socket. Until M6 resolves it: encode `CH` as the input channel, key the cache on `(socket,)` under the synthetic `DSNAKE` channel, and mark the family `EXPERIMENTAL`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_registry_preamp.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ReadOnlyParameterError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass
from qumidi.protocol.params import Direction, DsnakeSocket, Fixed, Tier
from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY
from qumidi.protocol.units import DSNAKE_GAIN_TAPER, LOCAL_GAIN_TAPER, TRIM_TAPER


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "name,nrpn_id,vx_filler",
    [
        ("usb_source", 0x12, 0x00),
        ("preamp_source", 0x57, 0x00),
        ("dsnake_patch", 0x5D, 0x00),
        ("local_gain", 0x19, 0x07),
        ("local_48v", 0x69, 0x07),
        ("digital_trim", 0x52, 0x07),
        ("stereo_trim", 0x54, 0x07),
        ("polarity", 0x6A, 0x07),
        ("insert", 0x6B, 0x07),
    ],
)
def test_ids_and_fillers(name, nrpn_id, vx_filler) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert spec.nrpn_id == nrpn_id
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, Fixed)
    assert spec.vx.value == vx_filler


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name,nrpn_id", [("dsnake_gain", 0x58), ("dsnake_pad", 0x59),
                                          ("dsnake_48v", 0x5A)])
def test_the_dsnake_family_is_socket_addressed_and_experimental(name, nrpn_id) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 7.2: CH is ambiguous until M6 resolves it.
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert spec.nrpn_id == nrpn_id
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, DsnakeSocket)
    assert spec.tier is Tier.EXPERIMENTAL


def test_dsnake_patch_is_rx_only() -> None:
    # PRD 7.2: it is the map from channel to socket, and the only way to know
    # which socket a channel's remote preamp lives on.
    assert REGISTRY.lookup("dsnake_patch").direction is Direction.RX_ONLY


def test_group_mode_is_rx_only_and_global() -> None:
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup("group_mode")
    assert spec.nrpn_id == 0x5E
    assert spec.direction is Direction.RX_ONLY
    assert ChannelClass.GLOBAL in spec.applies_to


def test_there_is_no_local_pad_parameter() -> None:
    # PRD 7.2: local preamp has no pad parameter; only dSNAKE does. Do not
    # invent one.
    from qumidi.errors import UnknownParameterError

    with pytest.raises(UnknownParameterError):
        REGISTRY.lookup("local_pad")


def test_the_two_gain_parameters_use_different_tapers() -> None:
    assert REGISTRY.lookup("local_gain").domain.taper is LOCAL_GAIN_TAPER  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert REGISTRY.lookup("dsnake_gain").domain.taper is DSNAKE_GAIN_TAPER  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


def test_trims_share_the_trim_taper_and_apply_to_the_right_classes() -> None:
    assert REGISTRY.lookup("digital_trim").domain.taper is TRIM_TAPER  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    stereo = REGISTRY.lookup("stereo_trim")
    assert stereo.applies_to == frozenset({ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT})


def test_preamp_parameters_do_not_apply_to_mixes() -> None:
    assert ChannelClass.MIX_MONO not in REGISTRY.lookup("local_gain").applies_to
    assert ChannelClass.LR not in REGISTRY.lookup("local_48v").applies_to
```

### Task 5.3: Registry — PEQ, HPF and GEQ

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/registry.py`
- Modify: `tests/fixtures/golden_params.yaml`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_registry_eq.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `peq.lf.gain/freq/width/type`, `peq.lm.gain/freq/width`, `peq.hm.gain/freq/width`, `peq.hf.gain/freq/width/type`, `peq_in`, `hpf_freq`, `hpf_in`, `geq_gain`, `geq_in`. Plus `GEQ_BANDS: tuple[float, ...]` (the 28 centre frequencies) and `geq_band_for_freq(hz: float) -> int`.

**The two traps here (PRD §7.2):**
- IDs `08` and `0C` are **unassigned**. The band blocks are 4 wide but the fourth slot is used only by LF and HF, for Type. Do not fill the gaps.
- `peq_in` (`11`), `hpf_in` (`14`) and `geq_in` (`71`) use `VX = 0x00`; every other EQ parameter uses `0x07`.

**GEQ band indices are 0-based throughout** to match the wire encoding, and 1 kHz is band **15**, not 14 — band 14 is 800 Hz (PRD §11.1, correcting v1.0).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_registry_eq.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError, UnknownParameterError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass
from qumidi.protocol.params import Fixed, GeqBand
from qumidi.protocol.registry import GEQ_BANDS, REGISTRY, geq_band_for_freq

PEQ_IDS = [
    ("peq.lf.gain", 0x01, 0x07), ("peq.lf.freq", 0x02, 0x07),
    ("peq.lf.width", 0x03, 0x07), ("peq.lf.type", 0x04, 0x07),
    ("peq.lm.gain", 0x05, 0x07), ("peq.lm.freq", 0x06, 0x07),
    ("peq.lm.width", 0x07, 0x07),
    ("peq.hm.gain", 0x09, 0x07), ("peq.hm.freq", 0x0A, 0x07),
    ("peq.hm.width", 0x0B, 0x07),
    ("peq.hf.gain", 0x0D, 0x07), ("peq.hf.freq", 0x0E, 0x07),
    ("peq.hf.width", 0x0F, 0x07), ("peq.hf.type", 0x10, 0x07),
    ("peq_in", 0x11, 0x00),
    ("hpf_freq", 0x13, 0x07), ("hpf_in", 0x14, 0x00),
    ("geq_in", 0x71, 0x00),
]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name,nrpn_id,filler", PEQ_IDS)
def test_eq_ids_and_fillers(name, nrpn_id, filler) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert spec.nrpn_id == nrpn_id
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, Fixed)
    assert spec.vx.value == filler


def test_ids_08_and_0c_are_unassigned() -> None:
    # PRD 7.2: the band blocks are 4 wide with the fourth slot used only by
    # LF/HF for Type. Do not fill the gaps.
    used = {spec.nrpn_id for spec in REGISTRY}
    assert 0x08 not in used
    assert 0x0C not in used


def test_only_lf_and_hf_have_a_type() -> None:
    REGISTRY.lookup("peq.lf.type")
    REGISTRY.lookup("peq.hf.type")
    for missing in ("peq.lm.type", "peq.hm.type"):
        with pytest.raises(UnknownParameterError):
            REGISTRY.lookup(missing)


def test_peq_type_is_an_enum_of_bell_and_shelf() -> None:
    domain = REGISTRY.lookup("peq.lf.type").domain
    assert domain.label(0x00) == "bell"  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert domain.label(0x06) == "shelf"  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        domain.validate_raw(0x01, "peq.lf.type", "Input 6")


def test_geq_gain_is_band_addressed() -> None:
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup("geq_gain")
    assert spec.nrpn_id == 0x70
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, GeqBand)


def test_geq_applies_only_to_mixes_and_lr() -> None:
    # PRD 5.3: GEQ 70 exists only on mixes/LR -- this is exactly why keying on
    # the parameter id alone is insufficient.
    applies = REGISTRY.lookup("geq_gain").applies_to
    assert ChannelClass.LR in applies
    assert ChannelClass.MIX_MONO in applies
    assert ChannelClass.INPUT not in applies


def test_there_are_28_geq_bands_starting_at_31_5_hz() -> None:
    assert len(GEQ_BANDS) == 28
    assert GEQ_BANDS[0] == 31.5
    assert GEQ_BANDS[-1] == 16000.0


def test_one_kilohertz_is_band_fifteen_not_fourteen() -> None:
    # PRD 11.1: the v1.0 example was wrong. Band 14 is 800 Hz.
    assert GEQ_BANDS[14] == 800.0
    assert GEQ_BANDS[15] == 1000.0
    assert geq_band_for_freq(1000) == 15


def test_geq_band_lookup_snaps_to_the_nearest_band() -> None:
    assert geq_band_for_freq(31.5) == 0
    assert geq_band_for_freq(16000) == 27
    assert geq_band_for_freq(1050) == 15
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        geq_band_for_freq(50000)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Append the rows and the band table**

```python
#: PRD 7.2: 28 bands, index 0x00-0x1B. 0-based throughout, matching the wire
#: encoding. Band 14 is 800 Hz and band 15 is 1 kHz (PRD 11.1).
GEQ_BANDS: tuple[float, ...] = (
    31.5, 40, 50, 63, 80, 100, 125, 160, 200, 250, 315, 400, 500, 630, 800,
    1000, 1250, 1600, 2000, 2500, 3150, 4000, 5000, 6300, 8000, 10000, 12500, 16000,
)


def geq_band_for_freq(hz: float) -> int:
    """Resolve a frequency to its 0-based GEQ band index.

    Most callers want this rather than the raw index -- see
    geq.set_band_db(freq=1000) in the object facade.
    """
    if not GEQ_BANDS[0] / 1.5 <= hz <= GEQ_BANDS[-1] * 1.5:
        raise ParameterRangeError(
            f"{hz} Hz is outside the GEQ range {GEQ_BANDS[0]}-{GEQ_BANDS[-1]} Hz"
        )
    return min(range(len(GEQ_BANDS)), key=lambda i: abs(GEQ_BANDS[i] - hz))


_PEQ_TYPE = EnumDomain({0x00: "bell", 0x06: "shelf"})
_EQ_CLASSES = _INPUTS | _FX_RETURNS | _MIXES
```

and the specs (append to `SPECS`):

```python
    ParamSpec(name="hpf_freq", nrpn_id=0x13, vx=Fixed(0x07),
              domain=Interpolated(lo=0x00, hi=0x7F, taper=HPF_TAPER),
              applies_to=_INPUTS),
    ParamSpec(name="hpf_in", nrpn_id=0x14, vx=Fixed(0x00), domain=Boolean(),
              applies_to=_INPUTS, notes="VX is 0x00 here, not 0x07."),

    # --- GEQ (PRD 7.2). Mixes and LR only. ---
    ParamSpec(name="geq_gain", nrpn_id=0x70, vx=GeqBand(),
              domain=Interpolated(lo=0x00, hi=0x7F, taper=GEQ_GAIN_TAPER),
              applies_to=_MIXES,
              notes="28 bands, index 0x00-0x1B. Band 15 is 1 kHz (PRD 11.1)."),
    ParamSpec(name="geq_in", nrpn_id=0x71, vx=Fixed(0x00), domain=Boolean(),
              applies_to=_MIXES, notes="VX is 0x00 here, not 0x07."),
```

### Task 5.4: Registry — gate, compressor, delay and FX delay

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/registry.py`
- Modify: `tests/fixtures/golden_params.yaml`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_registry_dynamics.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `gate.attack/release/hold/threshold/depth`, `gate_in`; `comp.type/attack/release/knee/ratio/threshold/makeup`, `comp_in`; `delay_time` (registered **twice**, disjoint `applies_to`), `delay_in`; `fx_delay_fine`, `fx_delay_coarse`, `fx_delay_link`.

**Three deliberate shapes here:**
- `gate_in` (`46`), `comp_in` (`68`) and `delay_in` (`6D`) use `VX = 0x00`. Everything else in these families uses `0x07`.
- **`delay_time` is registered twice with disjoint `applies_to` sets** (PRD §7.2). The input-channel range is half-scale (`0x00`–`0x40` = 0–85 ms) against mix/group/matrix (`0x00`–`0x7F` = 0–170 ms). Registering twice keeps `ParamSpec` uniform and makes the golden vectors cover both explicitly. Writing raw `0x41`–`0x7F` to an input channel is a `ParameterRangeError`; receiving it is clamped to `0x40` with a warning.
- **`48` serves double duty** as FX-delay coarse time and FX-delay link, discriminated only by `VX` (`05`/`07` vs `06`) — PRD §14.5. The registry key for this family must be `(id, vx)`, which is what `Registry.register_exact` exists for. For fine resolution, send **LSB first, then MSB**.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_registry_dynamics.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass, Input, Mix
from qumidi.protocol.params import Fixed, FxTap, Tier
from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY
from qumidi.protocol.units import INPUT_DELAY_TAPER, MIX_DELAY_TAPER

IDS = [
    ("gate.attack", 0x41, 0x07), ("gate.release", 0x42, 0x07),
    ("gate.hold", 0x43, 0x07), ("gate.threshold", 0x44, 0x07),
    ("gate.depth", 0x45, 0x07), ("gate_in", 0x46, 0x00),
    ("comp.type", 0x61, 0x07), ("comp.attack", 0x62, 0x07),
    ("comp.release", 0x63, 0x07), ("comp.knee", 0x64, 0x07),
    ("comp.ratio", 0x65, 0x07), ("comp.threshold", 0x66, 0x07),
    ("comp.makeup", 0x67, 0x07), ("comp_in", 0x68, 0x00),
    ("delay_in", 0x6D, 0x00),
]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name,nrpn_id,filler", IDS)
def test_ids_and_fillers(name, nrpn_id, filler) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert spec.nrpn_id == nrpn_id
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, Fixed)
    assert spec.vx.value == filler


def test_comp_type_enumerates_the_four_documented_modes() -> None:
    domain = REGISTRY.lookup("comp.type").domain
    assert domain.as_dict == {  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        0x00: "manual_rms",
        0x01: "auto_slow_opto",
        0x02: "auto_punchbag",
        0x03: "manual_peak",
    }


def test_delay_time_is_registered_twice_with_disjoint_classes() -> None:
    # PRD 7.2: this is the one place a single parameter needs two domains.
    on_input = REGISTRY.lookup("delay_time", ChannelClass.INPUT)
    on_mix = REGISTRY.lookup("delay_time", ChannelClass.MIX_MONO)
    assert on_input is not on_mix
    assert on_input.nrpn_id == on_mix.nrpn_id == 0x6C
    assert on_input.applies_to.isdisjoint(on_mix.applies_to)


def test_input_delay_is_capped_at_0x40() -> None:
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup("delay_time", ChannelClass.INPUT)
    assert (spec.domain.lo, spec.domain.hi) == (0x00, 0x40)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert spec.domain.taper is INPUT_DELAY_TAPER  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        spec.domain.validate_raw(0x41, "delay_time", "Input 6")


def test_mix_delay_uses_the_full_range() -> None:
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup("delay_time", ChannelClass.MIX_MONO)
    assert (spec.domain.lo, spec.domain.hi) == (0x00, 0x7F)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert spec.domain.taper is MIX_DELAY_TAPER  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert spec.domain.validate_raw(0x7F, "delay_time", "Mix 1") == 0x7F


def test_receiving_an_over_range_input_delay_clamps_rather_than_raising(caplog) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup("delay_time", ChannelClass.INPUT)
    assert spec.domain.clamp_inbound(0x60, "delay_time", "Input 6") == 0x40


def test_the_fx_delay_48_collision_resolves_on_vx() -> None:
    # PRD 14.5: 48 is both coarse time and link, discriminated only by VX.
    coarse = REGISTRY.resolve(ChannelClass.FX_SEND, 0x48, 0x05)
    link = REGISTRY.resolve(ChannelClass.FX_SEND, 0x48, 0x06)
    right = REGISTRY.resolve(ChannelClass.FX_SEND, 0x48, 0x07)
    assert coarse is not None and coarse.name == "fx_delay_coarse"
    assert link is not None and link.name == "fx_delay_link"
    assert right is not None and right.name == "fx_delay_coarse"


def test_fx_delay_parameters_are_experimental_and_use_the_tap_vx_mode() -> None:
    for name in ("fx_delay_fine", "fx_delay_coarse", "fx_delay_link"):
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
        assert spec.tier is Tier.EXPERIMENTAL
    assert isinstance(REGISTRY.lookup("fx_delay_fine").vx, FxTap)


def test_dynamics_do_not_apply_to_dcas() -> None:
    assert ChannelClass.DCA not in REGISTRY.lookup("gate.attack").applies_to
    assert ChannelClass.DCA not in REGISTRY.lookup("comp.ratio").applies_to
```

Then, after `REGISTRY = Registry(SPECS)`, resolve the collision explicitly:

```python
# PRD 14.5: 0x48 is both FX-delay coarse time and FX-delay link, discriminated
# only by VX. Registry lookup for inbound decode must key on (id, vx) for this
# family, not id alone.
_COARSE = REGISTRY.lookup("fx_delay_coarse")
REGISTRY.register_exact(_COARSE, FxTap.LEFT)
REGISTRY.register_exact(_COARSE, FxTap.RIGHT)
REGISTRY.register_exact(REGISTRY.lookup("fx_delay_link"), FxTap.LINK)
```

`FxTap.LEFT` etc. are dataclass field defaults, so reference them as `FxTap().LEFT` or promote them to module-level constants in `params.py` — prefer the latter: add `FX_TAP_LEFT = 0x05`, `FX_TAP_LINK = 0x06`, `FX_TAP_RIGHT = 0x07` to `params.py` and use those here and in `FxTap`.

### Task 5.5: Registry — assigns, and the golden-vector completeness gate

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/registry.py`
- Modify: `tests/fixtures/golden_params.yaml`
- Modify: `tests/test_golden_vectors.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_registry_assigns.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `dca_assign` (`40`/`07`, `FlaggedIndex`), `mute_group_assign` (`5C`/`07`, `FlaggedIndex`).
- Adds the **completeness test**: assert that the set of `(param, vx_mode)` pairs in the fixture covers the registry exhaustively, failing if any registry entry has no vector.

**Why completeness matters (PRD §13.2).** Without it, a parameter added later is silently untested — which is precisely the failure this fixture exists to prevent.

**The label erratum (PRD §14.2).** The spec's DCA Group Assign entry reads "Off Mute Grp 1-4 = 00 to 03", a copy-paste from the row above. The *encoding* is correct as given (`0x40 | index` for on); only the label is wrong. Do not "fix" the encoding.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_registry_assigns.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Input
from qumidi.protocol.params import Fixed, FlaggedIndex
from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY
from qumidi.state import sub_index_for


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name,nrpn_id", [("dca_assign", 0x40), ("mute_group_assign", 0x5C)])
def test_assigns_are_flagged_index_with_a_fixed_07_filler(name, nrpn_id) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert spec.nrpn_id == nrpn_id
    assert isinstance(spec.vx, Fixed) and spec.vx.value == 0x07
    assert isinstance(spec.domain, FlaggedIndex)
    assert spec.domain.count == 4
    assert spec.domain.on_flag == 0x40


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["dca_assign", "mute_group_assign"])
@pytest.mark.parametrize("index,on,expected", [
    (0, False, 0x00), (3, False, 0x03), (0, True, 0x40), (3, True, 0x43),
])
def test_the_documented_encoding(name, index, on, expected) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 14.2: the DCA row's *label* is a copy-paste error, but the encoding
    # is correct as given.
    domain = REGISTRY.lookup(name).domain
    assert domain.encode(index=index, on=on) == expected  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", ["dca_assign", "mute_group_assign"])
def test_the_sub_index_comes_from_the_value_not_vx(name) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    spec = REGISTRY.lookup(name)
    assert sub_index_for(spec, vx=0x07, value=0x42) == 2
    assert sub_index_for(spec, vx=0x07, value=0x02) == 2


def test_an_out_of_range_group_index_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        REGISTRY.lookup("dca_assign").domain.encode(index=4, on=True)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
```

Append the completeness gate to `tests/test_golden_vectors.py`:

```python
def test_the_fixture_covers_every_registry_entry() -> None:
    """PRD 13.2: a completeness test, not just a correctness test.

    Without this, a parameter added later is silently untested -- which is
    precisely the failure this fixture exists to prevent.
    """
    covered = {str(vec["param"]) for vec in VECTORS}
    registered = {spec.name for spec in REGISTRY}
    missing = sorted(registered - covered)
    assert not missing, (
        f"{len(missing)} registry parameters have no golden vector: {', '.join(missing)}. "
        f"Transcribe one from the PRD 7.2 table by hand -- never generate it."
    )


def test_the_fixture_covers_every_vx_mode() -> None:
    from qumidi.protocol.params import DsnakeSocket, Fixed, FxTap, GeqBand, SendTarget

    modes_in_fixture = set()
    for vec in VECTORS:
        ch = channel_from_raw(int(vec["channel"]))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        spec = spec_for(str(vec["param"]), ch.cls)
        modes_in_fixture.add(type(spec.vx))
    assert modes_in_fixture >= {Fixed, SendTarget, GeqBand, DsnakeSocket, FxTap}


def test_the_fixture_covers_both_fixed_filler_values() -> None:
    # The 0x07 / 0x00 alternation is the most common transcription slip.
    from qumidi.protocol.params import Fixed

    fillers = set()
    for vec in VECTORS:
        ch = channel_from_raw(int(vec["channel"]))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        spec = spec_for(str(vec["param"]), ch.cls)
        if isinstance(spec.vx, Fixed):
            fillers.add(spec.vx.value)
    assert fillers == {0x00, 0x07}
```

```bash
# PEQ: open the PEQ screen for channel 6 first.

qumidi set --host <ip> gate_in input:6 raw:1

qumidi set --host <ip> comp_in input:6 raw:1

# GEQ on LR: open the GEQ screen. Band 15 is 1 kHz.

qumidi set --host <ip> geq_gain lr -6db --to-band 15

# Preamp

# Delay: confirm the input ceiling behaves differently from the mix ceiling.
qumidi set --host <ip> delay_time input:6 raw:0x40      # ~85 ms
qumidi set --host <ip> delay_time mix:3 raw:0x7F        # ~170 ms

```

(The `--to-band` argument is the GEQ analogue of `--to`; add it to the `set` subcommand alongside `--to`, resolving to a raw `vx` integer.)

Expected: each console screen follows. The comp ratio should read **2.6:1** at raw `0x50` — that is the one interior anchor the spec gives and it validates the whole ratio curve. The last command must be refused by the library with a `ParameterRangeError` naming `Input 6` and `0x40`, without any bytes reaching the console.

Record anything that does *not* follow: that is an M6 item, and a wrong `VX` filler is the first hypothesis.

# Slice 6 — It knows the channel names

**Slice goal:** `qumidi dump-state --host H` shows your real channel names, and `qumidi set-name --host H input:6 "Kick"` changes the name on the console's display.

**Names are not in the state push (PRD §9.4).** A "complete" sync is the state sync *plus* a paced sweep of name requests over every valid channel. That sweep is the slowest part of connecting, which is why `sync_names` is skippable.

**`None`, not a sentinel string.** A missing name must not be the string `"UNKNOWN"` — a channel can legitimately be *named* "UNKNOWN", and the two must stay distinguishable.

---

### Task 6.1: Name encoding, validation and the sweep

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/sysex.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_names.py`
- Test: `tests/test_name_sweep.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `get_name(midi_channel: int, ch: int) -> bytes`
- `set_name(midi_channel: int, ch: int, name: str) -> bytes` — validates length and character set, raising `ParameterRangeError`.
- `parse_name_reply(sx: QuSysex) -> tuple[int, str]` — tolerant on receive.
- `NAMEABLE_CLASSES: frozenset[ChannelClass]`
- `mixer.name(ch) -> str | None`, `await mixer.set_name(ch, name)`, `await mixer.sweep_names()`
- `mixer.names: dict[ChannelId, str | None]`
- Constructor arguments `sync_names: bool = True`, `verify_name_writes: bool = True`, `name_sweep_interval: float = 0.020`.
- `FakeQu.names: dict[int, str]`
- `qumidi set-name --host H <channel-ref> "<name>"`

**Requirements from PRD §9.4, each with a test:**
- Sweep only channels valid for the detected model, driven by an explicit `NAMEABLE_CLASSES` set. Mute groups may not have names; a channel returning nothing after retries is recorded as "has no name" rather than retried on every resync.
- Pace requests at 20 ms by default. Do not fire 100 sysex requests at once.
- A missing name yields `None` and never an exception that aborts the sweep.
- Bound length and character set on write; over-long names raise rather than being silently truncated by the console, because a truncated name round-trips differently than it was set and will oscillate any state-mirroring application.
- On receive, replace bytes `>= 0x80` or `< 0x20` with `?` and log.
- A `03` name-set is **not acknowledged**. Confirm with a `01` get after the set (`verify_name_writes=True`).

`tests/protocol/test_names.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol import constants as c
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import QuSysex, get_name, parse_name_reply, set_name

HEADER = bytes([0xF0, 0x00, 0x00, 0x1A, 0x50, 0x11, 0x01, 0x00])


def test_get_name_request() -> None:
    assert get_name(0, 0x25) == HEADER + bytes([0x00, 0x01, 0x25, 0xF7])


def test_set_name_request() -> None:
    assert set_name(0, 0x25, "Kick") == HEADER + bytes(
        [0x00, 0x03, 0x25, ord("K"), ord("i"), ord("c"), ord("k"), 0xF7]
    )


def test_set_name_rejects_an_over_long_name() -> None:
    # PRD 9.4: raise rather than let the console truncate, because a truncated
    # name round-trips differently than it was set and oscillates any
    # state-mirroring application.
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError) as excinfo:
        set_name(0, 0x25, "A" * (c.MAX_NAME_LENGTH + 1))
    assert str(c.MAX_NAME_LENGTH) in str(excinfo.value)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", ["Ki\nck", "Kick\x00", "Kìck", "Kick\x7f"])
def test_set_name_rejects_non_printable_and_non_ascii(bad) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        set_name(0, 0x25, bad)


def test_parse_name_reply() -> None:
    sx = QuSysex(midi_channel=0, command=0x02, payload=bytes([0x25]) + b"Kick")
    assert parse_name_reply(sx) == (0x25, "Kick")


def test_parse_name_reply_is_tolerant_of_bad_bytes(caplog) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 9.4: on receive, replace bytes >= 0x80 or below 0x20 with ? and log.
    sx = QuSysex(midi_channel=0, command=0x02, payload=bytes([0x25, 0x4B, 0x01, 0x63]))
    ch, name = parse_name_reply(sx)
    assert (ch, name) == (0x25, "K?c")


def test_parse_name_reply_of_an_empty_name_returns_empty_string() -> None:
    sx = QuSysex(midi_channel=0, command=0x02, payload=bytes([0x25]))
    assert parse_name_reply(sx) == (0x25, "")


def test_parse_name_reply_rejects_an_empty_payload() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValueError):
        parse_name_reply(QuSysex(midi_channel=0, command=0x02, payload=b""))
```

`tests/test_name_sweep.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Dca, Input, Mix


async def test_names_arrive_after_sync(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick", 0x67: "MainLR"})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.name(Input(6)) == "Kick"
        assert mixer.name(Mix.LR) == "MainLR"


async def test_a_channel_with_no_name_is_none_not_a_sentinel_string(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 9.4: a channel can legitimately be *named* "UNKNOWN".
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick"}, name_silent={0x26})
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, name_request_timeout=0.05, name_request_retries=1
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.name(Input(7)) is None


async def test_the_sweep_is_paced(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 9.4: do not fire 100 sysex requests at once.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu16", names={})
    started = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, name_sweep_interval=0.01
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
    elapsed = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() - started
    assert elapsed >= 0.1


async def test_the_sweep_covers_only_channels_the_model_has(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu16", names={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        requested = fake.name_requests
    assert 0x2F in requested          # Input 16
    assert 0x30 not in requested      # Input 17 does not exist on a Qu-16
    assert 0x68 not in requested      # no groups on a Qu-16


async def test_the_sweep_skips_classes_that_have_no_names(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 9.4: driven by an explicit NAMEABLE_CLASSES set.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
    assert 0x50 not in fake.name_requests   # mute group masters


async def test_sync_names_false_skips_the_sweep_entirely(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick"})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, sync_names=False) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.name(Input(6)) is None
        assert fake.name_requests == []


async def test_set_name_round_trips_and_is_verified(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 9.4: a 03 name-set is not acknowledged; confirm with a 01 get.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick"})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fake.name_requests.clear()
        await mixer.set_name(Input(6), "Snare")
        assert fake.names[0x25] == "Snare"
        assert mixer.name(Input(6)) == "Snare"
        assert 0x25 in fake.name_requests  # the verifying get


async def test_set_name_reports_a_console_that_did_not_take_it(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick"}, ignore_name_sets=True)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError) as excinfo:
            await mixer.set_name(Input(6), "Snare")
        assert "Snare" in str(excinfo.value)


async def test_set_name_on_a_dca_works(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.set_name(Dca(1), "Drums")
        assert fake.names[0x10] == "Drums"


async def test_one_slow_name_does_not_abort_the_sweep(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(names={0x25: "Kick", 0x27: "Tom"}, name_silent={0x26})
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, name_request_timeout=0.05, name_request_retries=1
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.name(Input(6)) == "Kick"
        assert mixer.name(Input(7)) is None
        assert mixer.name(Input(8)) == "Tom"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the name codec to `qumidi/protocol/sysex.py`**

```python
def get_name(midi_channel: int, ch: int) -> bytes:
    """Build a ``01`` channel-name request (PRD 9.2)."""
    return build_sysex(midi_channel, bytes([c.CMD_GET_NAME, ch]))


def set_name(midi_channel: int, ch: int, name: str) -> bytes:
    """Build a ``03`` channel-name write, validating length and characters.

    PRD 9.4: over-long names raise rather than being silently truncated by the
    console -- a truncated name round-trips differently than it was set and
    will oscillate any state-mirroring application. The length limit is
    provisional until measured on hardware in M6.
    """
    if len(name) > c.MAX_NAME_LENGTH:
        raise ParameterRangeError(
            f"channel name {name!r} is {len(name)} characters; the console's strip "
            f"display holds {c.MAX_NAME_LENGTH}"
        )
    for index, char in enumerate(name):
        if not 0x20 <= ord(char) <= 0x7E:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"channel name {name!r} contains a non-printable or non-ASCII character "
                f"{char!r} at position {index}; the protocol carries 7-bit ASCII only"
            )
    return build_sysex(midi_channel, bytes([c.CMD_SET_NAME, ch]) + name.encode("ascii"))


def parse_name_reply(sx: QuSysex) -> tuple[int, str]:
    """Decode a ``02`` name reply, tolerantly (PRD 9.2, 9.4)."""
    if not sx.payload:
        raise ValueError("name reply has no channel byte")
    ch = sx.payload[0]
    chars = []
    replaced = False
    for byte in sx.payload[1:]:
        if 0x20 <= byte <= 0x7E:
            chars.append(chr(byte))
        else:
            chars.append("?")
            replaced = True
    if replaced:
        log.warning(
            "channel %#04x name contained non-printable bytes (%s); replaced with '?'",
            ch, sx.payload[1:].hex(" "),
        )
    return ch, "".join(chars)
```

Import `ParameterRangeError`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Add `NAMEABLE_CLASSES` and the sweep to the client**

In `qumidi/protocol/channels.py`:

```python
#: Which channel classes carry a name (PRD 9.4). Mute groups are excluded --
#: they are believed not to have names, and an explicit set stops the sweep
#: retrying them on every resync. Verify on hardware in M6.
NAMEABLE_CLASSES: frozenset[ChannelClass] = frozenset({
    ChannelClass.INPUT,
    ChannelClass.STEREO_INPUT,
    ChannelClass.MIX_MONO,
    ChannelClass.MIX_STEREO,
    ChannelClass.LR,
    ChannelClass.GROUP,
    ChannelClass.MATRIX,
    ChannelClass.DCA,
    ChannelClass.FX_SEND,
    ChannelClass.FX_RETURN,
})


def nameable_channels(caps: ModelCaps) -> list[ChannelId]:
    """Every channel on this model that the name sweep should visit."""
    out = []
    for ch in ALL_CHANNELS:
        if ch.cls not in NAMEABLE_CLASSES:
            continue
        limit, _ = _limit(caps, ch)
        if ch.index <= limit:
            out.append(ch)
    return out
```

In `qumidi/client_async.py`:

```python
        self.sync_names = sync_names
        self.verify_name_writes = verify_name_writes
        self.name_sweep_interval = name_sweep_interval
        self.name_request_timeout = name_request_timeout
        self.name_request_retries = name_request_retries
        self.names: dict[ChannelId, str | None] = {}
        self._name_waiters: dict[int, asyncio.Future[str]] = {}
```

```python
    def name(self, ch: ChannelId) -> str | None:
        """The console's name for a channel, or None if it has none.

        None, never a sentinel string -- a channel can legitimately be *named*
        "UNKNOWN" and the two must stay distinguishable (PRD 9.4).
        """
        return self.names.get(ch)

    async def sweep_names(self) -> None:
        """Request every nameable channel's name, paced (PRD 9.4).

        Not part of the state push, so this runs after sync. A missing name
        yields None and never aborts the sweep.
        """
        n = self._require_synced("sweep names")
        caps = self._caps or PERMISSIVE_CAPS
        for ch in nameable_channels(caps):
            self.names[ch] = await self._request_name(n, ch)
            await asyncio.sleep(self.name_sweep_interval)
        log.info("name sweep complete: %d named channels", sum(
            1 for v in self.names.values() if v))

    async def _request_name(self, n: int, ch: ChannelId) -> str | None:
        for attempt in range(self.name_request_retries + 1):
            future: asyncio.Future[str] = asyncio.get_running_loop().create_future()
            self._name_waiters[ch.value] = future
            try:
                await self._send(get_name(n, ch.value))
                return await asyncio.wait_for(future, timeout=self.name_request_timeout)
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                log.debug("no name reply for %s (attempt %d)", ch.label, attempt + 1)
            finally:
                self._name_waiters.pop(ch.value, None)
        return None

    async def set_name(self, ch: ChannelId, name: str) -> None:
        """Write a channel name, and confirm it (PRD 9.4).

        The console does not acknowledge a name write, so with
        verify_name_writes=True this issues a read-back and raises if the
        console did not take the name.
        """
        n = self._require_synced(f"set the name of {ch.label}")
        check_channel(self._caps, ch)
        if ch.cls not in NAMEABLE_CLASSES:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"{ch.label} does not have a name")
        await self._send(set_name(n, ch.value, name))
        if not self.verify_name_writes:
            self.names[ch] = name
            return
        observed = await self._request_name(n, ch)
        self.names[ch] = observed
        if observed != name:
            raise ParameterRangeError(
                f"{ch.label}: set the name to {name!r} but the console reports "
                f"{observed!r}. Name writes are unacknowledged, so this read-back is "
                f"the only confirmation available (PRD 9.4)."
            )
```

In `_on_sysex`, handle the reply:

```python

            try:
                raw_ch, name = parse_name_reply(parsed)
            except ValueError as exc:
                log.warning("malformed name reply: %s", exc)
                return
            waiter = self._name_waiters.get(raw_ch)
            if waiter is not None and not waiter.done():
                waiter.set_result(name)
```

And after the sync handshake succeeds in `_sync()`:

```python

            # PRD 9.3.8: keep the sync window short -- do not interleave the
            # name sweep with the burst.
            await self.sweep_names()
```

Note that `wait_synced()` currently resolves on the `14` marker, before the sweep. Change `_sync()` to set a separate `_ready_event` after the sweep, and have `wait_synced()` await that, so `mixer.name(...)` is populated by the time it returns.

# Slice 7 — Scene recall doesn't lie

**Slice goal:** `qumidi recall --host H 12` recalls scene 12, and a `qumidi monitor` running alongside shows the cache going stale, a `SceneRecalled` event, a resync, a name re-sweep, and finally `SyncComplete` — in that order.

**Why recall is a state operation and not a fire-and-forget write (PRD §7.2).** A scene changes hundreds of parameters at once, and the spec does not promise the console echoes every one of them. Treating recall as a write leaves the cache confidently wrong about the entire console.

---

### Task 7.1: Scene recall with invalidation and resync

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py`
- Test: `tests/test_scene_recall.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `await mixer.recall_scene(scene: int, *, resync: bool = True) -> None` — **1-based**, to match the console's own display. `recall_scene(1)` sends `SS = 0x00`.
- `mixer.last_recalled_scene: int | None`
- `mixer.on_scene_recalled(cb)`
- Constructor argument `scene_settle_delay: float = 0.5`.
- `FakeQu.current_scene: int | None`, `FakeQu.scenes: dict[int, dict]` (a per-scene parameter map that becomes the pushed state after a recall).
- `qumidi recall --host H <scene> [--no-resync]`

**The sequence (PRD §7.2), in this order:**
1. Send bank select **immediately before** the program change — the Qu ignores program changes if Bank 1 is not currently selected: `BN 00 00`, `BN 20 00`, `CN SS`.
2. Mark the entire parameter cache `STALE`.
3. Emit `SceneRecalled` **before** the resync, so applications can grey out their UI.
4. Wait `scene_settle_delay` (500 ms default — a heuristic, not a protocol guarantee; PRD §14.13), then run a full `resync()`, followed by a name sweep if `sync_names` is on. Channel names are scene data and will have changed.
5. Emit `SyncComplete` after.
6. Invalidate any registered `SurfaceMap` — the custom layer is scene data.

**And say plainly what cannot be done (PRD §7.2).** There is no way to read the current scene number, scene names, or the scene list. `mixer.last_recalled_scene` reflects only what *this client* recalled and is `None` after connect or after a recall from the console's own surface.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `recall_scene`**

```python
    @property
    def last_recalled_scene(self) -> int | None:
        """The scene this client last recalled, 1-based.

        There is no way to read the current scene number from the console, so
        this reflects only what this client recalled. It is None after connect
        and after any recall made from the console's own surface (PRD 7.2).
        """
        return self._last_recalled_scene

    async def recall_scene(self, scene: int, *, resync: bool = True) -> None:
        """Recall a scene, 1-based to match the console's display (PRD 7.2).

        A scene changes hundreds of parameters at once and the console does not
        promise to echo them, so this is a state-invalidating operation rather
        than a fire-and-forget write: the cache goes STALE, SceneRecalled is
        emitted, and after a settling delay the whole state is rebuilt.
        """
        n = self._require_synced(f"recall scene {scene}")
        if not 1 <= scene <= 100:
            raise ParameterRangeError(f"scene must be 1..100 (as displayed), got {scene}")
        status = c.CONTROL_CHANGE | n
        payload = bytes(
            [
                status, c.CC_BANK_SELECT_MSB, 0x00,
                status, c.CC_BANK_SELECT_LSB, 0x00,
                c.PROGRAM_CHANGE | n, scene - 1,
            ]
        )
        await self._send(payload)
        self._last_recalled_scene = scene

        # Everything we believe about the console is now suspect.
        self.cache.mark_all_stale()
        self._clear_expectations()
        for ch in list(self.names):
            self.names[ch] = None
        if self._surface_map is not None:
            # PRD 6.3: the custom layer is scene data and may have changed
            # without notification.
            self._surface_map.invalidate()
        self.events.emit_scene_recalled(scene)

        if not resync:
            log.warning(
                "scene %d recalled with resync=False; the cache is STALE and will not "
                "be rebuilt until you call resync()", scene,
            )
            return

        # PRD 14.13: 500 ms is a heuristic, not a protocol guarantee. Program
        # Change is unacknowledged and the console gives no "recall finished"
        # message; a large scene may need longer.
        await asyncio.sleep(self.scene_settle_delay)
        await self.resync()
        if self.sync_names:
            await self.sweep_names()
```

Add `self._last_recalled_scene: int | None = None`, `self._surface_map = None` and the `scene_settle_delay` constructor argument. `resync()` must itself emit `SyncComplete`, which it already does via the `14` handler.

### Task 7.2: Snapshot, export and diff

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/snapshot.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py`
- Test: `tests/test_snapshot.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `mixer.snapshot() -> dict` — plain, JSON-serialisable.
- `mixer.export_snapshot(path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> None`
- `mixer.diff(snapshot: dict) -> list[SnapshotDiff]`
- `SnapshotDiff` frozen dataclass: `channel, param, sub_index, snapshot_raw, current_raw, snapshot_freshness, current_freshness`.
- `load_snapshot(path) -> dict`
- `qumidi dump-state --host H --json` writes the same structure; add `--out FILE`.

**Restoring is explicitly *not* "push this snapshot to the console" (PRD §10.5).** `diff()` returns what differs and lets the application decide what to write. A blind `restore()` replaying hundreds of unpaced writes is the single most dangerous operation this library could offer, and a partial failure halfway through leaves the console in a state that is in neither snapshot. If an `apply_snapshot()` is ever added, it must be paced through the §4.5 queue, resumable, and dry-runnable.

**`UNKNOWN` and `UNCONFIRMED` entries are included with their freshness, not omitted** — a snapshot that silently drops unknowns is indistinguishable from one taken before sync.

Expected: FAIL — `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'qumidi.snapshot'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/snapshot.py`**

```python
"""Snapshot and diff (PRD 10.5).

The cache is the only complete picture of console state the library holds, and
it is expensive to rebuild -- a full sync plus a paced name sweep. This makes
it serialisable.

Restoring is deliberately NOT "push this snapshot to the console". diff()
reports what differs and the application decides what to write. A blind
restore replaying hundreds of unpaced writes is the single most dangerous
operation this library could offer, and a partial failure halfway through
leaves the console in a state that is in neither snapshot.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelClass, ChannelId
from qumidi.state import Freshness, StateCache

SNAPSHOT_VERSION = 1


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SnapshotDiff:
    channel: ChannelId
    param: str
    sub_index: int | None
    snapshot_raw: int | None
    current_raw: int | None
    snapshot_freshness: str
    current_freshness: str


def build_snapshot(
    *,
    box_id: int | None,
    model: str | None,
    firmware: tuple[int, int] | None,
    cache: StateCache,
    names: dict[ChannelId, str | None],
    now: float,
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    values = []
    for (raw_ch, cls, param, sub), value in cache.items():
        values.append(
            {
                "channel_raw": raw_ch,
                "channel_class": cls.name,
                "param": param,
                "sub_index": sub,
                "raw": value.raw,
                "freshness": value.freshness.name,
                "age": None if value.updated_at is None else round(now - value.updated_at, 3),
            }
        )
    return {
        "version": SNAPSHOT_VERSION,
        "box_id": box_id,
        "model": model,
        "firmware": None if firmware is None else list(firmware),
        "names": {str(ch.value): name for ch, name in names.items() if name is not None},
        "values": sorted(
            values, key=lambda v: (v["channel_raw"], v["param"], v["sub_index"] or -1)
        ),
    }


def load_snapshot(path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        data: dict[str, Any] = json.load(handle)
    if data.get("version") != SNAPSHOT_VERSION:
        raise ValueError(
            f"snapshot version {data.get('version')!r} is not {SNAPSHOT_VERSION}"
        )
    return data


def diff_snapshot(
    snapshot: dict[str, Any], cache: StateCache
) -> list[SnapshotDiff]:
    """Report every parameter whose current raw value differs from the snapshot."""
    from qumidi.protocol.channels import GLOBAL, channel_from_raw, dsnake_socket_channel

    out: list[SnapshotDiff] = []
    for entry in snapshot["values"]:
        cls = ChannelClass[entry["channel_class"]]
        if cls is ChannelClass.GLOBAL:
            ch = GLOBAL
        elif cls is ChannelClass.DSNAKE:
            ch = dsnake_socket_channel(int(entry["channel_raw"]))
        else:
            ch = channel_from_raw(int(entry["channel_raw"]))
        current = cache.get(ch, entry["param"], entry["sub_index"])
        if current.raw == entry["raw"]:
            continue
        out.append(
            SnapshotDiff(
                channel=ch,
                param=entry["param"],
                sub_index=entry["sub_index"],
                snapshot_raw=entry["raw"],
                current_raw=current.raw,
                snapshot_freshness=entry["freshness"],
                current_freshness=current.freshness.name,
            )
        )
    return out
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Wire it into the client**

```python
    def snapshot(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """A JSON-serialisable picture of everything the library knows.

        UNKNOWN and UNCONFIRMED entries are included with their freshness,
        never omitted (PRD 10.5).
        """
        return build_snapshot(
            box_id=self._box_id,
            model=None if self.model is None else self.model.name,
            firmware=self._firmware,
            cache=self.cache,
            names=self.names,
            now=time.monotonic(),
        )

    def export_snapshot(self, path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> None:
        with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            json.dump(self.snapshot(), handle, indent=2)

    def diff(self, snapshot: dict[str, Any]) -> list[SnapshotDiff]:
        """What differs between a snapshot and the current cache.

        There is deliberately no apply/restore counterpart -- see PRD 10.5.
        """
        return diff_snapshot(snapshot, self.cache)
```

# Slice 8 — It survives the cable being pulled

**Slice goal:** with `qumidi monitor --host H` running against a real console, unplug the network cable, wait, plug it back in, and watch the library transition to `RECONNECTING`, mark everything `STALE`, reconnect, re-sync, re-sweep names, and return to `READY` without intervention.

---

### Task 8.1: Reconnect with backoff, and a `close()` that actually closes

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/transport/tcp.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Test: `tests/transport/test_reconnect.py`
- Test: `tests/test_lifecycle.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `TcpTransport(..., auto_reconnect: bool = True, max_reconnect_attempts: int | None = None)`.
- `AsyncQuMixer` grows an `on_reconnect` internal hook driving the post-reconnect sequence.
- `FakeQu.drop_connection()` and a `drop_after_sync: bool` fault mode.

**Backoff (PRD §4.4):** 0.5 s → 30 s cap, ×2 per attempt, ±20% jitter. On exhaustion of `max_reconnect_attempts`, transition to `DISCONNECTED` and emit a terminal event rather than spinning forever.

**And the rule that makes `close()` trustworthy:** an explicit `close()` **disables reconnect**. A user-initiated close must not race with a pending backoff timer and resurrect the connection. `close()` is idempotent and cancels any in-flight reconnect task.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/transport/test_reconnect.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState
from qumidi.transport.tcp import TcpTransport


async def test_reconnects_after_the_server_drops_the_connection(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    states: list[ConnectionState] = []
    t = TcpTransport(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, auto_reconnect=True, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    )
    t.on_state_change(states.append)
    await t.connect()
    try:
        await fake.drop_connection()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
        assert ConnectionState.RECONNECTING in states
        assert t.is_connected
    finally:
        await t.close()


async def test_backoff_grows_and_is_jittered() -> None:
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=1, reconnect_backoff_min=1.0, reconnect_backoff_max=10.0)
    delays = [t._backoff_delay(n) for n in range(6)]  # noqa: SLF001
    assert 0.8 <= delays[0] <= 1.2
    assert 1.6 <= delays[1] <= 2.4
    assert delays[-1] <= 12.0  # capped, with jitter headroom
    # Jitter means two draws at the same attempt differ.
    assert len({round(t._backoff_delay(3), 6) for _ in range(20)}) > 1  # noqa: SLF001


async def test_max_attempts_gives_up_instead_of_spinning_forever() -> None:
    states: list[ConnectionState] = []
    t = TcpTransport(
        "127.0.0.1", port=1, connect_timeout=0.05,
        auto_reconnect=True, reconnect_backoff_min=0.01, max_reconnect_attempts=2,
    )
    t.on_state_change(states.append)
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        await t.connect()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.3)
    assert states[-1] is ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED
    await t.close()


async def test_close_during_backoff_does_not_resurrect_the_connection(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.4: a user-initiated close must not race with a pending backoff
    # timer. PRD 16.16.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    t = TcpTransport(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, auto_reconnect=True, reconnect_backoff_min=0.3
    )
    await t.connect()
    await fake.drop_connection()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.05)          # inside the backoff window
    await t.close()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.6)           # past when the retry would have fired
    assert not t.is_connected
    assert t.state is ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED


async def test_close_is_idempotent_and_leaves_no_tasks(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    before = len(asyncio.all_tasks())
    t = TcpTransport("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port)
    await t.connect()
    await t.close()
    await t.close()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
    assert len(asyncio.all_tasks()) <= before
```

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionLostError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Input
from qumidi.state import Freshness
from qumidi.transport.base import ConnectionState

SCENE = {(0x25, 0x17, 0x07): 0x62}


async def test_a_drop_marks_the_whole_cache_stale(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.4 and 16.4: never serve pre-disconnect values as current.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE)
    seen_stale = asyncio.Event()
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        def on_state(state: ConnectionState) -> None:
            if state is ConnectionState.RECONNECTING:
                assert mixer.get(Input(6), "fader").freshness is Freshness.STALE
                seen_stale.set()
        mixer.on_connection_state(on_state)
        await fake.drop_connection()
        await asyncio.wait_for(seen_stale.wait(), timeout=2.0)


async def test_reconnect_re_syncs_and_returns_to_ready(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE)
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        before = fake.sync_count
        await fake.drop_connection()
        for _ in range(60):
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            if mixer.state is ConnectionState.READY and fake.sync_count > before:
                break
        assert mixer.state is ConnectionState.READY
        assert mixer.get(Input(6), "fader").freshness is Freshness.KNOWN


async def test_pending_expectations_are_dropped_on_disconnect(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.4: leaving expectations live causes the first post-reconnect
    # surface move to be misclassified as an echo and swallowed.
    from qumidi.events import Source

    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE, echo=False)
    external: list[object] = []
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, echo_window=5.0, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.set(Input(6), "fader", raw=0x40)
        await fake.drop_connection()
        for _ in range(60):
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            if mixer.state is ConnectionState.READY:
                break
        mixer.on_change(lambda e: external.append(e) if e.source is Source.MIXER else None)
        await fake.send_surface_move(0x25, 0x17, 0x40, 0x07)
        await asyncio.sleep(0.15)
    assert len(external) == 1


async def test_box_id_and_channel_are_relearned_not_cached(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.4: the operator may have changed the console's MIDI channel, or
    # the host may now be a different console at the same IP.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE, midi_channel=0)
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.midi_channel == 0
        fake.midi_channel = 5
        await fake.drop_connection()
        for _ in range(60):
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            if mixer.midi_channel == 5:
                break
        assert mixer.midi_channel == 5


async def test_a_different_box_id_rebuilds_the_cache_from_empty(fake_qu_factory, caplog) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE, model="qu32")
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fake.box_id = 1  # a Qu-16 has appeared at this address
        fake._scene = {}  # noqa: SLF001
        await fake.drop_connection()
        for _ in range(60):
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            if mixer.box_id == 1:
                break
        assert mixer.get(Input(6), "fader").raw is None
    assert any("boxid" in r.message.lower() for r in caplog.records)


async def test_a_write_after_an_unrecoverable_drop_raises(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE)
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, auto_reconnect=False
    )
    await mixer.connect()
    await mixer.wait_synced()
    await fake.stop()
    await asyncio.sleep(0.2)
    with pytest.raises(QuConnectionLostError):
        await mixer.set(Input(6), "fader", raw=0x40)
    await mixer.close()


async def test_the_client_leaks_nothing_on_close(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 13.4: assert with asyncio.all_tasks() at teardown.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE)
    before = {t for t in asyncio.all_tasks()}
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.set(Input(6), "fader", raw=0x40)
    await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
    leaked = {t for t in asyncio.all_tasks()} - before
    assert not [t for t in leaked if not t.done()], leaked
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add reconnect to `TcpTransport`**

```python
        self.auto_reconnect = auto_reconnect
        self.max_reconnect_attempts = max_reconnect_attempts
        self.reconnect_backoff_min = reconnect_backoff_min
        self.reconnect_backoff_max = reconnect_backoff_max
        self._closing = False
        self._reconnect_task: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None
        self._on_reconnected: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]] | None = None
```

```python
    def set_reconnect_hook(self, hook: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]]) -> None:
        """Called after each successful reconnect, before READY."""
        self._on_reconnected = hook

    def _backoff_delay(self, attempt: int) -> float:
        """PRD 4.4: 0.5s -> 30s cap, x2 per attempt, +-20% jitter."""
        base = min(
            self.reconnect_backoff_min * (c.RECONNECT_BACKOFF_FACTOR ** attempt),
            self.reconnect_backoff_max,
        )
        jitter = base * c.RECONNECT_JITTER
        return base + random.uniform(-jitter, jitter)

    def _schedule_reconnect(self) -> None:
        if self._closing or not self.auto_reconnect:
            return
        if self._reconnect_task is not None and not self._reconnect_task.done():
            return
        self.set_state(ConnectionState.RECONNECTING)
        self._reconnect_task = asyncio.create_task(self._reconnect_loop(),
                                                   name="qumidi-reconnect")

    async def _reconnect_loop(self) -> None:
        attempt = 0
        while not self._closing:
            if self.max_reconnect_attempts is not None and attempt >= self.max_reconnect_attempts:
                log.error(
                    "giving up after %d reconnect attempts to %s:%d",
                    attempt, self.host, self.port,
                )
                self.set_state(ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED)
                return
            delay = self._backoff_delay(attempt)
            log.info("reconnecting to %s:%d in %.1fs (attempt %d)",
                     self.host, self.port, delay, attempt + 1)
            await asyncio.sleep(delay)
            if self._closing:
                return
            try:
                await self._open()
            except QuConnectionError as exc:
                log.debug("reconnect attempt %d failed: %s", attempt + 1, exc)
                attempt += 1
                continue
            self.set_state(ConnectionState.CONNECTED)
            if self._on_reconnected is not None:
                try:
                    await self._on_reconnected()
                except Exception:
                    log.exception("post-reconnect sync failed; will retry")
                    self._on_link_down()
                    attempt += 1
                    continue
            return
```

Split the socket setup out of `connect()` into `_open()`, and have `connect()` call `self._closing = False` then `_open()`. In `_on_link_down`, call `self._schedule_reconnect()`. In `close()`, set `self._closing = True` **first**, then cancel `self._reconnect_task` along with the other tasks. Import `random` and `Awaitable`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the post-reconnect sequence to `AsyncQuMixer`**

In `connect()`, after building the transport:

```python
        if isinstance(self._transport, TcpTransport):
            self._transport.set_reconnect_hook(self._after_reconnect)
        self._transport.on_state_change(self._on_transport_state)
```

```python
    def _on_transport_state(self, state: ConnectionState) -> None:
        if state in (ConnectionState.RECONNECTING, ConnectionState.DISCONNECTED):
            # PRD 4.4: mark the ENTIRE cache STALE before re-running the sync.
            # Never serve pre-disconnect values as current.
            self.cache.mark_all_stale()
            self._clear_expectations()
            self._outbound.clear()
            self._decoder.reset()
            self._ready_event.clear()
            self._sync_event.clear()

    async def _after_reconnect(self) -> None:
        """Re-establish everything the connection carried (PRD 4.4)."""
        previous_box_id = self._box_id
        # Re-learn N, BoxID and firmware every time. The operator may have
        # changed the console's MIDI channel, or the host may now be a
        # different console at the same IP.
        self._box_id = None
        self._firmware = None
        self._midi_channel = None
        self._caps = None
        await self._sync()
        if previous_box_id is not None and self._box_id != previous_box_id:
            log.warning(
                "BoxID changed from %s to %s across the reconnect; rebuilding the cache "
                "from empty rather than reusing keys for a different model",
                previous_box_id, self._box_id,
            )
            self.cache.clear()
            self.names.clear()
            await self._sync()
        if self.sync_names:
            # PRD 4.4: names are not in the state push and a scene may have
            # been recalled while we were disconnected.
            await self.sweep_names()
        if self._meters_enable_count > 0:
            await self._reenable_meters()
        self._ready_event.set()
```

`_meters_enable_count` and `_reenable_meters` are placeholders returning immediately until Slice 10; add them now as `self._meters_enable_count = 0` and an `async def _reenable_meters(self) -> None: return None` so this method does not need editing later.

In `_send`, raise when the link is gone and reconnect is disabled:

```python
    async def _send(self, data: bytes) -> None:
        if not self._transport.is_connected and not getattr(
            self._transport, "auto_reconnect", False
        ):
            raise QuConnectionLostError(
                f"connection to {self.host}:{self.port} was lost and auto_reconnect is off"
            )
        await self._outbound.send(data)
```

# Slice 9 — The ergonomic API

**Slice goal:** the PRD §11.1 example runs verbatim against a real console, and the PRD §11.2 synchronous example runs too.

**The invariant that makes this slice safe:** layer 5 contains **zero protocol constants**. Every proxy method is a thin call into `AsyncQuMixer.set`/`.get` with a registry-supplied name. If you find yourself typing a hex byte in `qumidi/model/`, the registry is missing an entry.

---

### Task 9.1: Parameter and section proxies

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/model/base.py`
- Test: `tests/model/test_proxies.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `ParamProxy(mixer, channel, param, sub=None)` with `await set_raw(v)`, `await set_db(v)`, `.raw -> int`, `.db -> float`, `.value -> CachedValue`, `await ramp_to_db(...)`, `await ramp_to_raw(...)`.
- `BoolProxy(ParamProxy)` with `await set(bool)` and `.enabled -> bool`.
- `EnumProxy(ParamProxy)` with `await set(label)` and `.label -> str`.
- `SectionProxy` base for grouped sections (`peq`, `gate`, `comp`).
- `MembershipProxy` for `dca_assign`/`mute_group_assign` with `await add(n)`, `await remove(n)`, `await set(n, on)`, `.groups -> frozenset[int]`.

**Reads mirror writes (PRD §11.1).** Every `set_db()`/`set_raw()` has a matching `.db`/`.raw` read returning tri-state semantics — a read of an `UNKNOWN` value **raises** `ValueUnknownError` rather than returning a plausible-looking number. `.value` returns the `CachedValue` itself for callers who want to inspect freshness.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/model/test_proxies.py` (create `tests/model/__init__.py` first):

```python
from qumidi.errors import ValueUnknownError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Input, Mix
from qumidi.state import Freshness

async def test_reading_an_unknown_value_raises_rather_than_guessing(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 11.1: a read of an UNKNOWN value raises rather than returning a

        assert "Input 6" in str(excinfo.value)
        assert "fader" in str(excinfo.value)


async def test_value_exposes_freshness_without_raising(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.input(6).fader.value.freshness is Freshness.UNKNOWN


async def test_bool_proxy(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene=SCENE)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.input(6).peq.enabled is True
        await mixer.input(6).peq.set_enabled(False)
        assert mixer.input(6).peq.enabled is False


async def test_enum_proxy_uses_labels(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.input(6).comp.set_type("manual_peak")
        assert mixer.input(6).comp.type.label == "manual_peak"


async def test_membership_proxy_keeps_two_dcas_independent(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 16.10.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        ch = mixer.input(6)
        await ch.dca_assign.add(1)
        await ch.dca_assign.add(3)
        with pytest.raises(ValueUnknownError):
            _ = ch.dca_assign.groups     # 2 and 4 are still unknown
        await ch.dca_assign.set(2, False)
        await ch.dca_assign.set(4, False)
        assert ch.dca_assign.groups == frozenset({1, 3})


async def test_send_proxy(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        send = mixer.input(6).send(Mix.MIX3)
        await send.set_level_db(-12.0)
        await send.set_pre(True)
        assert fake.state[(0x25, 0x50, 0x02)] == 0x01


async def test_ch_pan_is_sugar_for_the_lr_send(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 7.2: there is no separate "channel pan" parameter.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.input(6).pan.set_raw(0x25)
        assert fake.state[(0x25, 0x16, 0x07)] == 0x25
    assert "send(Mix.LR)" in (type(mixer.input(6)).pan.__doc__ or "")


async def test_the_facade_contains_no_protocol_constants() -> None:
    # The critical invariant from PRD 3: layer 5 is a thin proxy over layer 4.
    import pathlib
    import re

    for path in (pathlib.Path("qumidi/model")).rglob("*.py"):
        source = path.read_text()
        hex_literals = re.findall(r"0x[0-9A-Fa-f]{2}\b", source)
        assert not hex_literals, f"{path} contains protocol constants: {hex_literals}"
```
