- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/model/base.py`**

```python
"""Proxy objects for the ergonomic API (PRD 11.1).

CRITICAL INVARIANT (PRD 3): this layer contains zero protocol constants. Every
method here is a thin call into AsyncQuMixer.set/.get with a registry-supplied
parameter name. If you need a hex byte in this package, the registry is
missing an entry -- add it there instead.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from qumidi.errors import ValueUnknownError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelId
from qumidi.protocol.params import EnumDomain, Interpolated
from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY
from qumidi.state import CachedValue, Freshness

if TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover
    from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer


class ParamProxy:
    """One parameter on one channel (and optionally one sub-index)."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        mixer: AsyncQuMixer,
        channel: ChannelId,
        param: str,
        sub: int | None = None,
        vx: ChannelId | int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._channel = channel
        self._param = param
        self._sub = sub
        self._vx = vx
        self._ramp: asyncio.Task[None] | None = None

    # -- reads ------------------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def value(self) -> CachedValue:
        """The cached value with its freshness. Never raises."""
        return self._mixer.get(self._channel, self._param, self._sub)

    @property
    def raw(self) -> int:
        """The exact 7-bit value the console reported. Raises if unknown."""
        value = self.value
        if value.raw is None or value.freshness is Freshness.UNKNOWN:
            raise ValueUnknownError(
                f"{self._channel.label} {self._param}: the console has never reported "
                f"this value. Await wait_synced(), and note that not every parameter "
                f"appears in the state push."
            )
        return value.raw

    @property
    def db(self) -> float:
        """The value in engineering units. Approximate; .raw is the truth."""
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        if not isinstance(spec.domain, Interpolated):
            raise ValueUnknownError(
                f"{self._channel.label} {self._param} has no taper; read .raw instead"
            )
        return spec.domain.to_db(self.raw)

    @property
    def is_approximate(self) -> bool:
        """True where .db was extrapolated beyond the documented anchors."""
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        if not isinstance(spec.domain, Interpolated):
            return False
        return spec.domain.taper.is_approximate(self.raw) or not spec.domain.taper.verified

    # -- writes -----------------------------------------------------------

    async def set_raw(self, value: int) -> None:
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, self._param, raw=value, vx=self._vx)

    async def set_db(self, value: float) -> None:
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, self._param, db=value, vx=self._vx)

    # -- ramps (PRD 11.1) --------------------------------------------------

    async def ramp_to_raw(
        self, target: int, duration: float, steps: int | None = None
    ) -> None:
        """Move smoothly to ``target`` over ``duration`` seconds.

        Cancellable, paced through the outbound queue, and approximate in
        timing. A second ramp on the same parameter cancels the first rather
        than interleaving with it.
        """
        self.cancel_ramp()
        self._ramp = asyncio.create_task(self._run_ramp(target, duration, steps))
        await self._ramp

    async def ramp_to_db(
        self, target: float, duration: float, steps: int | None = None
    ) -> None:
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        if not isinstance(spec.domain, Interpolated):
            raise ValueUnknownError(f"{self._param} has no taper; use ramp_to_raw")
        await self.ramp_to_raw(spec.domain.to_raw(target), duration, steps)

    def cancel_ramp(self) -> None:
        if self._ramp is not None and not self._ramp.done():
            self._ramp.cancel()
        self._ramp = None

    async def _run_ramp(self, target: int, duration: float, steps: int | None) -> None:
        start = self.value.raw
        if start is None:
            # Nothing to interpolate from; jump and be honest about it.
            await self.set_raw(target)
            return
        span = abs(target - start)
        count = span if steps is None else max(1, steps)   # default: one per raw step
        if count == 0:
            return
        interval = duration / count
        for i in range(1, count + 1):
            await self.set_raw(round(start + (target - start) * i / count))
            await asyncio.sleep(interval)


class BoolProxy(ParamProxy):
    @property
    def enabled(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.raw)

    async def set_enabled(self, on: bool) -> None:
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        domain = spec.domain
        await self.set_raw(getattr(domain, "on" if on else "off"))


class EnumProxy(ParamProxy):
    @property
    def label(self) -> str:
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        assert isinstance(spec.domain, EnumDomain)
        result = spec.domain.label(self.raw)
        if result is None:
            raise ValueUnknownError(
                f"{self._channel.label} {self._param}: console reported undocumented "
                f"value {self.raw:#04x}"
            )
        return result

    async def set_label(self, label: str) -> None:
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        assert isinstance(spec.domain, EnumDomain)
        await self.set_raw(spec.domain.from_label(label))


class MembershipProxy:
    """A set-valued parameter (DCA / mute-group assign). Groups are 1-based."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId, param: str) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._channel = channel
        self._param = param

    @property
    def groups(self) -> frozenset[int]:
        """The 1-based groups this channel belongs to.

        Raises if any membership bit is unknown -- the library never fabricates
        a set from partial knowledge (PRD 10.1a).
        """
        membership = self._mixer.membership(self._channel, self._param)
        return frozenset(i + 1 for i in membership.require())

    @property
    def partial(self):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
        """The MembershipSet, including what is still unknown."""
        return self._mixer.membership(self._channel, self._param)

    async def set(self, group: int, on: bool) -> None:
        spec = REGISTRY.lookup(self._param, self._channel.cls)
        await self._mixer.set(
            self._channel, self._param, raw=spec.domain.encode(group - 1, on)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        )

    async def add(self, group: int) -> None:
        await self.set(group, True)

    async def remove(self, group: int) -> None:
        await self.set(group, False)


class SectionProxy:
    """A named group of parameters sharing a prefix (peq, gate, comp)."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId, prefix: str) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._channel = channel
        self._prefix = prefix

    def _param(self, leaf: str) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, f"{self._prefix}.{leaf}")
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `qumidi/model/channel.py` and the mixer accessors**

```python
"""Channel facades (PRD 11.1). No protocol constants live here."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from qumidi.model.base import (
    BoolProxy,
    EnumProxy,
    MembershipProxy,
    ParamProxy,
    SectionProxy,
)
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelId, Mix
from qumidi.protocol.registry import geq_band_for_freq

if TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover
    from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer


class PeqBand(SectionProxy):
    @property
    def gain(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._param("gain")

    @property
    def freq(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._param("freq")

    @property
    def width(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._param("width")

    async def set_gain_db(self, db: float) -> None:
        await self.gain.set_db(db)

    async def set_freq_hz(self, hz: float) -> None:
        await self.freq.set_db(hz)


class PeqShelvingBand(PeqBand):
    @property
    def type(self) -> EnumProxy:
        return EnumProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, f"{self._prefix}.type")


class Peq(BoolProxy):
    """The channel's parametric EQ. Reads/writes peq_in; bands hang off it."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        super().__init__(mixer, channel, "peq_in")

    @property
    def lf(self) -> PeqShelvingBand:
        return PeqShelvingBand(self._mixer, self._channel, "peq.lf")

    @property
    def lm(self) -> PeqBand:
        return PeqBand(self._mixer, self._channel, "peq.lm")

    @property
    def hm(self) -> PeqBand:
        return PeqBand(self._mixer, self._channel, "peq.hm")

    @property
    def hf(self) -> PeqShelvingBand:
        return PeqShelvingBand(self._mixer, self._channel, "peq.hf")


class Gate(BoolProxy):
    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        super().__init__(mixer, channel, "gate_in")

    def _leaf(self, name: str) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, f"gate.{name}")

    @property
    def attack(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("attack")

    @property
    def release(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("release")

    @property
    def hold(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("hold")

    @property
    def threshold(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("threshold")

    @property
    def depth(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("depth")


class Comp(BoolProxy):
    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        super().__init__(mixer, channel, "comp_in")

    def _leaf(self, name: str) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, f"comp.{name}")

    @property
    def type(self) -> EnumProxy:
        return EnumProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, "comp.type")

    async def set_type(self, label: str) -> None:
        await self.type.set_label(label)

    @property
    def attack(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("attack")

    @property
    def release(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("release")

    @property
    def knee(self) -> EnumProxy:
        return EnumProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, "comp.knee")

    @property
    def ratio(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("ratio")

    @property
    def threshold(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("threshold")

    @property
    def makeup(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return self._leaf("makeup")


class Geq(BoolProxy):
    """The 28-band graphic EQ on mixes and LR. Band indices are 0-based."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        super().__init__(mixer, channel, "geq_in")

    def band(self, index: int) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, "geq_gain", sub=index, vx=index)

    async def set_band_db(
        self, db: float, band: int | None = None, freq: float | None = None
    ) -> None:
        """Set one band's gain, by 0-based index or by frequency.

        Band indices are 0-based throughout to match the wire encoding. Band 14
        is 800 Hz and band 15 is 1 kHz. ``freq=`` resolves through the band
        table, which is what most callers actually want.
        """
        if (band is None) == (freq is None):
            raise ValueError("pass exactly one of band= or freq=")
        index = band if band is not None else geq_band_for_freq(float(freq))  # type: ignore[arg-type]
        await self.band(index).set_db(db)


class Preamp:
    """The channel's preamp, dispatching between local and dSNAKE."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._channel = channel

    async def set_gain_db(self, db: float) -> None:
        """Set preamp gain on whichever preamp this channel is using.

        Dispatches to local_gain or dsnake_gain based on the cached
        preamp_source, and raises rather than guessing if that is unknown
        (PRD 7.2).
        """
        source = self._mixer.get(self._channel, "preamp_source")
        if source.raw is None:
            from qumidi.errors import ValueUnknownError

            raise ValueUnknownError(
                f"{self._channel.label}: the console has not reported whether this "
                f"channel uses its local or dSNAKE preamp, so the gain write cannot be "
                f"routed. Set local_gain or dsnake_gain explicitly, or resync."
            )
        if not source.raw:
            await self._mixer.set(self._channel, "local_gain", db=db)
            return
        socket = self._mixer.get(self._channel, "dsnake_patch")
        if socket.raw is None:
            from qumidi.errors import ValueUnknownError

            raise ValueUnknownError(
                f"{self._channel.label} uses its dSNAKE preamp but the console has not "
                f"reported which socket it is patched to (dsnake_patch is receive-only)"
            )
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, "dsnake_gain", db=db, vx=socket.raw)

    async def set_phantom(self, on: bool) -> None:
        source = self._mixer.get(self._channel, "preamp_source")
        if source.raw:
            socket = self._mixer.get(self._channel, "dsnake_patch")
            if socket.raw is not None:
                await self._mixer.set(
                    self._channel, "dsnake_48v", raw=1 if on else 0, vx=socket.raw
                )
                return
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, "local_48v", raw=1 if on else 0)


class Send:
    """One send from a channel to one destination."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId, target: ChannelId) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._channel = channel
        self._target = target

    @property
    def level(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, "send_level",
                          sub=None, vx=self._target)

    @property
    def pan(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self._channel, "pan", sub=None, vx=self._target)

    async def set_level_db(self, db: float) -> None:
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, "send_level", db=db, vx=self._target)

    async def set_pre(self, pre: bool) -> None:
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, "mix_pre", raw=1 if pre else 0, vx=self._target)

    async def set_assigned(self, on: bool) -> None:
        param = "lr_assign" if self._target == Mix.LR else "mix_assign"
        vx = None if param == "lr_assign" else self._target
        await self._mixer.set(self._channel, param, raw=1 if on else 0, vx=vx)


class BaseStrip:
    """Anything with a fader, a mute and a name."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, channel: ChannelId) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self.id = channel

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return f"<{type(self).__name__} {self.id.label}>"

    @property
    def name(self) -> str | None:
        return self._mixer.name(self.id)

    async def set_name(self, name: str) -> None:
        await self._mixer.set_name(self.id, name)

    @property
    def fader(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "fader")

    @property
    def pafl(self) -> BoolProxy:
        return BoolProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "pafl")

    async def mute(self, muted: bool) -> None:
        await self._mixer.mute(self.id, muted)


class SendSource(BaseStrip):
    """A strip that can send to mixes, groups, matrices and FX."""

    def send(self, target: ChannelId) -> Send:
        return Send(self._mixer, self.id, target)

    @property
    def pan(self) -> ParamProxy:
        """The channel's LR pan.

        Sugar for send(Mix.LR).pan -- there is no separate channel-pan
        parameter in the protocol (PRD 7.2).
        """
        return self.send(Mix.LR).pan

    @property
    def dca_assign(self) -> MembershipProxy:
        return MembershipProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "dca_assign")

    @property
    def mute_group_assign(self) -> MembershipProxy:
        return MembershipProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "mute_group_assign")

    @property
    def peq(self) -> Peq:
        return Peq(self._mixer, self.id)

    @property
    def comp(self) -> Comp:
        return Comp(self._mixer, self.id)

    @property
    def delay(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "delay_time")


class InputChannel(SendSource):
    @property
    def preamp(self) -> Preamp:
        return Preamp(self._mixer, self.id)

    @property
    def gate(self) -> Gate:
        return Gate(self._mixer, self.id)

    @property
    def hpf(self) -> BoolProxy:
        return BoolProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "hpf_in")

    @property
    def hpf_freq(self) -> ParamProxy:
        return ParamProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "hpf_freq")

    @property
    def polarity(self) -> BoolProxy:
        return BoolProxy(self._mixer, self.id, "polarity")


class MixMaster(SendSource):
    @property
    def geq(self) -> Geq:
        return Geq(self._mixer, self.id)


class DcaMaster(BaseStrip):
    pass


class MuteGroupMaster(BaseStrip):
    async def fire(self, on: bool) -> None:
        """Fire the whole mute group.

        Identical to mute() -- a Note On to the mute-group master is the only
        way to fire a mute group; there is no dedicated message (PRD 7.2).
        """
        await self.mute(on)
```

Add the accessors to `AsyncQuMixer`:

```python
    def input(self, n: int) -> InputChannel:
        return InputChannel(self, Input(n))

    def stereo_input(self, n: int) -> InputChannel:
        return InputChannel(self, StereoInput(n))

    def mix(self, target: ChannelId) -> MixMaster:
        return MixMaster(self, target)

    @property
    def lr(self) -> MixMaster:
        return MixMaster(self, Mix.LR)

    def group(self, n: int) -> MixMaster:
        return MixMaster(self, Group(n))

    def matrix(self, n: int) -> MixMaster:
        return MixMaster(self, Matrix(n))

    def dca(self, n: int) -> DcaMaster:
        return DcaMaster(self, Dca(n))

    def mute_group(self, n: int) -> MuteGroupMaster:
        return MuteGroupMaster(self, MuteGroup(n))

    def fx_send(self, n: int) -> BaseStrip:
        return BaseStrip(self, FxSend(n))

    def fx_return(self, n: int) -> SendSource:
        return SendSource(self, FxReturn(n))

    async def all_fade_out(self, duration: float = 3.0) -> None:
        """Ramp the LR fader to -inf. Convenience; LR only (PRD 11.1)."""
        await self.lr.fader.ramp_to_raw(0x00, duration)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the ramp and pacing tests**

`tests/model/test_ramp.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Input


async def test_a_ramp_walks_the_value_to_the_target(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={(0x25, 0x17, 0x07): 0x62})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, max_send_rate=None) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.input(6).fader.ramp_to_raw(0x40, duration=0.2)
        assert mixer.input(6).fader.raw == 0x40
        assert fake.state[(0x25, 0x17, 0x07)] == 0x40


async def test_a_128_step_ramp_stays_within_the_send_rate(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 16.14. Each NRPN write is 12 bytes; 127 steps is ~1524 bytes, which
    # at 1000 B/s cannot complete in under 1.5s no matter what duration says.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={(0x25, 0x17, 0x07): 0x7F})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, max_send_rate=1000) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        started = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
        await mixer.input(6).fader.ramp_to_raw(0x00, duration=0.1)
        elapsed = asyncio.get_running_loop().time() - started
    assert elapsed >= 1.4, elapsed


async def test_the_keepalive_still_goes_out_while_the_queue_is_saturated(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 16.14: liveness must never be starved by a bulk write.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={(0x25, 0x17, 0x07): 0x7F})
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, max_send_rate=500, keepalive_interval=0.1
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fake.received.clear()
        ramp = asyncio.create_task(mixer.input(6).fader.ramp_to_raw(0x00, duration=0.1))
        await asyncio.sleep(0.55)
        ramp.cancel()
    assert bytes(fake.received).count(0xFE) >= 4


async def test_a_second_ramp_cancels_the_first(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(scene={(0x25, 0x17, 0x07): 0x7F})
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, max_send_rate=None) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fader = mixer.input(6).fader
        first = asyncio.create_task(fader.ramp_to_raw(0x00, duration=2.0))
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        await fader.ramp_to_raw(0x62, duration=0.1)
        assert first.cancelled() or first.done()
        assert mixer.input(6).fader.raw == 0x62
```

Note: for a proxy created fresh on each property access, the ramp task must be tracked on the *mixer*, keyed by `(channel, param, sub)`, not on the proxy instance — otherwise `mixer.input(6).fader` returns a new object each time and cancellation never finds the previous ramp. Store `self._ramps: dict[CacheKey, asyncio.Task[None]]` on `AsyncQuMixer` and have `ParamProxy` use it.

### Task 9.2: The synchronous facade

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/client_sync.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/__init__.py`
- Test: `tests/test_sync_facade.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `QuMixer(*args, **kwargs)` — the same constructor signature as `AsyncQuMixer`, plus `thread_safe: bool = False`.
- Context manager, `connect()`, `close()`, and every `AsyncQuMixer` coroutine method proxied without `await`.
- Property setters: `mixer.input(6).fader.db = -6.0`.

**Semantics that must be pinned down (PRD §11.2):**
- **One implementation only.** The sync class contains no protocol logic; it proxies coroutines onto a loop thread.
- **Property reads do not cross the thread boundary** — they read the cache under a lock.
- **The property setter blocks until the write is flushed** (the same guarantee as `await` in the async API) and raises the same exceptions. It does not return early. `fader.set_db(-6.0)` is the explicit form; the property is sugar.
- **Both APIs expose the same method names** — the sync facade is the async one minus `await`, never a hand-maintained parallel surface.
- **Usable from multiple caller threads.** Ordering between threads is not guaranteed; the §4.5 queue orders the writes.
- Shutdown joins the loop thread with a timeout and never leaves a non-daemon thread behind.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/client_sync.py`**

```python
"""The synchronous facade (PRD 11.2).

One implementation only: this class contains no protocol logic. It runs an
event loop in a daemon thread and proxies every coroutine method onto it. The
public surface is generated from AsyncQuMixer, so the two APIs cannot drift.

Property reads (cache lookups) do not cross the thread boundary -- they read
the cache directly. Property *setters* block until the write is flushed, the
same guarantee as await in the async API, and raise the same exceptions.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import inspect
import threading
from collections import deque
from collections.abc import Callable
from types import TracebackType
from typing import Any, TypeVar

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer

T = TypeVar("T")

#: Bounded, and drops with a warning when full (PRD 18).
_CALLBACK_QUEUE_MAX = 1024


class QuMixer:
    """Blocking control of one Qu console."""

    def __init__(self, *args: Any, thread_safe: bool = False, **kwargs: Any) -> None:
        self._thread_safe = thread_safe
        self._loop = asyncio.new_event_loop()
        self._thread = threading.Thread(
            target=self._run_loop, name="qumidi-loop", daemon=True
        )
        self._thread.start()
        self._async = self._call(self._make, args, kwargs)
        self._closed = False
        self._callback_queue: deque[Any] = deque(maxlen=_CALLBACK_QUEUE_MAX)
        self._queue_lock = threading.Lock()

    # -- loop plumbing ----------------------------------------------------

    def _run_loop(self) -> None:
        asyncio.set_event_loop(self._loop)
        self._loop.run_forever()

    async def _make(self, args: tuple[Any, ...], kwargs: dict[str, Any]) -> AsyncQuMixer:
        return AsyncQuMixer(*args, **kwargs)

    def _call(self, coro_fn: Callable[..., Any], *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
        future = asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(coro_fn(*args, **kwargs), self._loop)
        return future.result()

    def _run(self, coro: Any) -> Any:
        return asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(coro, self._loop).result()

    # -- lifecycle --------------------------------------------------------

    def connect(self) -> None:
        self._run(self._async.connect())

    def close(self) -> None:
        """Idempotent. Joins the loop thread; never leaves one behind."""
        if self._closed:
            return
        self._closed = True
        try:
            self._run(self._async.close())
        finally:
            self._loop.call_soon_threadsafe(self._loop.stop)
            self._thread.join(timeout=5.0)
            self._loop.close()

    def __enter__(self) -> QuMixer:
        self.connect()
        return self

    def __exit__(
        self,
        exc_type: type[BaseException] | None,
        exc: BaseException | None,
        tb: TracebackType | None,
    ) -> None:
        self.close()

    # -- thread-safe callbacks (PRD 11.2) ---------------------------------

    def drain_callbacks(self) -> list[Any]:
        """With thread_safe=True, collect queued events on the caller's thread."""
        with self._queue_lock:
            items = list(self._callback_queue)
            self._callback_queue.clear()
        return items

    def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> Any:
        """Proxy everything else to the async client.

        Coroutine functions are wrapped so the caller blocks; everything
        else -- properties, cache reads, the object facade -- passes through
        untouched and never crosses the thread boundary.
        """
        target = getattr(self._async, name)
        if inspect.iscoroutinefunction(target):
            def wrapper(*args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
                return self._run(target(*args, **kwargs))

            wrapper.__name__ = name
            wrapper.__doc__ = target.__doc__
            return wrapper
        return target
```

To make property *setters* work (`mixer.input(6).fader.db = -6.0`), `ParamProxy` needs setters that detect a running loop. Add to `qumidi/model/base.py`:

```python
    @db.setter
    def db(self, value: float) -> None:
        """Blocking sugar for set_db(). Only valid from the sync facade.

        The setter blocks until the write is flushed -- the same guarantee as
        await in the async API -- and raises the same exceptions (PRD 11.2).
        """
        self._blocking(self.set_db(value))

    @raw.setter
    def raw(self, value: int) -> None:
        self._blocking(self.set_raw(value))

    def _blocking(self, coro) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
        loop = getattr(self._mixer, "_owner_loop", None)
        if loop is None or loop.is_running() and _in_loop_thread(loop):
            coro.close()
            raise RuntimeError(
                "assignment is the synchronous API's sugar; from async code use "
                "await proxy.set_db(...) / set_raw(...) instead"
            )
        asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(coro, loop).result()
```

`AsyncQuMixer` sets `self._owner_loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()` in `connect()`, and `_in_loop_thread` compares `threading.get_ident()` against the loop's thread id recorded at connect time.

# Slice 10 — Meters

**Slice goal:** `qumidi monitor --host H --meters input:6` prints a live dB number; clap into a mic on channel 6 and the number jumps.

**Tier: experimental.** Marked so in the README and in every relevant docstring. The layout tables are unverified and one of them is provably wrong (PRD §14.12), so the runtime frame-size check — not the table — is the authority.

---

### Task 10.1: 7-bit unpacking and 7Q8 decoding

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/protocol/sysex.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_packing.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `unpack7to8(data: bytes) -> bytes`
- `pack8to7(data: bytes) -> bytes`
- `decode_meter_db(raw_u16: int) -> float`

**The unpacking scheme (PRD §12.2).** Every 7 payload bytes were encoded as 8 MIDI bytes, with a leading byte carrying the high bits:
```
AAAAaaaa BBBBbbbb ... GGGGgggg
  ->  0ABCDEFG 0AAAaaaa 0BBBbbbb ... 0GGGgggg
```
**Bit order, stated unambiguously:** in the leading byte `0ABCDEFG`, `A` is **bit 6** (the most significant data bit) and belongs to the *first* following byte; `G` is bit 0 and belongs to the seventh. A reversed convention also produces valid-looking output and would corrupt every meter above the first, so there is a dedicated regression test for it.

The final group may be short. With 2 remaining bytes it is `0AB00000 0AAAaaaa 0BBBbbbb` — the high-bit byte is **left-aligned**, padded with zeros on the right. Handle every partial-group length 1–6.

**Value decoding:** each meter is an unsigned 16-bit big-endian 7Q8 value with offset `0x8000`: `db = (raw_u16 - 0x8000) / 256.0`. dB values are unbounded above by the encoding (`(0xFFFF - 0x8000)/256 ≈ +128 dB`). **Do not clamp** — an out-of-range reading is diagnostic information about a layout error.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_packing.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.protocol.sysex import decode_meter_db, pack8to7, unpack7to8


def test_the_specs_worked_example() -> None:
    # PRD 12.2: packed 20 7C 00 -> unpacked 7C 80 -> 0x7C80 - 0x8000 = -896
    # -> /256.0 = -3.5 dB. Make this an explicit unit test.
    unpacked = unpack7to8(bytes([0x20, 0x7C, 0x00]))
    assert unpacked == bytes([0x7C, 0x80])
    value = int.from_bytes(unpacked, "big")
    assert value == 0x7C80
    assert decode_meter_db(value) == pytest.approx(-3.5)


def test_bit_order_regression_a_reversed_convention_must_fail() -> None:
    # PRD 12.2: A is bit 6 and belongs to the FIRST following byte. A reversed
    # convention also produces valid-looking output and would corrupt every
    # meter above the first.
    packed = bytes([0b1000000, 0x00, 0x00])   # only A set
    assert unpack7to8(packed) == bytes([0x80, 0x00])
    packed = bytes([0b0000001, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00])  # only G set
    assert unpack7to8(packed) == bytes([0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0x80])


def test_a_full_group_of_seven() -> None:
    packed = bytes([0b1111111, 0x01, 0x02, 0x03, 0x04, 0x05, 0x06, 0x07])
    assert unpack7to8(packed) == bytes([0x81, 0x82, 0x83, 0x84, 0x85, 0x86, 0x87])


@pytest.mark.parametrize("length", [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6])
def test_every_partial_group_length(length) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 12.2: handle every partial-group length 1-6, not just the example.
    payload = bytes(0x80 + i for i in range(length))
    assert unpack7to8(pack8to7(payload)) == payload


def test_pack_and_unpack_round_trip_over_many_lengths() -> None:
    for length in range(0, 40):
        payload = bytes((i * 37) % 256 for i in range(length))
        assert unpack7to8(pack8to7(payload)) == payload


def test_the_high_bit_byte_of_a_partial_group_is_left_aligned() -> None:
    # PRD 12.2: with 2 remaining bytes it is 0AB00000 ...
    assert pack8to7(bytes([0x80, 0x00])) == bytes([0b1000000, 0x00, 0x00])


def test_decode_is_signed_and_unbounded_above() -> None:
    # PRD 12.2: do not clamp to a plausible-looking range -- an out-of-range
    # reading is diagnostic information about a layout error.
    assert decode_meter_db(0x8000) == pytest.approx(0.0)
    assert decode_meter_db(0x0000) == pytest.approx(-128.0)
    assert decode_meter_db(0xFFFF) == pytest.approx(127.99609375)


def test_unpack_of_an_empty_payload_is_empty() -> None:
    assert unpack7to8(b"") == b""
```

Run: `python -m pytest tests/protocol/test_packing.py -v`
Expected: FAIL — `ImportError: cannot import name 'unpack7to8'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the codec**

```python
def unpack7to8(data: bytes) -> bytes:
    """Expand the Qu's 7-bit packing to 8-bit bytes (PRD 12.2).

    Every 7 payload bytes were sent as 8 MIDI bytes, with a leading byte
    carrying the high bits:

        0ABCDEFG 0AAAaaaa 0BBBbbbb ... 0GGGgggg

    A is bit 6 of the leading byte and belongs to the FIRST following byte;
    G is bit 0 and belongs to the seventh. A reversed convention produces
    valid-looking output and corrupts every meter above the first.
    """
    out = bytearray()
    for start in range(0, len(data), 8):
        group = data[start : start + 8]
        if not group:
            break
        high = group[0]
        for index, byte in enumerate(group[1:]):
            bit = (high >> (6 - index)) & 1
            out.append(byte | (bit << 7))
    return bytes(out)


def pack8to7(data: bytes) -> bytes:
    """Inverse of unpack7to8. The final group's high-bit byte is left-aligned."""
    out = bytearray()
    for start in range(0, len(data), 7):
        group = data[start : start + 7]
        high = 0
        for index, byte in enumerate(group):
            if byte & 0x80:
                high |= 1 << (6 - index)
        out.append(high)
        out.extend(byte & 0x7F for byte in group)
    return bytes(out)


def decode_meter_db(raw_u16: int) -> float:
    """Decode one 7Q8 meter value (PRD 12.2).

    Unbounded above by the encoding -- (0xFFFF - 0x8000)/256 is about +128 dB.
    Deliberately not clamped: an out-of-range reading is diagnostic
    information about a layout error, and hiding it is worse than showing it.
    """
    return (raw_u16 - 0x8000) / 256.0
```


### Task 10.2: Meter layouts and lazy frames

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/meters.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_meter_layout.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `Meter` enum (per block type), `BlockType` enum, `MeterBlock` dataclass.
- `MeterSlot` frozen dataclass with constructors `MeterSlot.input(n, meter)`, `MeterSlot.stereo_input(n, meter, side)`, `MeterSlot.mix(channel, meter, side)`, `MeterSlot.fx(n, meter)`, `MeterSlot.monitor(meter)`, `MeterSlot.rta(channel, band)`.
- `MeterLayout` with `.total_meters`, `.payload_bytes`, `.offset_of(slot) -> int`.
- `LAYOUTS: dict[str, MeterLayout]` keyed by model name, plus `QU24_ALTERNATE` for the second candidate.
- `MeterFrame(payload: bytes, layout: MeterLayout)` with `db(slot) -> float`, lazily decoded.

**Block contents (PRD §12.3), meter counts per block:**
- *Mono input* (10): Post Preamp, Post PEQ, Post Comp, Post Delay, Gate SC, Comp SC, Direct Out, Gate GR, Comp GR, Ducker GR.
- *Stereo input* (20): the above ×2, all L then all R.
- *Mono mix* (10): TB/SigGen, Pre-Insert, Post-PEQ, Post-GEQ, Post Comp, Post Fader, Post Insert, Comp SC, Comp GR, Ducker GR.
- *Stereo mix/group/matrix* (20): the above ×2, all L then all R.
- *Stereo monitor* (78): PAFL L, PAFL R, PAFL mono sum, Talkback, SigGen, Main Pre Fader L/R, Main Post Fader L/R, Main Mono Sum Pre/Post Fader, USB A Record Out L/R, 3 unused, RTA 31 bands L, RTA 31 bands R.
- *Stereo FX* (18): Send L, Send R, Send mono sum, Pre PEQ L/R, Tap Tempo L/R, Post PEQ L/R, 9 unused.

**"Unused" counts are meter slots, not blocks** (PRD §12.3) — read as blocks the Qu-16 frame would be nonsensically large. **"4 stereo mix" means Mix 5-6, 7-8, 9-10 *and* LR** — there are only three stereo mixes in the channel-id table; the fourth block is the main LR, metered here rather than in the monitor block. Getting this wrong shifts every subsequent block by 20 meters.

**The Qu-24 contradiction (PRD §14.12).** Taken literally, a Qu-24 frame is *larger* than a Qu-32 frame (830 vs 790 meters), which cannot be right. The `180 unused` figure is the suspect number; the hypothesis is that it is **100**, making the input region a uniform 400 meters across Qu-24/32/Pac/SB and giving Qu-24 a total of **750**. **Encode both candidates** and let the runtime frame-size check select or reject them against a real capture.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/protocol/test_meter_layout.py`:

```python
import pytest

from qumidi.protocol.meters import (
    LAYOUTS,
    QU24_ALTERNATE,
    Meter,
    MeterFrame,
    MeterSlot,
)
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Mix


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "model,total",
    [("qu16", 590), ("qu24", 830), ("qu32", 790), ("qupac", 790), ("qusb", 790)],
)
def test_totals_match_the_prd_arithmetic(model, total) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    assert LAYOUTS[model].total_meters == total


def test_the_qu24_alternate_candidate_is_encoded_too() -> None:
    # PRD 14.12: a Qu-24 frame cannot plausibly be larger than a Qu-32 frame.
    # Encode both candidates; the runtime size check picks.
    assert QU24_ALTERNATE.total_meters == 750
    assert LAYOUTS["qu24"].total_meters > LAYOUTS["qu32"].total_meters  # the suspect one


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "model,payload,encoded",
    [("qu16", 1180, 1349), ("qu24", 1660, 1898), ("qu32", 1580, 1806)],
)
def test_payload_and_encoded_sizes(model, payload, encoded) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    layout = LAYOUTS[model]
    assert layout.payload_bytes == payload
    assert layout.encoded_bytes == encoded


def test_mono_input_meters_are_ten_wide() -> None:
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    assert layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(1, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) == 0
    assert layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(1, Meter.DUCKER_GR)) == 9
    assert layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(2, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) == 10


def test_qu32_inputs_are_not_contiguous() -> None:
    # PRD 12.3: channels 25-32 appear after the stereo inputs and a 20-meter gap.
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    assert layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(24, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) == 230
    # 24 mono (240) + 3 stereo (60) + 20 unused = 320
    assert layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(25, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) == 320


def test_the_fourth_stereo_mix_block_is_lr() -> None:
    # PRD 12.3: getting this wrong shifts every subsequent block by 20 meters.
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    mix910 = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.mix(Mix.MIX910, Meter.POST_FADER))
    lr = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.mix(Mix.LR, Meter.POST_FADER))
    assert lr - mix910 == 20


def test_rta_has_31_bands_per_side() -> None:
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    left0 = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.rta("L", 0))
    left30 = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.rta("L", 30))
    right0 = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.rta("R", 0))
    assert left30 - left0 == 30
    assert right0 - left0 == 31
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.rta("L", 31))


def test_asking_for_a_meter_the_block_does_not_have_is_an_error() -> None:
    # PRD 12.1: asking for POST_PREAMP on a mix block is an error, not a
    # silent wrong index.
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.mix(Mix.MIX1, Meter.POST_PREAMP))


def test_frames_decode_lazily_and_correctly() -> None:
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    payload = bytearray(layout.payload_bytes)
    offset = layout.offset_of(MeterSlot.input(6, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) * 2
    payload[offset : offset + 2] = (0x7C80).to_bytes(2, "big")
    frame = MeterFrame(bytes(payload), layout)
    assert frame.db(MeterSlot.input(6, Meter.POST_PREAMP)) == pytest.approx(-3.5)


def test_a_frame_of_the_wrong_size_is_rejected_at_construction() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        MeterFrame(b"\x00" * 10, LAYOUTS["qu32"])


def test_an_odd_payload_length_is_rejected() -> None:
    # PRD 12.2: an odd length means the frame was truncated or the packing is
    # misunderstood.
    layout = LAYOUTS["qu32"]
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        MeterFrame(b"\x00" * (layout.payload_bytes - 1), layout)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/meters.py`**

```python
"""Meter frame layout and decoding (PRD 12.3). Tier: EXPERIMENTAL.

The block counts below are transcribed from the spec and are UNVERIFIED. One
of them is provably wrong: taken literally, a Qu-24 frame is larger than a
Qu-32 frame (PRD 14.12). Both Qu-24 candidates are encoded here, and the
runtime frame-size check -- not this table -- is the authority.

Frames decode lazily. A Qu-32 frame holds ~790 meters including two 31-band
RTAs; eagerly building 790 floats at frame rate is wasteful when the caller
wants six of them.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum, auto

from qumidi.errors import MeterLayoutError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelId, Mix
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import decode_meter_db


class Meter(Enum):
    """A meter tap. Which of these a block has depends on the block type."""

    # Mono input block (10)
    POST_PREAMP = auto()
    POST_PEQ = auto()
    POST_COMP = auto()
    POST_DELAY = auto()
    GATE_SC = auto()
    COMP_SC = auto()
    DIRECT_OUT = auto()
    GATE_GR = auto()
    COMP_GR = auto()
    DUCKER_GR = auto()
    # Mono mix block (10). POST_PEQ/POST_COMP/COMP_SC/COMP_GR/DUCKER_GR are shared.
    TB_SIGGEN = auto()
    PRE_INSERT = auto()
    POST_GEQ = auto()
    POST_FADER = auto()
    POST_INSERT = auto()
    # FX block (18)
    SEND_L = auto()
    SEND_R = auto()
    SEND_MONO = auto()
    PRE_PEQ_L = auto()
    PRE_PEQ_R = auto()
    TAP_TEMPO_L = auto()
    TAP_TEMPO_R = auto()
    POST_PEQ_L = auto()
    POST_PEQ_R = auto()
    # Monitor block (78)
    PAFL_L = auto()
    PAFL_R = auto()
    PAFL_MONO = auto()
    TALKBACK = auto()
    SIGGEN = auto()
    MAIN_PRE_FADER_L = auto()
    MAIN_PRE_FADER_R = auto()
    MAIN_POST_FADER_L = auto()
    MAIN_POST_FADER_R = auto()
    MAIN_MONO_PRE_FADER = auto()
    MAIN_MONO_POST_FADER = auto()
    USB_RECORD_L = auto()
    USB_RECORD_R = auto()


MONO_INPUT_METERS = (
    Meter.POST_PREAMP, Meter.POST_PEQ, Meter.POST_COMP, Meter.POST_DELAY,
    Meter.GATE_SC, Meter.COMP_SC, Meter.DIRECT_OUT, Meter.GATE_GR,
    Meter.COMP_GR, Meter.DUCKER_GR,
)
MONO_MIX_METERS = (
    Meter.TB_SIGGEN, Meter.PRE_INSERT, Meter.POST_PEQ, Meter.POST_GEQ,
    Meter.POST_COMP, Meter.POST_FADER, Meter.POST_INSERT, Meter.COMP_SC,
    Meter.COMP_GR, Meter.DUCKER_GR,
)
MONITOR_METERS = (
    Meter.PAFL_L, Meter.PAFL_R, Meter.PAFL_MONO, Meter.TALKBACK, Meter.SIGGEN,
    Meter.MAIN_PRE_FADER_L, Meter.MAIN_PRE_FADER_R,
    Meter.MAIN_POST_FADER_L, Meter.MAIN_POST_FADER_R,
    Meter.MAIN_MONO_PRE_FADER, Meter.MAIN_MONO_POST_FADER,
    Meter.USB_RECORD_L, Meter.USB_RECORD_R,
)
#: 13 named + 3 unused + 31 RTA L + 31 RTA R = 78.
MONITOR_RTA_OFFSET = len(MONITOR_METERS) + 3
MONITOR_BLOCK_SIZE = 78
RTA_BANDS = 31

FX_METERS = (
    Meter.SEND_L, Meter.SEND_R, Meter.SEND_MONO,
    Meter.PRE_PEQ_L, Meter.PRE_PEQ_R,
    Meter.TAP_TEMPO_L, Meter.TAP_TEMPO_R,
    Meter.POST_PEQ_L, Meter.POST_PEQ_R,
)
FX_BLOCK_SIZE = 18   # 9 named + 9 unused


class BlockType(Enum):
    MONO_INPUT = auto()
    STEREO_INPUT = auto()
    UNUSED = auto()
    MONO_MIX = auto()
    STEREO_MIX = auto()
    MONITOR = auto()
    FX = auto()


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeterBlock:
    kind: BlockType
    #: Number of *channels* in this block; UNUSED carries its meter count here.
    count: int

    @property
    def meters(self) -> int:
        if self.kind is BlockType.MONO_INPUT:
            return self.count * 10
        if self.kind is BlockType.STEREO_INPUT:
            return self.count * 20
        if self.kind is BlockType.UNUSED:
            # PRD 12.3: "unused" counts are meter SLOTS, not blocks.
            return self.count
        if self.kind is BlockType.MONO_MIX:
            return self.count * 10
        if self.kind is BlockType.STEREO_MIX:
            return self.count * 20
        if self.kind is BlockType.MONITOR:
            return self.count * MONITOR_BLOCK_SIZE
        return self.count * FX_BLOCK_SIZE


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeterSlot:
    """One addressable meter. Constructed through the factories below."""

    kind: BlockType
    index: int          # 1-based channel within the block kind
    meter: Meter | None
    side: str = "L"     # "L" or "R" for stereo blocks
    band: int | None = None

    @staticmethod
    def input(n: int, meter: Meter) -> MeterSlot:
        return MeterSlot(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, n, meter)

    @staticmethod
    def stereo_input(n: int, meter: Meter, side: str = "L") -> MeterSlot:
        return MeterSlot(BlockType.STEREO_INPUT, n, meter, side)

    @staticmethod
    def mix(channel: ChannelId, meter: Meter, side: str = "L") -> MeterSlot:
        kind = BlockType.MONO_MIX if channel in _MONO_MIXES else BlockType.STEREO_MIX
        return MeterSlot(kind, _MIX_ORDER.index(channel) + 1, meter, side)

    @staticmethod
    def fx(n: int, meter: Meter) -> MeterSlot:
        return MeterSlot(BlockType.FX, n, meter)

    @staticmethod
    def monitor(meter: Meter) -> MeterSlot:
        return MeterSlot(BlockType.MONITOR, 1, meter)

    @staticmethod
    def rta(channel: str, band: int) -> MeterSlot:
        return MeterSlot(BlockType.MONITOR, 1, None, side=channel, band=band)


_MONO_MIXES = (Mix.MIX1, Mix.MIX2, Mix.MIX3, Mix.MIX4)
#: PRD 12.3: "4 stereo mix" is Mix 5-6, 7-8, 9-10 AND LR. LR is metered here,
#: not in the monitor block. Getting this wrong shifts every later block by 20.
_STEREO_MIXES = (Mix.MIX56, Mix.MIX78, Mix.MIX910, Mix.LR)
_MIX_ORDER = _MONO_MIXES + _STEREO_MIXES


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class MeterLayout:
    name: str
    blocks: tuple[MeterBlock, ...]

    @property
    def total_meters(self) -> int:
        return sum(block.meters for block in self.blocks)

    @property
    def payload_bytes(self) -> int:
        return self.total_meters * 2

    @property
    def encoded_bytes(self) -> int:
        """Bytes on the wire after 7-bit packing."""
        payload = self.payload_bytes
        groups, remainder = divmod(payload, 7)
        return groups * 8 + (remainder + 1 if remainder else 0)

    def offset_of(self, slot: MeterSlot) -> int:
        """The 0-based meter index of ``slot`` within a frame."""
        offset = 0
        seen = 0
        for block in self.blocks:
            if block.kind is not slot.kind:
                offset += block.meters
                continue
            if slot.index > seen + block.count:
                seen += block.count
                offset += block.meters
                continue
            within = slot.index - seen - 1
            return offset + within * (block.meters // block.count) + self._within(slot)
        raise MeterLayoutError(
            f"{self.name} has no meter block for {slot.kind.name} index {slot.index}"
        )

    def _within(self, slot: MeterSlot) -> int:
        if slot.kind in (BlockType.MONO_INPUT, BlockType.STEREO_INPUT):
            table, width = MONO_INPUT_METERS, 10
        elif slot.kind in (BlockType.MONO_MIX, BlockType.STEREO_MIX):
            table, width = MONO_MIX_METERS, 10
        elif slot.kind is BlockType.FX:
            table, width = FX_METERS, FX_BLOCK_SIZE
        else:
            return self._within_monitor(slot)
        side = width if slot.side == "R" else 0
        if slot.meter is None or slot.meter not in table:
            raise MeterLayoutError(
                f"{slot.kind.name} blocks have no {slot.meter} meter; valid: "
                f"{', '.join(m.name for m in table)}"
            )
        if slot.kind is BlockType.FX:
            return table.index(slot.meter)
        return side + table.index(slot.meter)

    def _within_monitor(self, slot: MeterSlot) -> int:
        if slot.band is not None:
            if not 0 <= slot.band < RTA_BANDS:
                raise MeterLayoutError(f"RTA band must be 0..{RTA_BANDS - 1}, got {slot.band}")
            side = RTA_BANDS if slot.side == "R" else 0
            return MONITOR_RTA_OFFSET + side + slot.band
        if slot.meter is None or slot.meter not in MONITOR_METERS:
            raise MeterLayoutError(f"the monitor block has no {slot.meter} meter")
        return MONITOR_METERS.index(slot.meter)


def _layout(name: str, *blocks: MeterBlock) -> MeterLayout:
    return MeterLayout(name, blocks)


B = MeterBlock

#: PRD 12.3. Qu-16: 16 mono input, 80 unused, 3 stereo input, 20 unused,
#: 4 mono mix, 4 stereo mix, 1 stereo monitor, 4 stereo FX = 590 meters.
#: The 80 unused pads 16 inputs out to a 24-input stride.
QU16 = _layout(
    "Qu-16",
    B(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, 16), B(BlockType.UNUSED, 80),
    B(BlockType.STEREO_INPUT, 3), B(BlockType.UNUSED, 20),
    B(BlockType.MONO_MIX, 4), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 4),
    B(BlockType.MONITOR, 1), B(BlockType.FX, 4),
)

#: PRD 12.3 as written: 830 meters. PRD 14.12 flags this as internally
#: inconsistent -- a Qu-24 frame cannot plausibly be larger than a Qu-32's 790.
QU24_AS_WRITTEN = _layout(
    "Qu-24",
    B(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, 24), B(BlockType.STEREO_INPUT, 3),
    B(BlockType.UNUSED, 180),
    B(BlockType.MONO_MIX, 4), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 4),
    B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 2), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 2),
    B(BlockType.MONITOR, 1), B(BlockType.FX, 4),
)

#: PRD 14.12's hypothesis: the unused figure is 100, not 180, making the input
#: region a uniform 400 meters across Qu-24/32/Pac/SB. Total 750.
QU24_ALTERNATE = _layout(
    "Qu-24 (alternate)",
    B(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, 24), B(BlockType.STEREO_INPUT, 3),
    B(BlockType.UNUSED, 100),
    B(BlockType.MONO_MIX, 4), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 4),
    B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 2), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 2),
    B(BlockType.MONITOR, 1), B(BlockType.FX, 4),
)

#: PRD 12.3. Note the inputs are NOT contiguous: channels 25-32 appear after
#: the stereo inputs and a 20-meter gap. Total 790.
QU32 = _layout(
    "Qu-32",
    B(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, 24), B(BlockType.STEREO_INPUT, 3),
    B(BlockType.UNUSED, 20), B(BlockType.MONO_INPUT, 8),
    B(BlockType.MONO_MIX, 4), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 4),
    B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 4), B(BlockType.STEREO_MIX, 2),
    B(BlockType.MONITOR, 1), B(BlockType.FX, 4),
)

LAYOUTS: dict[str, MeterLayout] = {
    "qu16": QU16,
    "qu24": QU24_AS_WRITTEN,
    "qu32": QU32,
    "qupac": QU32,
    "qusb": QU32,
}

QU24_CANDIDATES: tuple[MeterLayout, ...] = (QU24_AS_WRITTEN, QU24_ALTERNATE)


class MeterFrame:
    """One meter frame. Decoded lazily on access (PRD 12.3)."""

    __slots__ = ("_buffer", "layout")

    def __init__(self, payload: bytes, layout: MeterLayout) -> None:
        if len(payload) % 2:
            raise MeterLayoutError(
                f"meter payload length {len(payload)} is odd; meters are 16-bit, so the "
                f"frame was truncated or the packing is misunderstood"
            )
        if len(payload) != layout.payload_bytes:
            raise MeterLayoutError(
                f"meter payload is {len(payload)} bytes but the {layout.name} layout "
                f"computes {layout.payload_bytes}. The layout table is wrong for this "
                f"console -- use mixer.meters.set_layout() to correct it (PRD 12.3)."
            )
        self._buffer = memoryview(payload)
        self.layout = layout

    def raw(self, slot: MeterSlot) -> int:
        offset = self.layout.offset_of(slot) * 2
        return int.from_bytes(self._buffer[offset : offset + 2], "big")

    def db(self, slot: MeterSlot) -> float:
        return decode_meter_db(self.raw(slot))
```

Note the Qu-32 block list encodes "4 mono mix, 4 stereo mix, 4 stereo group, 2 stereo matrix" as four `STEREO_MIX` blocks of counts 4, 4 and 2 — groups and matrices share the stereo-mix block shape (20 meters each). `offset_of` walks blocks of the same kind cumulatively, which is what makes the Qu-32 split input region work.

### Task 10.3: Metering control, validation and the CLI

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/meters.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py`
- Test: `tests/test_meters.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `mixer.meters: MeterController` with `async with mixer.meters.enabled():`, `await enable()`, `await disable()`, `stream() -> AsyncIterator[MeterFrame]`, `subscribe(slots)`, `set_layout(layout)`, `.dropped_frames: int`, `.enable_count: int`.
- `FakeQu(..., meter_frames: Iterable[bytes] | None, meter_rate: float = 20.0)`.
- `qumidi monitor --host H --meters <channel-ref>`.

**The rules (PRD §12.1, §12.3, §12.4):**
- Meters are off by default. The context manager exists so a firehose cannot be left running by accident, **including on exception paths**.
- Track an enable **count**, not a flag, so two overlapping context managers do not disable metering when the inner one exits.
- `enable()` before `wait_synced()` raises `NotSyncedError` — the model determines the frame layout.
- **Fail loudly at runtime, not only in tests.** On the first frame after enabling, compare the unpacked length against the computed layout. On mismatch: disable metering, emit a `MeterLayoutError` event naming both lengths, and do **not** deliver frames. A silently mis-indexed meter reads a plausible dB value from the wrong channel, which is worse than no meter at all — a user will trust it.
- Decode off the read path: hand the raw payload to a bounded queue. If consumers fall behind, **drop old frames** — a stale meter has no value. Count drops.
- Meter enable/disable bypasses the send throttle.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`tests/test_meters.py`:

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import NotSyncedError
from qumidi.protocol.meters import LAYOUTS, Meter, MeterSlot
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import pack8to7


def frame_for(model: str, slot: MeterSlot, raw_u16: int) -> bytes:
    layout = LAYOUTS[model]
    payload = bytearray(layout.payload_bytes)
    offset = layout.offset_of(slot) * 2
    payload[offset : offset + 2] = raw_u16.to_bytes(2, "big")
    return bytes(payload)


async def test_meters_are_off_by_default(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        assert mixer.meters.enable_count == 0
        assert fake.meters_enabled is False


async def test_enable_before_sync_raises(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 12.1: the model determines the frame layout.
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=1)
    with pytest.raises(NotSyncedError):
        await mixer.meters.enable()


async def test_the_context_manager_enables_and_disables(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        async with mixer.meters.enabled():
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            assert fake.meters_enabled is True
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert fake.meters_enabled is False


async def test_the_context_manager_disables_on_an_exception_path(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
            async with mixer.meters.enabled():
                raise RuntimeError("boom")
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert fake.meters_enabled is False


async def test_nested_enables_use_a_count_not_a_flag(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 12.1: two overlapping context managers must not disable metering
    # when the inner one exits.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory()
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        async with mixer.meters.enabled():
            async with mixer.meters.enabled():
                assert mixer.meters.enable_count == 2
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            assert fake.meters_enabled is True
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
        assert fake.meters_enabled is False


async def test_a_frame_decodes_to_the_expected_db(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    slot = MeterSlot.input(6, Meter.POST_PREAMP)
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(
        model="qu32", meter_frames=[frame_for("qu32", slot, 0x7C80)], meter_rate=50.0
    )
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        async with mixer.meters.enabled():
            frame = await asyncio.wait_for(mixer.meters.stream().__anext__(), timeout=2.0)
            assert frame.db(slot) == pytest.approx(-3.5)


async def test_a_wrong_size_frame_disables_metering_and_delivers_nothing(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 16.13: a silently mis-indexed meter reads a plausible dB value from
    # the wrong channel, which is worse than no meter at all.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu32", meter_frames=[bytes(100)], meter_rate=50.0)
    errors: list[str] = []
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        mixer.events.on_meter_layout_error(errors.append)
        await mixer.meters.enable()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.3)
        assert errors
        assert "100" in errors[0] and "1580" in errors[0]
        assert mixer.meters.enable_count == 0
        assert fake.meters_enabled is False


async def test_an_odd_payload_is_counted_and_dropped(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu32", meter_frames=[bytes(1579)], meter_rate=50.0)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.meters.enable()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.3)
        assert mixer.meters.malformed_frames >= 1


async def test_slow_consumers_drop_old_frames_rather_than_growing(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 12.4: meters are a sampled signal; a stale frame has no value.
    slot = MeterSlot.input(1, Meter.POST_PREAMP)
    frames = [frame_for("qu32", slot, 0x8000 + i) for i in range(50)]
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu32", meter_frames=frames, meter_rate=500.0)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, meter_queue_size=4) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.meters.enable()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.4)
        assert mixer.meters.dropped_frames > 0
        await mixer.meters.disable()


async def test_set_layout_lets_a_user_correct_an_untested_console(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 12.3: a user with a console we have never tested can fix their own
    # installation without waiting for a release.
    from qumidi.protocol.meters import QU24_ALTERNATE

    slot = MeterSlot.input(6, Meter.POST_PREAMP)
    payload = bytearray(QU24_ALTERNATE.payload_bytes)
    offset = QU24_ALTERNATE.offset_of(slot) * 2
    payload[offset : offset + 2] = (0x7C80).to_bytes(2, "big")
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(model="qu24", meter_frames=[bytes(payload)], meter_rate=50.0)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        mixer.meters.set_layout(QU24_ALTERNATE)
        async with mixer.meters.enabled():
            frame = await asyncio.wait_for(mixer.meters.stream().__anext__(), timeout=2.0)
            assert frame.db(slot) == pytest.approx(-3.5)


async def test_metering_is_re_enabled_after_a_reconnect(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.4: if metering was enabled before the drop, re-enable after sync.
    slot = MeterSlot.input(1, Meter.POST_PREAMP)
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(
        model="qu32", meter_frames=[frame_for("qu32", slot, 0x8000)], meter_rate=50.0
    )
    async with AsyncQuMixer(
        "127.0.0.1", port=fake.port, reconnect_backoff_min=0.05
    ) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        await mixer.meters.enable()
        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
        await fake.drop_connection()
        for _ in range(60):
            await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
            if fake.meters_enabled:
                break
        assert fake.meters_enabled is True
        await mixer.meters.disable()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/meters.py`**

```python
"""Live metering (PRD 12). Tier: EXPERIMENTAL -- the API may change.

Meters are off by default and the context manager exists so a firehose cannot
be left running by accident, including on exception paths.

The layout tables are unverified, so the runtime frame-size check is the
authority. A silently mis-indexed meter reads a plausible dB value from the
wrong channel, which is worse than no meter at all -- a user will trust it.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import contextlib
import logging
from collections.abc import AsyncIterator, Iterable
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

from qumidi.errors import MeterLayoutError, NotSyncedError
from qumidi.protocol.meters import LAYOUTS, MeterFrame, MeterLayout, MeterSlot
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import meters_enable, unpack7to8

if TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover
    from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class MeterController:
    """Enable, decode and deliver meter frames."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, queue_size: int = 4) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        self._enable_count = 0
        self._layout: MeterLayout | None = None
        self._layout_override: MeterLayout | None = None
        self._queue: asyncio.Queue[bytes] = asyncio.Queue(maxsize=queue_size)
        self._subscribed: tuple[MeterSlot, ...] | None = None
        self.dropped_frames = 0
        self.malformed_frames = 0

    # -- control ----------------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def enable_count(self) -> int:
        """Nesting depth, not a flag (PRD 12.1)."""
        return self._enable_count

    @property
    def layout(self) -> MeterLayout | None:
        return self._layout_override or self._layout

    def set_layout(self, layout: MeterLayout) -> None:
        """Override the per-model layout.

        These numbers are unverified, so a user with a console we have never
        tested can correct their own installation without waiting for a
        release (PRD 12.3). `qumidi record` captures a raw frame for exactly
        this purpose.
        """
        self._layout_override = layout

    async def enable(self) -> None:
        if self._mixer.midi_channel is None or self._mixer.model is None:
            raise NotSyncedError(
                "meters cannot be enabled before sync: the model determines the frame "
                "layout. Await wait_synced() first."
            )
        self._enable_count += 1
        if self._enable_count > 1:
            return
        self._layout = LAYOUTS.get(self._mixer.model.name.lower())
        if self._layout is None:
            log.warning("no meter layout for %s; frames will be rejected", self._mixer.model)
        # PRD 4.5: meter enable/disable bypasses the throttle.
        await self._mixer._outbound.send_priority(  # noqa: SLF001
            meters_enable(self._mixer.midi_channel, True)
        )

    async def disable(self) -> None:
        if self._enable_count == 0:
            return
        self._enable_count -= 1
        if self._enable_count > 0:
            return
        if self._mixer.midi_channel is not None:
            await self._mixer._outbound.send_priority(  # noqa: SLF001
                meters_enable(self._mixer.midi_channel, False)
            )

    @contextlib.asynccontextmanager
    async def enabled(self) -> AsyncIterator[MeterController]:
        """Enable metering for the duration of the block, exception paths included."""
        await self.enable()
        try:
            yield self
        finally:
            await self.disable()

    async def _force_disable(self, reason: str) -> None:
        self._enable_count = 0
        if self._mixer.midi_channel is not None:
            with contextlib.suppress(Exception):
                await self._mixer._outbound.send_priority(  # noqa: SLF001
                    meters_enable(self._mixer.midi_channel, False)
                )
        log.error("metering disabled: %s", reason)
        self._mixer.events.emit_meter_layout_error(reason)

    # -- ingest -----------------------------------------------------------

    def feed(self, encoded: bytes) -> None:
        """Called from the read loop. Decodes nothing; queues the payload.

        PRD 12.4: meter frames must not delay control traffic or the
        keepalive, so unpacking and decoding happen off the read path.
        """
        try:
            self._queue.put_nowait(encoded)
        except asyncio.QueueFull:
            # PRD 12.4: drop OLD frames. A stale meter has no value.
            with contextlib.suppress(asyncio.QueueEmpty):
                self._queue.get_nowait()
                self.dropped_frames += 1
            with contextlib.suppress(asyncio.QueueFull):
                self._queue.put_nowait(encoded)

    async def stream(self) -> AsyncIterator[MeterFrame]:
        """Yield validated frames. Never yields a frame of the wrong size."""
        while True:
            encoded = await self._queue.get()
            frame = await self._decode(encoded)
            if frame is not None:
                yield frame

    async def _decode(self, encoded: bytes) -> MeterFrame | None:
        payload = unpack7to8(encoded)
        layout = self.layout
        if layout is None:
            await self._force_disable(
                f"no meter layout is known for {self._mixer.model}; "
                f"observed {len(payload)} payload bytes"
            )
            return None
        if len(payload) % 2:
            # PRD 12.2: an odd length means truncation or a misunderstanding.
            self.malformed_frames += 1
            log.warning(
                "meter frame payload is %d bytes (odd); discarding rather than decoding "
                "a misaligned array", len(payload),
            )
            return None
        if len(payload) != layout.payload_bytes:
            await self._force_disable(
                f"meter frame is {len(payload)} payload bytes but the {layout.name} "
                f"layout computes {layout.payload_bytes}. The layout table is wrong for "
                f"this console; capture a frame with `qumidi record` and supply a "
                f"corrected layout via mixer.meters.set_layout()."
            )
            return None
        try:
            return MeterFrame(payload, layout)
        except MeterLayoutError as exc:
            await self._force_disable(str(exc))
            return None
```

Wire it into `AsyncQuMixer`: `self.meters = MeterController(self, queue_size=meter_queue_size)`, dispatch `CMD_METER_DATA` sysex to `self.meters.feed(parsed.payload)`, and replace the `_reenable_meters` stub:

```python
    async def _reenable_meters(self) -> None:
        """PRD 4.4: if metering was enabled before the drop, re-enable it."""
        count = self.meters.enable_count
        if count == 0:
            return
        self.meters._enable_count = 0  # noqa: SLF001
        for _ in range(count):
            await self.meters.enable()
```

```python
        self._meter_frames = list(meter_frames or [])

```

In `_on_message`'s sysex branch:

```python
        elif parsed.command == c.CMD_METERS:
            self.meters_enabled = bool(parsed.payload and parsed.payload[0])
```

```python
    async def _stream_meters(self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter) -> None:
        from itertools import cycle
        from qumidi.protocol.sysex import pack8to7

        try:
            for payload in cycle(self._meter_frames):
                if not self.meters_enabled:
                    return
                writer.write(build_sysex(
                    self.midi_channel, bytes([c.CMD_METER_DATA]) + pack8to7(payload)
                ))
                await writer.drain()
                await asyncio.sleep(1.0 / self.meter_rate)
        except (asyncio.CancelledError, OSError):
            pass
```

- [ ] **Step 7: HAND TEST — Slice 10 acceptance**

**Against a real console**, with a microphone or a signal source on channel 6:

```bash
qumidi monitor --host <ip> --meters input:6
```

Expected: a live dB number. Clap into the mic and it jumps. Silence and it falls toward the noise floor.

**The frame-size check is the real test here.** If the layout is wrong for your console, you should get a clear `MeterLayoutError` naming both lengths and metering should switch itself off — **not** a plausible-looking number from the wrong channel. If you get an error:

```bash
qumidi record --host <ip> --meters --out frame.bin --frames 1
python -c "
from qumidi.protocol.sysex import unpack7to8
from qumidi.protocol.meters import LAYOUTS, QU24_ALTERNATE
payload = unpack7to8(open('frame.bin','rb').read())
print('observed payload bytes:', len(payload), 'meters:', len(payload)//2)
for name, layout in list(LAYOUTS.items()) + [('qu24-alt', QU24_ALTERNATE)]:
    print(f'  {name}: {layout.payload_bytes} ({layout.total_meters} meters)')
"
```

(`qumidi record` arrives in Slice 11; if you are running slices in order, capture the frame with `--wire` logging instead.)

**This is the M6 resolution for PRD §14.12.** On a Qu-24, the observed meter count tells you directly whether `180 unused` or `100 unused` is correct. Record the answer, make that layout the default for Qu-24, and note it in `docs/tiers.md`.

Also confirm acceptance criterion 8: a known-silent channel reads as a very low dB, and a channel with signal reads plausibly. If channel 6 shows signal when you clap into channel 7's mic, the layout is off by a block.

# Slice 11 — Strips, shutdown, surface maps and capture

**Slice goal:** the remaining protocol surface, plus the capture/replay tooling that turns every hardware session into a permanent regression fixture.

---

### Task 11.1: MIDI fader strips

**Files:**
- Create: `qumidi/protocol/strips.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Test: `tests/protocol/test_strips.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `StripId` frozen dataclass — **a distinct type from `ChannelId`, mutually non-assignable** (PRD §14.7).
- `Strip(n: int) -> StripId` (1-based, 1–32).
- `StripKey` enum: `MUTE, SEL, PAFL`, plus `BANK_UP`, `BANK_DOWN`.
- `mixer.strips: StripApi | None` with `await set_fader(strip, raw)`, `await press(strip, key)`, `await bank(up: bool)`, `on_strip_event(cb)`.
- `mixer.strips_available: bool`

**Tier: experimental, exposed as a raw sub-API only.** No HUI/Mackie translation — PRD §1 non-goals.

**Two things this task exists to get right:**
- **Strip numbers are strip identities, not surface positions**, and are unrelated to channel ids (PRD §6.3). MIDI strip 5 sends `0x04` regardless of where it is placed. They live on MIDI channel `N+1`, and moving one emits **no** channel NRPN at all — code listening only on channel `N` will not see strip activity.
- **`N + 1` overflows when the console's MIDI channel is 16** (PRD §14.11). `N = 0x0F` and there is no channel `N+1`. On sync, if `N == 15`, disable the strip sub-API, set `strips_available = False`, and log a warning naming the cause. **Never emit a status byte of `B0 | 16`**, which would corrupt into a system message.
- **Every derived channel is computed once at sync time and validated**, not recomputed at each call site.

```python
import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError, UnsupportedOnModelError
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Input
from qumidi.protocol.strips import Strip, StripId, StripKey


def test_strip_ids_are_one_based_and_zero_indexed_on_the_wire() -> None:
    assert Strip(1).value == 0x00
    assert Strip(5).value == 0x04
    assert Strip(32).value == 0x1F
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Strip(0)
    with pytest.raises(ParameterRangeError):
        Strip(33)


def test_strip_id_is_not_a_channel_id() -> None:
    # PRD 14.7: strip key numbers (0x00-0x5F) DO overlap input channel ids.
    # They are different namespaces on different MIDI channels.
    assert not isinstance(Strip(1), type(Input(1)))
    assert not isinstance(Input(1), StripId)


def test_key_numbers_follow_the_documented_blocks() -> None:
    assert StripKey.MUTE.base == 0x00
    assert StripKey.SEL.base == 0x20
    assert StripKey.PAFL.base == 0x40
    assert StripKey.MUTE.note(Strip(1)) == 0x00
    assert StripKey.SEL.note(Strip(1)) == 0x20
    assert StripKey.PAFL.note(Strip(32)) == 0x5F


async def test_strip_fader_goes_out_on_channel_n_plus_one(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]

    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        fake.received.clear()
        await mixer.strips.press(Strip(1), StripKey.MUTE)
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
    raw = bytes(fake.received)
    assert bytes([0x91, 0x00, 0x7F]) in raw
    assert bytes([0x81, 0x00, 0x00]) in raw


async def test_bank_up_and_down(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]

        fake.received.clear()
        await mixer.strips.bank(up=True)
        await mixer.strips.bank(up=False)
        await asyncio.sleep(0.05)
    raw = bytes(fake.received)
    assert bytes([0x91, 0x7E, 0x7F]) in raw
    assert bytes([0x91, 0x7F, 0x7F]) in raw


async def test_midi_channel_16_disables_the_strip_api(fake_qu_factory, caplog) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]

    # Never emit a status byte of B0|16, which corrupts into a system message.
    fake = await fake_qu_factory(midi_channel=15)
    async with AsyncQuMixer("127.0.0.1", port=fake.port) as mixer:

        assert mixer.strips is None

async def test_strip_traffic_is_not_ingested_as_channel_state(fake_qu_factory) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]

        await asyncio.sleep(0.1)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `qumidi/protocol/strips.py`**

```python
"""MIDI fader strips, the DAW layer (PRD 7.2, 6.3, 14.7, 14.11).

Tier: EXPERIMENTAL, exposed as a raw sub-API only. No HUI/Mackie translation.

Strips are a SEPARATE NAMESPACE from channels. They are numbered by their own
identity (MIDI strip 5 sends 0x04 regardless of where it is placed on the
custom layer), they live on MIDI channel N+1, and moving one emits no channel
NRPN at all. StripId is deliberately not assignable to a ChannelId, because
strip key numbers (0x00-0x5F) DO overlap input channel ids (0x20-0x3F).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum

from qumidi.errors import ParameterRangeError


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class StripId:
    """A MIDI strip identity. Distinct from ChannelId, and not interchangeable."""

    value: int
    index: int
    label: str


def Strip(n: int) -> StripId:  # noqa: N802
    """MIDI strip 1-32, 1-based to match the console's own numbering."""
    if not 1 <= n <= 32:
        raise ParameterRangeError(f"MIDI strip must be 1..32, got {n}")
    return StripId(value=n - 1, index=n, label=f"MIDI strip {n}")


class StripKey(Enum):
    """Strip key blocks (PRD 7.2). Note numbers within each block are 0x00-0x1F."""

    MUTE = 0x00
    SEL = 0x20
    PAFL = 0x40

    @property
    def base(self) -> int:
        return int(self.value)

    def note(self, strip: StripId) -> int:
        return self.base + strip.value


BANK_UP_NOTE = 0x7E
BANK_DOWN_NOTE = 0x7F
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the strip sub-API to the client**

```python
class StripApi:
    """Raw access to the DAW-layer MIDI strips. Experimental."""

    def __init__(self, mixer: AsyncQuMixer, midi_channel: int) -> None:
        self._mixer = mixer
        # PRD 7.2: every derived channel is computed once at sync time and
        # validated, not recomputed at each call site.
        self._channel = midi_channel
        self._callbacks: list[Callable[[object], None]] = []

    async def set_fader(self, strip: StripId, raw: int) -> None:
        await self._mixer._send(  # noqa: SLF001
            ControlChange(channel=self._channel, controller=strip.value, value=raw).to_bytes()
        )

    async def press(self, strip: StripId, key: StripKey) -> None:
        note = key.note(strip)
        await self._mixer._send(  # noqa: SLF001
            NoteOn(channel=self._channel, note=note, velocity=0x7F).to_bytes()
            + NoteOff(channel=self._channel, note=note, velocity=0x00).to_bytes()
        )

    async def bank(self, up: bool) -> None:
        note = BANK_UP_NOTE if up else BANK_DOWN_NOTE
        await self._mixer._send(  # noqa: SLF001
            NoteOn(channel=self._channel, note=note, velocity=0x7F).to_bytes()
        )

    def on_strip_event(self, cb: Callable[[object], None]) -> None:
        """Subscribe to raw inbound strip messages on channel N+1."""
        self._callbacks.append(cb)

    def _dispatch(self, msg: MidiMessage) -> None:
        for cb in list(self._callbacks):
            try:
                cb(msg)
            except Exception:
                log.exception("strip callback raised; continuing")
```

In `_on_state_reply`, after learning `N`:

```python
        if parsed.midi_channel == 15:
            # PRD 14.11: N+1 does not exist when the console is on MIDI
            # channel 16. Never emit a status byte of B0|16, which would
            # corrupt into a system message.
            self.strips = None
            self.strips_available = False
            log.warning(
                "the console is on MIDI channel 16, so the MIDI-strip layer's channel "
                "N+1 does not exist; the strip sub-API is disabled. Move the console to "
                "channel 1-15 to use it."
            )
        else:
            self.strips = StripApi(self, parsed.midi_channel + 1)
            self.strips_available = True
```

In `_dispatch`, route channel-`N+1` traffic to the strip API and **not** to the cache:

```python
        channel = getattr(msg, "channel", None)
        if (
            channel is not None
            and self._midi_channel is not None
            and channel == self._midi_channel + 1
        ):
            if self.strips is not None:
                self.strips._dispatch(msg)  # noqa: SLF001
            return
```

Note that `_ingest` already guards on `update.midi_channel != self._midi_channel`; this earlier return makes the intent explicit and gives strip subscribers the raw message.

Add `FakeQu.send_raw(data: bytes)` for the test.
