### Task 11.2: Remote shutdown, MMC and SurfaceMap

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/client_async.py`
- Create: `qumidi/layout.py`
- Test: `tests/test_shutdown.py`
- Test: `tests/test_surface_map.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `AsyncQuMixer(..., allow_shutdown: bool = False)`; `await mixer.shutdown(confirm: bool = False)`.
- `await mixer.transport(command: MmcCommand)`; `MmcCommand` enum: `STOP=0x01, PLAY=0x02, FF=0x04, RW=0x05, RECORD=0x06, PAUSE=0x09`.
- `SurfaceMap.from_dict({...})`, `.channel_at(pos)`, `.position_of(ch)`, `.invalidate()`, `.valid: bool`, `SurfaceMap.from_json(path)`.
- `mixer.register_surface_map(map)`.

**Remote shutdown (PRD §7.2).** The sequence is `B0 63 00  B0 62 5F  B0 06 00  B0 26 00` — hardcoded to MIDI channel 0 and `CH = 00` in the spec, **not** the mixer's configured channel `N`. Implement it literally as specified.

**Guarded twice, because recovery requires a physical power cycle:** `AsyncQuMixer(..., allow_shutdown=True)` at construction **and** `await mixer.shutdown(confirm=True)` at the call site. Default `allow_shutdown=False` raises `ShutdownNotPermittedError`. **Do not expose it in the object facade.**

**SurfaceMap (PRD §6.3)** is a purely client-side utility with **no protocol involvement whatsoever** and no attempt to infer the mapping from traffic. Its docstring must state that the map is user-supplied configuration, that the library cannot validate it against the console, and that **recalling a scene can change the console's custom layer assignment without any notification**, silently invalidating the map.

from qumidi.errors import ShutdownNotPermittedError

import json

import pytest

from qumidi.layout import SurfaceMap
from qumidi.protocol.channels import Dca, Input, Mix


def test_lookup_in_both_directions() -> None:
    custom = SurfaceMap.from_dict({1: Input(6), 2: Input(7), 3: Dca(1), 4: Mix.MIX3})
    assert custom.channel_at(3) == Dca(1)

    assert custom.channel_at(3) == Mix.LR

    assert custom.valid is True
    custom.invalidate()
    assert custom.valid is False

    assert "scene" in str(excinfo.value).lower()

    # PRD 6.3: the docstring must state that the map is user-supplied, that
    # the library cannot validate it, and that a scene recall can silently
    # invalidate it.
    doc = (SurfaceMap.__doc__ or "").lower()
    assert "user-supplied" in doc or "user supplied" in doc
    assert "cannot validate" in doc
    assert "scene" in doc

    assert not re.search(r"0x[0-9A-Fa-f]{2}\b", source)

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement shutdown and MMC**

```python
class MmcCommand(Enum):
    """MIDI Machine Control transport commands (PRD 7.2)."""

    STOP = 0x01
    PLAY = 0x02
    FF = 0x04
    RW = 0x05
    RECORD = 0x06
    PAUSE = 0x09


    async def transport(self, command: MmcCommand) -> None:
        """Send an MMC transport command: F0 7F 7F 06 TC F7."""
        self._require_synced(f"send MMC {command.name}")
        await self._send(bytes([0xF0, 0x7F, 0x7F, 0x06, command.value, 0xF7]))

    async def shutdown(self, confirm: bool = False) -> None:
        """Remotely shut the console down. Recovery requires a power cycle.

        Guarded twice on purpose (PRD 7.2): allow_shutdown=True at
        construction AND confirm=True here. Deliberately absent from the
        object facade.
        """
        if not self.allow_shutdown:
            raise ShutdownNotPermittedError(
                "remote shutdown requires AsyncQuMixer(..., allow_shutdown=True). "
                "Recovery from a remote shutdown requires physically power-cycling "
                "the console."
            )
        if not confirm:
            raise ShutdownNotPermittedError(
                "remote shutdown requires confirm=True at the call site as well as "
                "allow_shutdown=True at construction. Recovery requires a physical "
                "power cycle."
            )
        self._require_synced("shut down the console")
        log.warning("sending remote shutdown to %s; recovery needs a power cycle", self.host)
        # PRD 7.2: hardcoded to MIDI channel 0 and CH = 00 in the spec, NOT
        # the console's configured channel N. Implemented literally.
        await self._send(
            bytes([0xB0, 0x63, 0x00, 0xB0, 0x62, 0x5F, 0xB0, 0x06, 0x00, 0xB0, 0x26, 0x00])
        )
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `qumidi/layout.py`**

```python
"""Client-side surface mapping (PRD 6.3).

THE PROTOCOL IS ENTIRELY IDENTITY-ADDRESSED AND NEVER POSITION-ADDRESSED. No
message carries a physical strip position or a layer index, and there is no
way to query the layer configuration, to read which channels are assigned to
which custom-layer positions, or to learn which layer is currently selected.

This module is therefore pure client-side bookkeeping with no protocol
involvement of any kind, and it makes no attempt to infer a mapping from
traffic.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
from collections.abc import Mapping

from qumidi.protocol.channels import ChannelId, parse_channel_ref


class SurfaceMap:
    """A position-to-identity map for the console's custom layer.

    This map is user-supplied configuration. The library cannot validate it
    against the console, because the protocol offers no way to read the layer
    configuration back.

    Recalling a scene can change the console's custom layer assignment without
    any notification, silently invalidating this map. Applications that care
    should re-load their map after a scene recall; registering the map with
    ``mixer.register_surface_map()`` makes the library mark it invalid
    automatically when it recalls a scene, but a recall made from the
    console's own surface cannot be detected at all.
    """

    def __init__(self, mapping: Mapping[int, ChannelId]) -> None:
        self._by_position = dict(mapping)
        self._by_channel = {ch: pos for pos, ch in mapping.items()}
        self._valid = True

    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, mapping: Mapping[int, ChannelId]) -> SurfaceMap:
        return cls(mapping)

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, path: str | os.PathLike[str]) -> SurfaceMap:
        """Load ``{"1": "input:6", "2": "dca:1"}`` from a JSON file."""
        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            data = json.load(handle)
        return cls({int(pos): parse_channel_ref(ref) for pos, ref in data.items()})

    @property
    def valid(self) -> bool:
        return self._valid

    def invalidate(self) -> None:
        """Mark the map stale, typically because a scene was recalled."""
        self._valid = False

    def _check(self) -> None:
        if not self._valid:
            raise RuntimeError(
                "this SurfaceMap has been invalidated because a scene was recalled, "
                "which can change the console's custom layer without notification. "
                "Re-load the map from your configuration."
            )

    def channel_at(self, position: int) -> ChannelId | None:
        self._check()
        return self._by_position.get(position)

    def position_of(self, channel: ChannelId) -> int | None:
        self._check()
        return self._by_channel.get(channel)
```

Add `mixer.register_surface_map(map)` setting `self._surface_map`, which `recall_scene` already invalidates.

### Task 11.3: `qumidi record` and `replay`

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/cli.py`
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Test: `tests/test_record_replay.py`

**Interfaces:**
- `qumidi record --host H --out FILE [--seconds N] [--meters] [--frames N]` — writes a raw byte capture of the inbound stream.
- `qumidi replay FILE [--port P]` — serves the capture through `FakeQu`.
- `FakeQu(..., replay: str | os.PathLike[str] | None = None)`.

**Why this matters (PRD §13.1, §13.5).** Recording every hardware session and committing the captures makes them the regression corpus, and lets CI test against real traffic shapes forever after. `qumidi record` is also how a user captures a meter frame to derive a corrected layout.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `record` and `replay`**

```python
async def cmd_record(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
    from qumidi.transport.tcp import TcpTransport

    captured = bytearray()

    class RecordingTransport(TcpTransport):
        async def _read_loop(self) -> None:  # type: ignore[override]
            assert self._reader is not None
            while True:
                data = await self._reader.read(4096)
                if not data:
                    break
                captured.extend(data)
                self._last_inbound = asyncio.get_running_loop().time()
                for msg in self._parser.feed(data):
                    from qumidi.midi.messages import SystemRealtime

                    if isinstance(msg, SystemRealtime) and not self.forward_active_sense:
                        continue
                    with contextlib.suppress(asyncio.QueueFull):
                        self._inbox.put_nowait(msg)

    transport = RecordingTransport(args.host, args.port)
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer(args.host, port=args.port, transport=transport)
    async with mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        if args.meters:
            await mixer.meters.enable()
        await asyncio.sleep(args.seconds)
        if args.meters:
            await mixer.meters.disable()
    with open(args.out, "wb") as handle:
        handle.write(bytes(captured))
    print(f"wrote {len(captured)} bytes to {args.out}")
    return 0


async def cmd_replay(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
    from qumidi.testing.fake_qu import FakeQu

    fake = FakeQu(replay=args.capture)
    await fake.start(args.bind, args.port)
    print(f"replaying {args.capture} on {args.bind}:{fake.port}; Ctrl-C to stop")
    await asyncio.Event().wait()
    return 0
```

```python
    recp = sub.add_parser("record", help="capture the inbound MIDI stream to a file")
    recp.add_argument("--host", required=True)
    recp.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=c.QU_TCP_PORT)
    recp.add_argument("--out", required=True)
    recp.add_argument("--seconds", type=float, default=5.0)
    recp.add_argument("--meters", action="store_true", help="enable metering while recording")
    recp.set_defaults(func=cmd_record)

    rep = sub.add_parser("replay", help="serve a capture through FakeQu")
    rep.add_argument("capture")
    rep.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=c.QU_TCP_PORT)
    rep.add_argument("--bind", default="127.0.0.1")
    rep.set_defaults(func=cmd_replay)
```

In `FakeQu`, when `replay` is set, `_handle` writes the capture's bytes to the client (after a short delay to let the client send its request) instead of running the synthesised state machine.

### Task 12.1: Complete the `FakeQu` fault matrix

**Files:**
- Modify: `qumidi/testing/fake_qu.py`
- Test: `tests/testing/test_fault_modes.py`

**Interfaces:** every fault mode from PRD §13.1, each as a constructor flag or a method:
- `split_writes: int | None` — split every outbound write at this byte boundary.
- `fe_inside_sysex: bool` — insert `FE` mid-sysex.
- `fe_between_every_byte: bool` — running-status NRPN bursts with `FE` between every pair of bytes.
- `drop_after_sync: bool` (from Slice 8), `send_end_marker: bool` (from Slice 1), `box_id` (Slice 2), `firmware` (Slice 2), `midi_channel=15` (Slice 11).
- `malformed_sysex: bool` — emit a sysex with no `F7`.
- `duplicate_state_reply: bool` — send a second `11`.
- `name_bytes_high: bool` — a name reply containing bytes `>= 0x80`.
- `meter_frames` carrying a truncated (odd-length) or wrong-sized frame (Slice 10).
- `running_status: bool` — emit the state burst under running status.

**Why one task rather than sprinkling these through earlier slices:** each earlier slice added the fault it needed. This task audits PRD §13.1's list and adds whatever is still missing, so the fault matrix is complete and asserted in one place rather than assumed.

@pytest.mark.parametrize("split", [1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 13])

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the remaining fault modes to `FakeQu`**

Route every outbound write through a single `_write(writer, data)` helper that applies the injection flags:

```python
    async def _write(self, writer: asyncio.StreamWriter, data: bytes) -> None:
        """Every outbound byte passes through here, so the fault flags apply."""
        if self.fe_between_every_byte:
            data = b"".join(bytes([b, c.ACTIVE_SENSING]) for b in data)
        if self.split_writes:
            for start in range(0, len(data), self.split_writes):
                writer.write(data[start : start + self.split_writes])
                await writer.drain()
                await asyncio.sleep(0)
            return
        writer.write(data)
        await writer.drain()
```

For `fe_inside_sysex`, splice `FE` into the middle of each built sysex before writing. For `running_status`, emit the state burst with the status byte omitted after the first message. For `malformed_sysex`, prepend a sysex with no `F7` before the state reply. For `duplicate_state_reply`, write the `11` reply twice with a different BoxID the second time. For `name_bytes_high`, corrupt one byte of each name reply to `0x80 | x`. Add `partial_sync_ok` handling in `AsyncQuMixer._sync()`:

```python
        except asyncio.TimeoutError as exc:
            if self.partial_sync_ok:
                log.warning(
                    "no end-of-sync marker within %.1fs; proceeding with %d values "
                    "received so far (partial_sync_ok=True)",
                    self.sync_timeout, len(self.cache),
                )
                for update in self._decoder.flush():
                    self._ingest(update)
                self._sync_event.set()
            else:
                raise SyncTimeoutError(...) from exc
```

### Task 12.2: The hardware conformance suite

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/hardware/conftest.py`
- Create: `tests/hardware/test_conformance.py`
- Modify: `pyproject.toml` (already has the marker)
- Test: run against real hardware.

**Interfaces:** `pytest -m hardware --qu-host=<ip> [--qu-spare-scene=99]`, excluded from CI by default.

**The safety fixture is mandatory (PRD §13.5).** The suite **must save the current scene to a spare slot before running and restore it after, including on failure.** Wrap in a fixture with `try/finally`. Without this, a conformance run leaves someone's console in a state they did not ask for.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the fixture and the suite**

`tests/hardware/conftest.py`:

```python
"""Hardware conformance fixtures (PRD 13.5). Opt-in, excluded from CI."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer


def pytest_addoption(parser: pytest.Parser) -> None:
    parser.addoption("--qu-host", default=None, help="IP of a real Qu mixer")
    parser.addoption("--qu-spare-scene", type=int, default=100,
                     help="a scene slot the suite may overwrite")


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def qu_host(request: pytest.FixtureRequest) -> str:
    host = request.config.getoption("--qu-host")
    if not host:
        pytest.skip("no --qu-host given")
    return str(host)


@pytest.fixture
async def hw(qu_host: str, request: pytest.FixtureRequest):  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    """A connected mixer whose state is snapshotted and restored.

    PRD 13.5: the suite must save the current scene to a spare slot before
    running and restore it after, INCLUDING ON FAILURE. Without this, a
    conformance run leaves someone's console in a state they did not ask for.
    """
    spare = int(request.config.getoption("--qu-spare-scene"))
    mixer = AsyncQuMixer(qu_host)
    await mixer.connect()
    await mixer.wait_synced()
    before = mixer.snapshot()
    print(
        f"\n*** Before running: store the console's current state to scene {spare} from "
        f"the console's own UI (Scenes -> Store). The suite will recall it afterwards. ***"
    )
    try:
        yield mixer
    finally:
        try:
            await mixer.recall_scene(spare)
            after = mixer.snapshot()
            drift = mixer.diff(before)
            if drift:
                print(f"\n*** {len(drift)} parameters differ from the pre-test snapshot ***")
                for d in drift[:20]:
                    print(f"    {d.channel.label} {d.param}: "
                          f"{d.snapshot_raw} -> {d.current_raw}")
        finally:
            await mixer.close()
```

`tests/hardware/test_conformance.py`:

```python
"""Conformance against real hardware (PRD 13.5, 16).

Run: pytest -m hardware --qu-host=192.168.1.60
Each test closes one of the open questions in PRD 19.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio

import pytest

from qumidi.protocol.channels import Group, Input, Mix
from qumidi.protocol.meters import LAYOUTS, QU24_ALTERNATE, Meter, MeterSlot
from qumidi.state import Freshness

pytestmark = pytest.mark.hardware


async def test_model_firmware_and_channel_are_detected(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # Acceptance criterion 1.
    assert hw.model is not None, f"unrecognised BoxID {hw.raw_box_id}"
    assert hw.firmware is not None and hw.firmware >= (1, 9)
    assert hw.midi_channel is not None
    print(f"\n{hw.model.name} firmware {hw.firmware} on MIDI channel "
          f"{hw.midi_channel_display}")


async def test_the_state_push_is_substantial(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # Record how complete the push actually is -- this is not documented.
    print(f"\nstate push delivered {len(hw.cache)} values")
    assert len(hw.cache) > 10


async def test_a_fader_write_round_trips(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    await hw.set(Input(1), "fader", raw=0x40)
    await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
    assert hw.get(Input(1), "fader").raw == 0x40


async def test_whether_the_console_echoes_writes(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 19 open question. Not an assertion -- a measurement.
    await hw.set(Input(1), "fader", raw=0x30)
    await asyncio.sleep(1.5)
    value = hw.get(Input(1), "fader")
    print(f"\nafter a write and a 1.5s wait the value is {value.freshness.name}; "
          f"the console {'DOES' if value.freshness is Freshness.KNOWN else 'does NOT'} "
          f"echo commanded changes")


async def test_name_get_set_round_trip(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    original = hw.name(Input(1))
    try:
        await hw.set_name(Input(1), "Test")
        assert hw.name(Input(1)) == "Test"
    finally:
        if original:
            await hw.set_name(Input(1), original)


async def test_measure_the_name_length_limit(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 19 open question: channel-name length limit.
    from qumidi.protocol.sysex import set_name

    original = hw.name(Input(1))
    limit = 0
    try:
        for length in range(1, 17):
            candidate = "".join(str(i % 10) for i in range(length))
            await hw._send(set_name(hw.midi_channel, Input(1).value, candidate))  # noqa: SLF001
            await asyncio.sleep(0.2)
            observed = await hw._request_name(hw.midi_channel, Input(1))  # noqa: SLF001
            if observed != candidate:
                break
            limit = length
    finally:
        if original:
            await hw.set_name(Input(1), original)
    print(f"\nmeasured name length limit: {limit} characters "
          f"(constants.MAX_NAME_LENGTH is currently set to a provisional value)")
    assert limit > 0


async def test_the_meter_frame_size_matches_the_computed_layout(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # Acceptance criterion 8 and PRD 19's Qu-24 meter layout question.
    errors: list[str] = []
    hw.events.on_meter_layout_error(errors.append)
    async with hw.meters.enabled():
        try:
            frame = await asyncio.wait_for(hw.meters.stream().__anext__(), timeout=5.0)
        except asyncio.TimeoutError:
            pytest.fail(f"no valid meter frame arrived. Layout errors: {errors}")
    print(f"\nmeter frame validated against the {frame.layout.name} layout "
          f"({frame.layout.total_meters} meters)")
    assert not errors


@pytest.mark.skipif(True, reason="enable manually when a Qu-24 is connected")
async def test_which_qu24_meter_layout_is_correct(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 14.12: resolve the 180-vs-100 unused question.
    assert hw.model is not None and hw.model.name == "QU24"
    for candidate in (LAYOUTS["qu24"], QU24_ALTERNATE):
        hw.meters.set_layout(candidate)
        errors: list[str] = []
        hw.events.on_meter_layout_error(errors.append)
        async with hw.meters.enabled():
            try:
                await asyncio.wait_for(hw.meters.stream().__anext__(), timeout=3.0)
                print(f"\nQu-24 meter layout is: {candidate.name} "
                      f"({candidate.total_meters} meters)")
                return
            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
                continue
    pytest.fail("neither Qu-24 candidate layout matched the observed frame size")


@pytest.mark.skipif(True, reason="enable manually when a Qu-24 is connected")
async def test_whether_a_qu24_has_group_5_6(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 6.2 dagger and PRD 19: write to Group 5-6 and observe whether it responds.
    await hw.set(Group(3), "fader", raw=0x40)
    await asyncio.sleep(1.0)
    value = hw.get(Group(3), "fader")
    print(f"\nQu-24 Group 5-6 responded: {value.freshness is Freshness.KNOWN}")


async def test_the_console_refuses_a_second_connection(qu_host, hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 4.2, and the number one user support question.
    from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
    from qumidi.errors import QuConnectionError

    second = AsyncQuMixer(qu_host, reply_timeout=3.0)
    with pytest.raises(QuConnectionError):
        await second.connect()
    await second.close()


async def test_measure_the_scene_recall_settling_time(hw, request) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 14.13 and PRD 19: calibrate the delay.
    spare = int(request.config.getoption("--qu-spare-scene"))
    for delay in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        hw.scene_settle_delay = delay
        before = len(hw.cache)
        await hw.recall_scene(spare)
        if len(hw.cache) >= before:
            print(f"\nscene recall settled within {delay}s "
                  f"({len(hw.cache)} values after resync)")
            return
    pytest.fail("scene recall did not settle within 2s")


async def test_group_mode_reports_its_channel(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 7.2 and PRD 19: record the CH observed on hardware.
    from qumidi.protocol.channels import GLOBAL

    value = hw.get(GLOBAL, "group_mode")
    print(f"\ngroup_mode from the state push: {value.freshness.name} raw={value.raw}")


async def test_dsnake_addressing(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 7.2 and PRD 19: does CH address anything, or is the socket in VX the
    # sole address? Only meaningful with an AR unit connected.
    patch = hw.get(Input(1), "dsnake_patch")
    print(f"\nInput 1 dsnake_patch: {patch.freshness.name} raw={patch.raw}")


async def test_behaviour_at_midi_channel_16(hw) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    # PRD 14.11 and PRD 19. Requires manually setting the console to channel 16.
    if hw.midi_channel != 15:
        pytest.skip("set the console to MIDI channel 16 and re-run to check this")
    assert hw.strips_available is False
```

### Task 12.3: Taper calibration

**Files:**
- Create: `tools/calibrate.py`
- Test: manual, against hardware.

**Interfaces:** `python tools/calibrate.py --host H --param peq.lf.freq --channel input:1 --out tables/peq_freq.py`

**This is the main thing hardware access buys that nothing else can (PRD §8.4, §13.5).** The frequency, width and gate/compressor time curves currently have only endpoints. The tool sweeps raw `0x00`–`0x7F`, pauses for the operator to read the console display, and emits a table ready to paste into `units.py`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the tool**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Sweep a parameter and record what the console displays (PRD 8.4, 13.5).

The spec gives endpoints only for frequencies, widths and the gate/compressor
times, so those tapers ship as best-effort log interpolations marked
unverified. This tool replaces them with measured ones.

Usage:
    python tools/calibrate.py --host 192.168.1.60 --param peq.lf.freq \\
        --channel input:1 --step 8 --out tables/peq_lf_freq.py

Open the relevant screen on the console, then read each value as prompted.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import asyncio

from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer
from qumidi.protocol.channels import parse_channel_ref


async def sweep(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
    ch = parse_channel_ref(args.channel)
    points: list[tuple[int, float]] = []
    async with AsyncQuMixer(args.host, port=args.port) as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        print(f"Open the screen showing {args.param} for {ch.label}, then press Enter.")
        input()
        for raw in range(0x00, 0x80, args.step):
            await mixer.set(ch, args.param, raw=raw)
            await asyncio.sleep(args.settle)
            while True:
                entry = input(f"raw {raw:#04x} -> value shown on the console (blank to skip): ")
                if not entry.strip():
                    break
                try:
                    points.append((raw, float(entry)))
                    break
                except ValueError:
                    print("  enter a number, or blank to skip")
        # Always finish at the top of the range.
        if 0x7F % args.step:
            await mixer.set(ch, args.param, raw=0x7F)
            await asyncio.sleep(args.settle)
            entry = input("raw 0x7f -> value shown on the console: ")
            if entry.strip():
                points.append((0x7F, float(entry)))

    body = ",\n        ".join(f"({raw:#04x}, {value!r})" for raw, value in points)
    source = (
        f'#: Measured on hardware, {args.param} on {ch.label}.\n'
        f'{args.param.upper().replace(".", "_")}_TAPER = Taper(\n'
        f"    points=(\n        {body},\n    ),\n"
        f'    unit="{args.unit}",\n'
        f"    verified=True,\n)\n"
    )
    if args.out:
        with open(args.out, "w", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            handle.write(source)
        print(f"\nwrote {args.out}")
    print("\n" + source)
    return 0


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument("--host", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=51325)
    ap.add_argument("--param", required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--channel", default="input:1")
    ap.add_argument("--step", type=int, default=8)
    ap.add_argument("--settle", type=float, default=0.3)
    ap.add_argument("--unit", default="Hz")
    ap.add_argument("--out")
    return asyncio.run(sweep(ap.parse_args()))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

Anyone who can reach port 51325 can mute the PA, recall a scene mid-show, or trigger the remote shutdown. - [ ] **Step 1: Write `docs/limitations.md`**

```markdown
# What this library cannot do

Every item here is a limitation of the Qu MIDI protocol itself, not of this
implementation. They are listed because otherwise each one arrives as a bug
report.

## No layer or custom-layer readback

The protocol is entirely identity-addressed. No message carries a physical
strip position or a layer index. You cannot query the layer configuration,
read which channels are assigned to which Custom Layer positions, or learn
which layer is currently selected.

When an operator moves a physical fader, the console transmits the *channel's*
identity, not the strip that was touched. A listener therefore always knows
which channel changed and can never know which strip was moved. `SurfaceMap`
in `qumidi.layout` lets you supply a mapping by hand; the library cannot
validate it, and a scene recall can change the console's custom layer without
any notification.

## No current-scene, scene-name or scene-list query

There is no message to read the current scene number, a scene's name, or the
list of scenes. `mixer.last_recalled_scene` reflects only what *this client*
recalled; it is `None` after connecting and after any recall made from the
console's own surface.

## No targeted parameter get

There is no "read this one parameter" message. `mixer.refresh()` is
implemented as a full `resync()` and is documented as such. A resync is
cheap enough to do occasionally and too expensive to do per parameter.

## No acknowledgement for name writes or scene recalls

A `03` name-set gets no reply; the library issues a read-back to confirm
(`verify_name_writes=True`). A Program Change scene recall gets no reply and
the console gives no "recall finished" signal; the library waits a settling
delay and resyncs, which is a heuristic rather than a guarantee.

## One TCP client at a time

The mixer permits exactly one TCP MIDI connection. If Qu-Pad, Qu-Control or
another script holds the session, this library cannot connect. This is the
single most common support question.

## No USB transport in v1

The transport interface accommodates one; there is no implementation.

## No FX parameter editing beyond delay

The spec documents FX delay time and link, and nothing else about the FX
units.

## Unverified tapers

The dB tapers for faders, sends, preamp gain, trim and EQ/GEQ gain come from
measured tables in the spec and are accurate to about ±0.5 dB (the limit of
128 steps). Frequency, filter width and the gate/compressor times have only
endpoints in the spec; the helpers for those are best-effort interpolations
and are marked as unverified in `docs/tiers.md`. `.raw` is always exact.

## No access control

The protocol is unauthenticated and unencrypted. See the security section of
the README.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `docs/troubleshooting.md`**

```markdown
# Troubleshooting

## "I connected but nothing happened"

Run `qumidi probe --host <ip>` first. It connects, reports the model,
firmware and MIDI channel, and disconnects.

If it hangs or refuses: **the Qu permits exactly one TCP MIDI connection at a
time.** Close Qu-Pad, Qu-Control, and any other script.

## "My write did nothing"

Work down this list in order.

1. **Check `write_unconfirmed`.** Subscribe with
   `mixer.on_write_unconfirmed(print)`. If the write fires this event, the
   console did not confirm it and the value is now `UNCONFIRMED` rather than
   `KNOWN`. That is the library telling you it sent bytes into the void.

2. **Check Group mode.** Group level, pan and pre/post are accepted only when
   the console is in Mix mode. Read `mixer.get(GLOBAL, "group_mode")`.

3. **Check the model capability.** `mixer.caps` reports what the detected
   console actually has. A Qu-16 has no groups, no matrices, and only FX 1-2.

4. **Check `Direction`.** `dsnake_patch` (`5D`) and `group_mode` (`5E`) are
   receive-only; writing them raises `ReadOnlyParameterError` rather than
   emitting bytes the console ignores.

5. **Enable the wire log.** `qumidi <command> --wire`, or in code:

   ```python
   import logging
   logging.getLogger("qumidi.wire").setLevel(logging.DEBUG)
   ```

   Compare the emitted bytes against `tests/fixtures/golden_params.yaml`. A
   wrong `VX` filler byte produces a message the console silently ignores, and
   is the most common cause of a write that does nothing.

## "The value I read back is wrong"

`.raw` is the ground truth. `.db` is a convenience and is approximate --
about ±0.5 dB, limited by 128 steps across the range. Round-tripping
`db -> raw -> db` is not exact by design. For fader raw values `0x01`-`0x0B`,
`raw -> db -> raw` deliberately does *not* round-trip; see `docs/tiers.md`.

## "My meter numbers look plausible but are from the wrong channel"

They should not be: the library validates the frame length against the
computed layout on the first frame and disables metering with a
`MeterLayoutError` on a mismatch, rather than delivering mis-indexed data.

If metering disabled itself, capture a frame and supply a corrected layout:

```bash
qumidi record --host <ip> --out frame.bin --seconds 2 --meters
```

then `mixer.meters.set_layout(...)`. Please open an issue with the observed
frame length and your console model.

## "Reading a value raises ValueUnknownError"

The console has never reported that value. Await `wait_synced()` first, and
note that not every parameter appears in the state push. This is deliberate:
the library never invents a plausible-looking number for something it has not
been told.
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `README.md`**

```markdown
# qumidi

Control and observe Allen & Heath Qu-series digital mixers (Qu-16, Qu-24,
Qu-32, Qu-Pac, Qu-SB) from Python, over MIDI-on-TCP. No runtime dependencies.

## Install

```bash
pip install qumidi
```

## ⚠️ One connection at a time

**The mixer permits exactly one TCP MIDI connection.** If Qu-Pad, Qu-Control,
or another script holds the session, this library cannot connect. This is the
first thing to check when something does not work.

## ⚠️ Security

The Qu MIDI protocol is **unauthenticated and unencrypted**. Anyone who can
reach port 51325 can mute the PA, recall a scene mid-show, or trigger a remote
shutdown that requires a physical power cycle to recover from. Put the mixer
on a trusted, isolated network. This library provides no access control and
cannot: there is nothing to authenticate against.

## Quickstart

```python
import asyncio
from qumidi import AsyncQuMixer, Mix

async def main():
    async with AsyncQuMixer("192.168.1.60") as mixer:
        await mixer.wait_synced()
        print(mixer.model, mixer.firmware)

        ch = mixer.input(6)
        print(ch.name)
        await ch.fader.set_db(-6.0)
        await ch.send(Mix.MIX3).set_level_db(-12.0)
        await ch.mute(False)

asyncio.run(main())
```

Or synchronously:

```python
from qumidi import QuMixer

with QuMixer("192.168.1.60") as mixer:
    mixer.wait_synced()
    mixer.input(6).fader.db = -6.0
```

## Command line

```bash
qumidi probe      --host 192.168.1.60     # model, firmware, MIDI channel
qumidi monitor    --host 192.168.1.60     # print changes as they happen
qumidi dump-state --host 192.168.1.60     # sync and print everything
qumidi set        --host 192.168.1.60 fader input:6 -6db
qumidi recall     --host 192.168.1.60 12
qumidi record     --host 192.168.1.60 --out capture.bin
```

## Without hardware

A complete fake console ships with the library:

```bash
python -m qumidi.testing.fake_qu --model qu32 --port 51325
qumidi probe --host 127.0.0.1
```

## Reading values

The raw 0-127 value is always the truth; `.db` is a convenience and is
approximate. A value the console has never reported reads as `UNKNOWN` and
raises rather than returning a plausible-looking number — see
[docs/limitations.md](docs/limitations.md).

## Documentation

- [What this library cannot do](docs/limitations.md) — read this first
- [Troubleshooting](docs/troubleshooting.md) — "my write did nothing"
- [Parameter tiers](docs/tiers.md) — what is verified against hardware
- [Protocol notes](docs/protocol-notes.md) — deliberate deviations from the vendor spec

## License

MIT.
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `docs/protocol-notes.md` and `docs/tiers.md`**

`docs/protocol-notes.md` transcribes each of PRD §14's thirteen errata with its reasoning, so a future maintainer reading the vendor PDF does not "fix" the code back. Each entry names the file and function that implements the deviation.

`docs/tiers.md` is generated by a small script from the registry so it cannot drift:

```python
# tools/gen_tiers.py
from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY

rows = sorted(REGISTRY, key=lambda s: (s.tier.name, s.name))
print("| Parameter | ID | VX | Tier | Notes |")
print("|---|---|---|---|---|")
for spec in rows:
    vx = type(spec.vx).__name__
    print(f"| `{spec.name}` | `{spec.nrpn_id:#04x}` | {vx} | {spec.tier.name} | "
          f"{spec.notes.replace('|', '/')} |")
```

Add a section listing which tapers are `verified=True` after calibration and which are still interpolated.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `tests/test_docs.py`**

```python
"""The documentation deliverables exist and are honest (PRD 17)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pathlib
import subprocess
import sys

import pytest

DOCS = pathlib.Path("docs")
EXAMPLES = pathlib.Path("examples")


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "path",
    ["README.md", "docs/limitations.md", "docs/troubleshooting.md",
     "docs/tiers.md", "docs/protocol-notes.md"],
)
def test_the_document_exists_and_is_not_a_stub(path) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    text = pathlib.Path(path).read_text()
    assert len(text) > 500, f"{path} looks like a stub"
    assert "TODO" not in text and "TBD" not in text


def test_the_readme_carries_both_warnings_prominently() -> None:
    # PRD 17 and 18: the single-TCP-connection caveat prominently placed, and
    # the security statement said plainly.
    text = pathlib.Path("README.md").read_text()
    head = text[:2000]
    assert "one TCP MIDI connection" in head.lower() or "one connection" in head.lower()
    assert "unauthenticated" in head.lower()
    assert "docs/limitations.md" in text


def test_the_library_offers_no_password_parameter() -> None:
    # PRD 18: do not add a "password" parameter -- there is nothing to
    # authenticate against.
    import inspect

    from qumidi.client_async import AsyncQuMixer

    params = inspect.signature(AsyncQuMixer.__init__).parameters
    assert not any("pass" in name or "auth" in name for name in params)


def test_limitations_covers_every_documented_gap() -> None:
    text = pathlib.Path("docs/limitations.md").read_text().lower()
    for topic in ("custom layer", "scene", "targeted", "acknowledge",
                  "one tcp client", "usb", "fx", "unverified"):
        assert topic in text, f"limitations.md does not mention {topic}"


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "script",
    ["fader_follow.py", "scene_automation.py", "meter_monitor.py", "state_dump.py"],
)
def test_every_example_runs_against_fake_qu(script, fake_qu_sync) -> None:  # type: ignore[no-untyped-def]
    result = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, str(EXAMPLES / script), "--host", "127.0.0.1",
         "--port", str(fake_qu_sync.port), "--once"],
        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=30,
    )
    assert result.returncode == 0, result.stderr


def test_the_tier_table_matches_the_registry() -> None:
    from qumidi.protocol.registry import REGISTRY

    text = pathlib.Path("docs/tiers.md").read_text()
    for spec in REGISTRY:
        assert f"`{spec.name}`" in text, f"{spec.name} is missing from docs/tiers.md"


def test_the_library_configures_no_logging_handlers() -> None:
    # PRD 18: the library configures no handlers and never calls basicConfig.
    for path in pathlib.Path("qumidi").rglob("*.py"):
        if path.name in ("cli.py", "fake_qu.py"):
            continue   # entry points may configure logging
        source = path.read_text()
        assert "basicConfig" not in source, path
        assert "addHandler" not in source, path
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Verify every acceptance criterion**

Work down PRD §16 and confirm each has a passing test. Write `tests/test_acceptance.py` mapping each criterion to the test that covers it, so the mapping is checked rather than asserted in prose:

```python
"""One entry per PRD 16 acceptance criterion, naming the test that covers it."""

CRITERIA = {
    1: "tests/hardware/test_conformance.py::test_model_firmware_and_channel_are_detected",
    2: "tests/test_golden_vectors.py::test_golden_vector_parses_back_to_the_same_logical_value",
    3: "tests/test_echo.py::test_a_write_never_fires_external_change",
    4: "tests/test_lifecycle.py::test_reconnect_re_syncs_and_returns_to_ready",
    5: "tests/test_golden_vectors.py::test_the_fixture_covers_every_registry_entry",
    6: "the whole suite runs against FakeQu; see tests/conftest.py",
    7: "the mypy --strict and ruff gates in every task",
    8: "tests/hardware/test_conformance.py::test_the_meter_frame_size_matches_the_computed_layout",
    9: "tests/test_shutdown.py::test_shutdown_without_the_constructor_flag_raises",
    10: "tests/test_state_cache.py::test_two_dca_memberships_are_independently_known",
    11: "tests/test_echo.py::test_an_unmatched_expectation_downgrades_to_unconfirmed",
    12: "tests/test_scene_recall.py::test_scene_recalled_precedes_sync_complete",
    13: "tests/test_meters.py::test_a_wrong_size_frame_disables_metering_and_delivers_nothing",
    14: "tests/model/test_ramp.py::test_a_128_step_ramp_stays_within_the_send_rate",
    15: "the fake_qu_factory(midi_channel=5) parametrisation across the suite",
    16: "tests/transport/test_reconnect.py::test_close_during_backoff_does_not_resurrect_the_connection",
}


def test_every_criterion_names_a_real_test() -> None:
    import pathlib
    import subprocess
    import sys

    for number, ref in CRITERIA.items():
        if "::" not in ref:
            continue
        path, _, name = ref.partition("::")
        assert pathlib.Path(path).exists(), f"criterion {number}: {path} does not exist"
        source = pathlib.Path(path).read_text()
        assert f"def {name}" in source, f"criterion {number}: {name} not found in {path}"
```

QUMIDI_TEST_MIDI_CHANNEL=5 python -m pytest -m "not hardware" -v
```

## Appendix: Slice-by-slice hand-test summary

Keep this list to hand — it is the quickest way to check the library end to end after a change.

| Slice | Command | What to look for |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `qumidi probe --host <ip>` | Correct BoxID, firmware, MIDI channel |
| 2 | `qumidi set --host <ip> mute input:6 on` | Channel 6 mute LED lights |
| 2 | `qumidi set --host <ip> mute mg:1 on` | The whole mute group fires |
| 3 | `qumidi set --host <ip> fader input:6 -6db` | Fader physically moves |
| 3 | `qumidi set --host <ip> pan input:6 raw:0x25 --to lr` | Pan reads **centre**, not hard left |
| 4 | `qumidi monitor --host <ip>` + move a fader | A `SURFACE` line appears |
| 4 | `qumidi set ...` while monitoring | A `local` line, **no** `SURFACE` line |
| 5 | `qumidi set --host <ip> comp.ratio input:6 raw:0x50` | Console reads **2.6:1** |
| 5 | `qumidi set --host <ip> delay_time input:6 raw:0x7F` | Refused locally, nothing sent |
| 6 | `qumidi dump-state --host <ip>` | Your real channel names, sweep under ~4 s |
| 6 | `qumidi set-name --host <ip> input:6 Kick` | Strip display changes |
| 7 | `qumidi recall --host <ip> 12` | Console recalls; cache goes stale then rebuilds |
| 8 | Unplug the cable, wait, plug back in | `RECONNECTING` → resync → `READY`, unattended |
| 9 | `python examples/quickstart.py` | Every line lands; GEQ moves band 15 (1 kHz) |
| 10 | `qumidi monitor --host <ip> --meters input:6` | Clap into a mic, the number jumps |
| 11 | Move a MIDI strip on the custom layer | A raw strip event; **nothing** in the cache |
| 12 | `pytest -m hardware --qu-host=<ip> -v -s` | Green, with every M6 answer printed |

---

## Appendix: PRD §19 open questions and where each is closed

| Open question | Closed by | Then update |
|---|---|---|
| Qu-24 group count | Task 12.2 `test_whether_a_qu24_has_group_5_6` | `CAPS_BY_BOX_ID[2].groups`, clear `groups_provisional` |
| Qu-24 meter layout (180 vs 100 unused) | Task 12.2 `test_which_qu24_meter_layout_is_correct` | `LAYOUTS["qu24"]`, `docs/tiers.md` |
| dSNAKE `CH`/`VX` addressing | Task 12.2 `test_dsnake_addressing` | The dSNAKE family's `tier`, its cache keying |
| Group Mode's `CH` | Task 12.2 `test_group_mode_reports_its_channel` | The `group_mode` spec's `notes`, its golden vector |
| Whether the console echoes writes | Task 12.2 `test_whether_the_console_echoes_writes` | `docs/troubleshooting.md`, the echo section of `docs/protocol-notes.md` |
| Channel-name length limit | Task 12.2 `test_measure_the_name_length_limit` | `constants.MAX_NAME_LENGTH` |
| Behaviour at MIDI channel 16 | Task 12.2 `test_behaviour_at_midi_channel_16` | `docs/protocol-notes.md` erratum 14.11 |
| Scene-recall settling time | Task 12.2 `test_measure_the_scene_recall_settling_time` | `constants.SCENE_SETTLE_DELAY` |
| `55/07` vs `18` for LR assign | Observe which the console emits while monitoring an LR assign toggle (Slice 4 hand test) | The `mix_assign` spec's `notes`, erratum 14.8 |
| Unverified frequency/time/width tapers | Task 12.3 calibration sweeps | `units.py`, `verified=True`, `docs/tiers.md` |

Every parameter's promotion to `Tier.VERIFIED` is gated on the relevant row here. `VERIFIED` is set by the calibration run, never by hand.
